# Yaoshi Design v0.0.1

Status: normative product and development design for Yaoshi v0.0.1.

Yaoshi v0.0.1 is a Debian-native local raw-image builder, a local content-addressed artifact cache, a sparse zstd target-write payload generator, a minimal local disk installer, a standard Debian trixie installed system with Yaoshi runtime takeover, deterministic terminal-screen diagnostic artifacts, one non-gating real-machine Dashboard redeploy and capture check for development observation, and one read-only local machine dashboard. The version is `0.0.1`.

The v0.0.1 design defines one current product and development contract. It defines no historical mode, no migration mode, no compatibility mode, no build profile switch, no alternate kernel path, no alternate product profile, and no compatibility implementation. This document is the source of truth.

The core split is exact:

```text
installer-media-envelope = Debian trixie kernel artifact
                         + systemd-boot fallback EFI binary
                         + installer base initramfs layer
                         + installer app initramfs layer
                         + one static musl Yaoshi PID 1 installer binary supplied as /init by the app layer
                         + bounded installer kernel module closure

installed-target-payload = frozen Debian trixie amd64 package root generated by mmdebstrap
                         + customized root tar generated by the build-system script without package-set mutation
                         + fixed Yaoshi installed-root bridge overlay merged after Debian package-root construction
                         + installed ext4 root generated from the merged installed-root source tar by e2fsprogs
                         + installed ESP containing Debian boot artifacts, runtime prepare, first-boot script, dashboard, hostname, and root SSH keys
                         + YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1 sparse target-write payload generated from the virtual installed target graph

published-image = final MBR/composite media layout
                + installer-media-envelope in partition 1
                + installed-target-payload in partition 2

installed = Debian trixie amd64 package root generated by mmdebstrap
          + customized root content generated by the build-system script
          + installed ext4 root generated by e2fsprogs from the merged root source tar
          + Debian trixie linux-image-amd64 kernel
          + Debian initramfs generated by initramfs-tools
          + systemd-boot fallback boot path
          + fixed Yaoshi root bridge in the ext4 root
          + Yaoshi runtime prepare, one-shot first-boot script, Yaoshi Dashboard binary, and build-staged runtime configuration on the ESP
```

Yaoshi installer envelope is replaceable. Yaoshi installed target payload is independent of installer-envelope inputs. Installed target payload changes only when installed-target inputs change.

Yaoshi does not compile Linux. Yaoshi does not render a kernel config. Yaoshi does not invoke Kbuild. Yaoshi does not require Linux source. Yaoshi does not own the installed-system kernel lifecycle. The installed system is Debian trixie plus Yaoshi takeover, not a custom Yaoshi Linux distribution.

Yaoshi intentionally delegates Debian package resolution and installed-root ext4 construction to Debian-native tools. `mmdebstrap` produces the frozen Debian package-root tar and Debian boot export. The build-system script customizes that frozen package root without changing the package set or Debian boot artifacts. Yaoshi merges the fixed root bridge overlay after package-root construction. `mke2fs` creates the installed ext4 root filesystem from the merged installed-root source tar. read-only `e2fsck`, `e2label`, and read-only `debugfs` validate the ext4 image before it enters the artifact cache. Yaoshi owns the image graph, GPT/MBR/FAT32 writers, payload container, installer safety gates, virtual target graph extent planning, installed runtime takeover through the fixed root bridge and ESP runtime, terminal renderer, dashboard, cache reuse, composite final image materialization, and QEMU release acceptance.

The installer is a minimal installation runtime. It is not a live Debian environment. It contains no shell, no package manager, no service manager, no login, no SSH, no network daemon, no Debian root filesystem, no documentation, no locale database, no package database, and no dashboard. It exists to discover one installer image, validate one adjacent payload partition structurally, select one target disk, write declared target data extents from the payload extent table through a high-throughput functional writer, clear declared zero extents through target zeroing primitives rather than zero-data copies when possible, flush the selected target write path, and then either reboot or power off after install completion or return to target selection after metadata erase completion.

Yaoshi cache is a local layered functional build graph cache. A cache index maps one cacheable-output intent digest to one validated output reference. Foundation objects are frozen local objects: when the intent matches and the object validates, the object is reused without refreshing from mirrors or rechecking producer tools. Runtime objects are isolated from foundation objects so installer TUI, dashboard TUI, ESP runtime script, hostname, and root SSH key changes rebuild only their direct runtime and image dependents. Composite final images are cached as logical byte-stream manifests, not as duplicated raw image byte files. No cache metadata exists for audit, provenance, explanation, approval, or reporting. There is no cache subcommand, remote cache, promotion workflow, approval workflow, signing workflow, provenance workflow, attestation workflow, or audit workflow. A developer resets reuse by deleting `.yaoshi/cache/` outside Yaoshi.

Product UI is generated only by the shared production renderer. Product-visible text is ASCII-only. Product color is foreground-only. Product action color is selection-only: among enabled actions, only the focused action uses a non-neutral foreground color. The dashboard is a single clean status surface for boot, access, compute, memory, storage, network, thermal, and bounded hardware identity, including CPU vendor/model identity, DMI-placeholder-aware machine identity, DIMM capacity/vendor summary, and disk capacity/usage/vendor/model identity.

The build architecture is exact: heavy producers are functional cache outputs; external Debian/e2fsprogs tools are cache-miss producers only; generated images and payload extent tables are validated before CAS entry creation; cache hits never rerun producer tools. The product build has one implementation path for each artifact. Build switches, compatibility paths, alternate filesystem writers, loop-mounted construction, host-mounted mutation, standalone installed-target raw materialization, standalone write-plan artifacts, duplicated raw final-image cache objects, and external target flashing tools are absent.

Yaoshi template generation is exact. Every repository-owned text artifact whose contents are modeled as a template is rendered through Askama from source templates under `crates/yaoshi-build/templates/`. `.yaoshi/` is the only local Yaoshi instance state root. It contains generated or user-edited instance configuration, runtime state, caches, work outputs, checks, and published images. It contains no source templates, no tracked default files, and no product defaults provided through `.gitignore` exception rules.

## 0. Current-version contract

The v0.0.1 implementation contract is exact:

```text
raw installer image path and format
build command grammar and exit codes
configuration schema and state root
automatic StateRoot instance bootstrap
Askama-rendered product text artifact templates
cache-first build resolver graph
automatic local content-addressed artifact cache
current-image fast path for product build and QEMU release acceptance
producer-local cache-miss environment checks
Cargo target-cache and local sccache development acceleration
Debian trixie package archive reuse
frozen Debian trixie package-root construction through mmdebstrap
build-system customization layered after the frozen Debian package root
fixed Yaoshi root bridge overlay merged after root customization
e2fsprogs-installed ext4 root filesystem
Debian trixie kernel and initramfs reuse
systemd-boot UEFI fallback boot path
split Yaoshi installer base/app initramfs layers
bounded installer kernel module closure
replaceable installer media envelope
installer-independent installed target payload
virtual installed target graph
YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1 sparse zstd target-write payload container
MBR, GPT, FAT32, ext4, virtual target graph, payload, composite-file, and payload-copy invariants
installer PID 1 runtime and destructive write safety gates
fast first-frame installer safety contract
installed prepare service and one-shot first-boot customization service
root SSH public-key runtime
root tty2 rescue shell
single-page read-only Yaoshi Dashboard
differential tty1 dashboard presenter with bounded recovery full repaint
single-Yaoshi-target installed boot invariant
borderless ASCII terminal renderer
foreground-only semantic styles
selection-only action color
regular-body and bold-emphasis console typography
two-size deterministic display-density negotiation
quiet dashboard metric rows below warning threshold
installed dashboard starts after prepare completion even when prepare failed
deterministic ASCII cell/style validation artifacts
non-gating real-machine Dashboard redeploy and capture check for Dashboard development observation
single full-flow QEMU release acceptance test
standardized references
```

Implementation closure rules:

1. Every behavior implemented by Yaoshi v0.0.1 is named in this document.
2. Every named behavior has one active implementation path.
3. Behavior not defined in this document is absent.
4. A code path that compiles Linux, renders Linux kernel config, invokes Kbuild, consumes a Linux source tree, builds an installed-system kernel, or builds an installer kernel is invalid.
5. A code path that produces a product-visible screen, runtime service, image artifact, disk write, cache artifact, command, or acceptance condition outside this document is invalid.
6. Runtime UI layout is produced only by `yaoshi-screen` production renderer functions.
7. The installed-system payload embedded in the installer image is always `YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1`; a raw installed-system partition inside the installer, payload format negotiation, tar restoration, cpio restoration, squashfs mutable root, EROFS mutable root, bmap XML file, loop-mounted extraction path, external disk layout command path, and file-level installer path are absent.
8. Product UI contains no screen routes, product labels, command surfaces, or action semantics beyond the installer transaction screens and the single Yaoshi Dashboard screen.
9. No installer block-device module load, installation-media scan, payload parse, target-disk discovery, target writable open, or target write occurs before the first complete Prepare frame has been rendered and flushed on tty1.
10. Engineering validation treats this document as the source of truth when code, tests, comments, generated artifacts, and external references disagree.
11. Every template-managed product text artifact is rendered through `askama::Template` from the closed template inventory in Section 2.1.1.
12. A path under `.yaoshi/` is never a source template, never a tracked default configuration file, and never the source of product defaults except as the local instance configuration or script bytes after bootstrap.

### 0.1 Current implementation policy

The v0.0.1 implementation has one product path for every kernel, root filesystem, payload, installer, runtime configuration, cache object, test route, and published artifact. A behavior listed as absent in this document is not a hidden fallback, not a development-only mode, and not a backend selected by a flag.

The following concepts are absent from the implementation:

```text
Yaoshi-built Linux kernel
Yaoshi-rendered Linux kernel config
Linux source input
modern-amd64-zstd-v1 kernel profile
Kbuild invocation
kbuild cache directory
installer EFI-stub kernel built from source
installed EFI-stub kernel built from source
CONFIG_CMDLINE override owned by Yaoshi
Yaoshi installed-system kernel lifecycle
canonical Debian fs-tree as product root source
Yaoshi deterministic ext4 pack-from-tree writer
writer-emitted ext4 allocation graph
writer-emitted installed-root required extent graph
Rust ext4 parser as required product build validator
compose-installed-root-ext4 phase
minimize-installed-root-ext4 phase
existing-ext4 mutation by debugfs write commands
host loop-device root construction
host mounted root construction
standalone installed-target.raw build artifact
standalone target write-plan build artifact
monolithic installer initramfs carrying both module closure and installer app bytes
raw final installer image as a duplicated full-byte CAS file object
payload version negotiation
payload format migration path
nonzero payload blob deduplication
remote artifact cache
rebuild/fresh/cache mode product flags
tracked `.yaoshi` default configuration
tracked `.yaoshi` default scripts
`.gitignore` exception rules that make `.yaoshi` product-input defaults
runtime-loaded source templates from `.yaoshi`
Tera, Handlebars, MiniJinja, Liquid, or ad hoc template engines for template-managed product text artifacts
```

The following implementation path is active and exclusive:

```text
Debian trixie linux-image-amd64 provides the only kernel image used by installer and installed targets
Debian trixie initramfs-tools produces the only installed-system initramfs
Debian trixie systemd-boot-efi-amd64-signed provides the only UEFI fallback boot binary
Yaoshi produces the only installer base initramfs layer and the only installer app initramfs layer
Yaoshi renders the closed template-managed product text artifact set through Askama from `crates/yaoshi-build/templates/`
Yaoshi bootstraps missing `.yaoshi/yaoshi.toml`, `.yaoshi/scripts/build-system.sh`, and `.yaoshi/scripts/first-boot.sh` from Askama templates before configuration loading on the bare build command
Yaoshi installer userspace is one static musl binary supplied as `/init` by the app layer; the base layer supplies only the exact kernel modules, mountpoint directories, and `/dev/console` required to expose local block devices and local console input
mmdebstrap --mode=unshare --format=tar produces the frozen Debian package-root tar artifact
build-system customization runs as a separate cached layer after Debian package-root construction
Yaoshi root bridge overlay is merged after root customization and before installed-root ext4 construction
mke2fs creates installed-root.ext4 from the merged installed-root source tar artifact
mke2fs receives explicit Yaoshi ext4 options, size, label, UUID, inode size, and block size
Yaoshi validates installed-root.ext4 with read-only e2fsck, e2label, and read-only debugfs before CAS entry creation
Yaoshi deterministic FAT32 writer emits installer boot FAT32 and installed ESP FAT32
Yaoshi Rust MBR/GPT writers emit final installer MBR bytes and installed target GPT bytes
Yaoshi constructs a virtual installed target graph from GPT bytes, installed ESP FAT32 bytes, installed root ext4 bytes, and zero ranges
YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1 is assembled directly from the virtual installed target graph into a sparse zstd payload extent table and blob area
Each nonzero payload extent owns one encoded blob range
Zero payload extents contain no blob bytes
```

Line count is not an acceptance signal. Acceptance depends on whether every runtime behavior, artifact, invariant, validation predicate, cache behavior, and test route required by this document is implemented exactly. A shorter document is invalid when it omits required behavior. A longer document is invalid when it introduces another implementation path.

### 0.2 Normative implementation selections

The implementation selections below are closed. Each row defines the only accepted implementation for that concern. No selection is replaceable by a runtime flag, build flag, environment variable, feature flag, alternate backend, compatibility mode, or test-only product path.

| Concern                             | Required v0.0.1 selection                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Excluded selections                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ----------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Kernel provider                     | Debian trixie `linux-image-amd64` extracted from the Debian root build export.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Linux source trees, Yaoshi-built kernels, Kbuild, kernel config rendering, kernel profile switches.                                                                                                                                |
| Installed initramfs                 | Debian trixie `initramfs-tools` output extracted from the Debian root build export.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Yaoshi-generated installed initramfs, dracut, host `mkinitramfs` invocation after rootfs assembly.                                                                                                                                 |
| UEFI boot binary                    | Debian trixie `systemd-boot-efi-amd64-signed` staged at `/EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | GRUB, shim, EFI-stub-only boot, UEFI NVRAM entry creation.                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Debian package root materialization | `mmdebstrap --mode=unshare --format=tar` produces one frozen Debian package-root tar artifact and Debian boot export. The package root contains Debian package content and build-time identity cleanup only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Host package installation outside `mmdebstrap`, manually assembled Debian roots, mounted root construction, Yaoshi root bridge injection during package-root construction.                                                         |
| Installed root filesystem           | `mke2fs` creates `installed-root.ext4` from the merged installed-root source tar using exact Yaoshi ext4 options; read-only `e2fsck`, `e2label`, and read-only `debugfs` validate it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Yaoshi self-written ext4 packer, external ext4 mutation libraries, debugfs write mutation, host loop mounts, host mounted mutation.                                                                                                |
| FAT filesystems                     | Yaoshi deterministic FAT32 writer emits installer boot FAT32 and installed ESP FAT32.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Host FAT tools and mounted FAT images.                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Partition layout                    | Yaoshi Rust MBR and GPT writers emit layout bytes for the final installer image and virtual installed target graph.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Host partitioning tools, loop-device layout construction, external image flashing tools, materialized installed-target raw images.                                                                                                 |
| Payload format                      | `YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1` is the only installed-system payload format. It binds target size, partitions, sparse extent table, blob area, build-time validation hashes, and installer runtime structural metadata. Runtime installation is functional and throughput-oriented rather than per-extent audit-oriented.                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Payload format negotiation, tar/cpio/squashfs/EROFS restoration paths, bmap XML product path, artifact SHA fields on media, runtime per-extent digest audit as a mandatory install step.                                           |
| Runtime configuration               | Hostname, root SSH authorized keys, Yaoshi Dashboard binary, first-boot script, and Yaoshi runtime prepare script are build-staged on the ESP and applied through the fixed installed-root bridge before SSH, dashboard, and root shell readiness.                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Rebuilding the Debian package root, customized root, root bridge, or installed root ext4 for hostname, SSH key, dashboard, first-boot script, or prepare-runtime changes; runtime package installation; interactive account setup. |
| Text artifact rendering             | Askama with `#[derive(Template)]` and `escape = "none"` renders every template-managed product text artifact from `crates/yaoshi-build/templates/`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Runtime template loading, non-Askama template engines, `.yaoshi` template sources, multiline string templates for the closed template inventory.                                                                                   |
| Instance bootstrap                  | The bare build command creates missing `.yaoshi/yaoshi.toml` and default script files from Askama templates and never overwrites existing instance files.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | A separate init command, tracked `.yaoshi` defaults, `.gitignore` exceptions for `.yaoshi` defaults, automatic overwrite of user instance files.                                                                                   |
| Cache                               | Local layered content-addressed cache indexes map one cacheable-output intent digest to one validated `OutputRef`. File, tree, and composite-file objects are supported. Cache lookup is always active.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Remote cache, cache subcommands, cache approval, provenance, signing, attestation, audit workflow, rebuild/fresh switches.                                                                                                         |
| Rust build speed                    | Cargo keeps `target/`; `.cargo/config.toml` uses local `sccache` as `rustc-wrapper`; Yaoshi runtime binaries are cached as separate file objects and co-built on double miss.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Yaoshi cleanup of `target/`, runtime binary rebuilds on unrelated artifact changes, remote compiler-cache requirements.                                                                                                            |
| Installer runtime                   | Two zstd `newc` initramfs layers: a base layer with `/dev/console`, mountpoints, `YAOSHI-MODULES`, and kernel modules, followed by an app layer whose `/init` is the static musl `yaoshi-installer` PID 1.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Shell, package manager, systemd, udev, SSH, network service, dashboard, Debian rootfs inside installer, monolithic app-plus-modules initramfs.                                                                                     |

It redeploys only the dashboard binary to one already-installed physical Yaoshi machine, restarts only `yaoshi-dashboard.service`, captures tty1 cells and tty1 attributes, attempts `/dev/fb0` framebuffer capture with exact `captured` or `skipped` state, writes deterministic framebuffer observation artifacts, and writes `framebuffer.png` only when framebuffer capture succeeds. | QEMU screenshot oracle, BMC/IPMI/Redfish/KVM screenshot substitute, PNG as functional oracle, product CLI flag, release acceptance gate, local-completion gate, runtime configuration interface.                                   |
| Acceptance                          | Default nextest profile for static/unit/parser/model checks and one KVM QEMU flow for release acceptance.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | TCG acceptance, direct-kernel QEMU boot, snapshot evidence, multiple QEMU process acceptance, bus matrix acceptance.                                                                                                               |

## 1. Product boundary

Yaoshi v0.0.1 consists exactly of:

```text
Yaoshi = Rust Debian-native image builder
       + local content-addressed artifact cache
       + local Cargo/sccache development acceleration
       + Debian trixie artifact resolver
       + e2fsprogs installed-root ext4 constructor and validator
       + deterministic FAT32, MBR, and GPT image writers
       + virtual installed target graph
       + sparse zstd target-write payload container
       + Askama-rendered product text artifact templates
       + automatic `.yaoshi` instance bootstrap
       + deterministic UI validation artifact generation through tests
       + raw installer disk image
       + minimal Yaoshi Installer runtime
       + standard Debian trixie installed runtime with Yaoshi takeover
       + borderless ASCII terminal screen renderer
       + single-page Yaoshi Dashboard
       + user-editable installed-system package list
       + build-time and first-boot user shell script hooks
```

The builder produces exactly one published raw installer disk image:

```text
.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img
```

The installer image boots on a supported local UEFI amd64 machine, with USB removable media as the primary real-machine installation medium. The installer owns `tty1`, renders a borderless density-adapted transaction screen, lets the operator select one target disk, validates one embedded `YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1` sparse zstd installed-system payload structurally, writes declared data extents to the selected target disk at declared offsets, clears declared zero extents through target zeroing primitives, flushes the write path, and then reboots or powers off from the install completion screen. The rescue metadata erase path clears target head and tail metadata, flushes writes, renders a separate erase result screen, and can return directly to target selection without reboot or poweroff.

The installed system boots from the selected target disk through systemd-boot fallback, uses the Debian trixie kernel and Debian-generated initramfs, prepares the target on first boot through one Yaoshi prepare service, grows the root partition and filesystem when possible, applies build-staged hostname and root SSH key configuration from the ESP before SSH and dashboard start, runs one nonblocking first-boot script before SSH service ordering, starts SSH root-key access, starts a root rescue shell on `tty2`, and renders exactly one read-only `Yaoshi Dashboard` screen on `tty1` with bounded machine, CPU, memory, storage, network, thermal, access, and health identity.

The supported installed boot state contains exactly one attached Yaoshi-installed target disk. Attaching two or more disks created from the same Yaoshi v0.0.1 image during installed boot is unsupported because GPT partition GUIDs, filesystem labels, and loader entries are intentionally fixed in v0.0.1.

### 1.1 Runtime platform

The supported runtime platform is:

```text
UEFI amd64
UEFI fallback boot path
UEFI Secure Boot disabled
512-byte logical block devices
systemd-networkd generic DHCP profile and systemd-resolved for installed-system network access
local display and keyboard for installer tty1
USB HID keyboard input through xHCI for installer tty1 before storage probing
local display for installed tty1 Yaoshi Dashboard
local keyboard for installed tty2 root rescue shell
ttyS0 serial device for QEMU test synchronization
installer kernel boot console routed to ttyS0 only
installed kernel boot console mirrored to ttyS0 and tty1 before Yaoshi Dashboard takeover
Linux framebuffer virtual console
Linux virtual-console font ioctls
Linux framebuffer metrics through FBIOGET_VSCREENINFO
Linux virtual-console cell capture through /dev/vcs1 for real-dashboard-check
Linux virtual-console attribute capture through /dev/vcsa1 for real-dashboard-check
Linux framebuffer fixed-screen metrics through FBIOGET_FSCREENINFO for real-dashboard-check framebuffer artifacts when /dev/fb0 is readable and supported
/dev/fb0 framebuffer artifact source for real-dashboard-check when readable and supported
vendored Terminus PSF virtual-console fonts
foreground-only Linux console SGR color
ASCII product output on a Linux virtual console
```

A dedicated discrete GPU is not a product requirement. Product display support follows the Linux virtual-console and framebuffer display-negotiation rules in Section 7.1. The real-machine Dashboard check validates the live tty1 cell and attribute surfaces through `/dev/vcs1` and `/dev/vcsa1`. It records the raw framebuffer artifact as `skipped` when `/dev/fb0` cannot be used for capture after the required tty predicates pass; this does not fail the check.

UEFI boot uses only the fallback path:

```text
/EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI
```

Yaoshi does not create UEFI NVRAM boot entries.

### 1.2 Closed product names

The installed local status surface is named exactly:

```text
Yaoshi Dashboard
```

Installed runtime dashboard path:

```text
/usr/bin/yaoshi-dashboard
```

`/usr/bin/yaoshi-dashboard` is an ext4 symlink to the dashboard binary stored on the installed ESP:

```text
/usr/bin/yaoshi-dashboard -> /boot/YAOSHI/DASHBOARD/YAOSHI-DASHBOARD
```

Systemd service names:

```text
yaoshi-prepare.service
yaoshi-first-boot.service
yaoshi-dashboard.service
yaoshi-root-shell.service
```

Workspace package names are:

```text
yaoshi
yaoshi-build
yaoshi-common
yaoshi-debian
yaoshi-image
yaoshi-initramfs
yaoshi-payload
yaoshi-screen
yaoshi-installer
yaoshi-dashboard
yaoshi-test
```

The package name `yaoshi-kernel` is absent. The string `yaoshi-console` never appears in a product path, product service, product binary, rendered product frame, or acceptance artifact predicate.

The repository root contains these source and development contract paths:

```text
Cargo.toml
Cargo.lock

flake.lock

.config/nextest.toml
docs/design.md
crates/
crates/yaoshi-build/templates/
keys/root.pub.example
.gitignore
```

The repository contains no tracked path under:

```text
.yaoshi/
```

The Yaoshi-specific `.gitignore` contract is exact:

```gitignore
/.yaoshi/
```

No `.gitignore` negation rule beginning with `!/.yaoshi` or `!.yaoshi` exists. Default Yaoshi configuration and default Yaoshi scripts are not supplied by committed `.yaoshi` files. They are rendered by `bootstrap-instance-files` from Askama templates.

`keys/root.pub.example` is a documentation-only source file. It is not a product input and is not read by `load-config`. It contains exactly:

```text
# Put one or more OpenSSH public key lines in ./keys/root.pub.
```

### 2.1.1 Askama template source contract

`yaoshi-build` owns the closed template-managed product text artifact set. Template source files live only under:

```text
crates/yaoshi-build/templates/
```

The template source inventory is exact:

```text
config/yaoshi.toml.askama
config/build-system.sh.askama
config/first-boot.sh.askama

debian/build-system-runner.sh.askama
debian/root-export-helper.sh.askama
debian/mke2fs.conf.askama

overlay/prepare-launcher.sh.askama
overlay/first-boot-launcher.sh.askama
overlay/fstab.askama
overlay/hostname.askama
overlay/systemd/yaoshi-dashboard.service.askama
overlay/systemd/yaoshi-root-shell.service.askama
overlay/systemd/yaoshi-prepare.service.askama
overlay/systemd/yaoshi-first-boot.service.askama
overlay/systemd/ssh-order.conf.askama
overlay/network/20-yaoshi-dhcp.network.askama
overlay/ssh/10-yaoshi.conf.askama
overlay/repart/20-yaoshi-root.conf.askama

esp/boot/KERNEL-RELEASE.askama
esp/config/HOSTNAME.askama
esp/config/AUTHKEYS.askama
esp/runtime/PREPARE.askama
esp/loader/loader.conf.askama
esp/loader/yaoshi.conf.askama

installer-boot/boot/KERNEL-RELEASE.askama
installer-boot/loader/loader.conf.askama
installer-boot/loader/yaoshi-installer.conf.askama
```

Renderer rules:

1. Every file in the template source inventory has exactly one Rust context type in `yaoshi-build`.
2. Every context type derives `askama::Template`.
3. Every renderer uses `#[template(path = <inventory-path>, escape = "none")]`.
4. The `askama` crate is a `yaoshi-build` dependency locked by `Cargo.lock`.
5. Template rendering happens in the builder process. Product runtimes never load source templates.
6. Rendered bytes are UTF-8. Product-visible or runtime-consumed ASCII files are ASCII-validated after rendering by their existing artifact validators.
7. Template contexts contain only functional values required to produce the rendered bytes. They contain no timestamp, user, host, path-to-workdir, reason, provenance, approval, audit, report, or explanation fields.
8. A template source digest and a renderer context grammar identifier are included in the cache intent of every cacheable output whose bytes can change because of that template.
9. The rendered bytes of the shown default configuration, default scripts, root bridge overlay files, ESP runtime files, and loader entries match the exact bytes specified in this document for the active v0.0.1 context. Debian helper templates render the exact behaviors specified in Section 5.1 and enter cache keys by template source digest and rendered byte digest.
10. Symlinks, directory nodes, binary images, MBR, GPT, FAT32, ext4, cpio, zstd frames, payload prelude, payload extent table, composite-file manifests, canonical JSON data files, cache indexes, `packages.index`, QEMU logs, progress logs, and screen-capture artifacts are produced by deterministic writers and are not source templates.
11. No product code uses Tera, Handlebars, MiniJinja, Liquid, runtime Jinja loading, `include_str!` multiline templates, or hand-maintained `.yaoshi` defaults for any file in the template source inventory.

`.envrc` contains exactly:

```text
use flake
```

`.cargo/config.toml` contains exactly:

```toml
[build]
rustc-wrapper = "sccache"

[target.x86_64-unknown-linux-musl]
linker = "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl-gcc"
```

The Rust compiler cache is a development accelerator. It is not a Yaoshi artifact cache and does not create product-visible behavior. The devshell sets local sccache storage under `.yaoshi/sccache/` and does not require or configure remote sccache storage.

### 2.2 Development shell boundary

The repository uses Nix flake, devshell, and direnv only as the development environment mechanism.

`flake.nix` exposes exactly this development shell output for Linux development:

```text
devShells.x86_64-linux.default
```

A developer grants direnv for the repository once:

```text
direnv allow
```

After that grant, entering the repository loads the flake devshell through direnv. Yaoshi development commands are run from that loaded shell.

Nix, devshell, and direnv provide command names through `PATH` and local development environment variables. They do not define product image layout, build graph semantics, kernel command lines, runtime screen behavior, target disk selection, installed-system runtime behavior, Yaoshi product configuration, or Yaoshi build decisions.

When `flake.nix`, `flake.lock`, `.envrc`, or any Nix file imported by `flake.nix` changes, maintainers or CI run:

```bash
direnv exec . bash -lc '
  set -eu
  command -v cargo >/dev/null
  command -v cargo-nextest >/dev/null
  command -v sccache >/dev/null
  command -v mmdebstrap >/dev/null
  command -v mke2fs >/dev/null
  command -v e2fsck >/dev/null
  command -v e2label >/dev/null
  command -v debugfs >/dev/null
  command -v xz >/dev/null
  command -v x86_64-unknown-linux-musl-gcc >/dev/null
  command -v qemu-system-x86_64 >/dev/null
  command -v ssh >/dev/null
  mmdebstrap --version >/dev/null 2>&1
  mke2fs -V >/dev/null 2>&1
  e2fsck -V >/dev/null 2>&1
  e2label 2>&1 | head -n 1 >/dev/null
  debugfs -V >/dev/null 2>&1
  sccache --version >/dev/null 2>&1
'
```

This validation checks the development tool surface only. `make`, host `zstd`, host FAT tools, host GPT partitioning tools, Linux kernel source, and kernel build tools are not required product build inputs.

### 2.3 Product and release acceptance command set

The complete product command set is:

```text
cargo run --locked -p yaoshi --
cargo run --locked -p yaoshi -- --version
```

The complete release acceptance command set is:

```text
cargo run --locked -p yaoshi -- --version
cargo run --locked -p yaoshi --
cargo nextest run --locked --workspace
cargo nextest run --locked --workspace --profile qemu
```

The product command accepts no subcommand, no product build flag, no positional argument, and no product help command. The only product flag is `--version`.

`--version` prints exactly:

```text
yaoshi 0.0.1
```

It exits `0` and performs no workspace mutation.

### 2.4 CLI grammar and exit codes

The product grammar is:

```text
yaoshi_args := empty | "--version"
```

Rules:

1. Empty arguments run one complete image build or the current-image fast path.
2. The single argument `--version` prints the version line and exits `0`.
3. Every other argument sequence fails with `USAGE`.
4. Argument parsing depends only on argv.

Exit codes:

| Name          | Code | Meaning                                                                                                  |
| ------------- | ---: | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `OK`          |    0 | Success.                                                                                                 |
| `USAGE`       |    2 | Invalid command, argument, flag, or output path kind.                                                    |
| `CONFIG`      |   10 | Configuration parse, schema, validation, or path-resolution failure.                                     |
| `ENVIRONMENT` |   20 | Required command missing or host precondition failure.                                                   |
| `BUILD`       |   40 | Runtime binary, Debian artifact, rootfs, overlay, initramfs, payload, or filesystem image build failure. |
| `IMAGE`       |   50 | MBR, GPT, FAT32, ext4, payload-container, virtual-target, or installer image check failure.              |
| `PUBLISH`     |   60 | Publish failure.                                                                                         |
| `INTERNAL`    |  100 | Invariant violation.                                                                                     |

Errors render to stderr as:

```text
error[<NAME>:<CODE>]: <message>
```

### 2.5 Cargo workspace

Root `Cargo.toml` is a virtual workspace file:

```toml
[workspace]
members = [
  "crates/yaoshi",
  "crates/yaoshi-build",
  "crates/yaoshi-common",
  "crates/yaoshi-debian",
  "crates/yaoshi-image",
  "crates/yaoshi-initramfs",
  "crates/yaoshi-payload",
  "crates/yaoshi-screen",
  "crates/yaoshi-installer",
  "crates/yaoshi-dashboard",
  "crates/yaoshi-test",
]
resolver = "3"

[workspace.package]
edition = "2024"
rust-version = "1.96.0"
version = "0.0.1"
license = "Apache-2.0"
```

Runtime Yaoshi binaries included in images are built for:

```text
x86_64-unknown-linux-musl
```

Crate responsibilities:

| Package            | Responsibility                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `yaoshi`           | Product CLI, argument parsing, top-level error rendering, and invocation of `yaoshi-build`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `yaoshi-build`     | Product configuration bootstrap and parsing, Askama template context definitions and rendering, command checks, lazy work tree preparation, runtime binary resolution, frozen Debian package-root construction through mmdebstrap, customized-root construction through the build-system layer, fixed root bridge overlay rendering, installed-root source merge, installed-root ext4 construction through e2fsprogs, boot artifact export, filesystem packing, virtual installed target graph construction, sparse payload build, current-image resolver, and final installer image build. |
| `yaoshi-common`    | Shared constants, byte-size types, disk references, exit names, runtime marker names, payload constants, layout constants, CAS types, virtual target graph types, payload extent types, and screen model constants.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `yaoshi-debian`    | Debian package index parsing, Debian boot artifact validation, installer module-closure selection, mmdebstrap export validation, and Debian artifact copy-out checks.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `yaoshi-image`     | MBR writer/parser, GPT writer/parser, deterministic FAT32 writer/parser, virtual installed target graph construction, graph byte-source iteration, final installer image checks, and lightweight filesystem image validation helpers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `yaoshi-initramfs` | Minimal installer initramfs tree rendering, deterministic `newc` archive generation, and zstd initramfs compression through the Rust-linked zstd library.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `yaoshi-payload`   | `YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1` prelude, canonical manifest, target extent table, blob area, virtual target graph consumption, zstd compression/decompression, build-time payload hashing and validation, installer-side structural payload validation, and installer-side payload writer.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `yaoshi-screen`    | Shared ASCII terminal screen renderer, foreground-only semantic style map, selection-only action style contract, terminal-control contract, vendored Terminus PSF font profiles, surface-specific display negotiation, installer transaction screens, dashboard screen, ASCII input sanitization, byte-column truncation, numeric formatting, ASCII gauges, tables, action rows, clean dashboard rows, and complete-frame rendering, and dashboard differential terminal presenting.                                                                                                        |
| `yaoshi-installer` | PID 1 installer runtime, mount setup, module loading, first frame, installation-media discovery, structural payload validation, target selection, pre-write target validation, sparse payload writing, metadata erase, zero extent writing, progress sampling, return-to-target after erase, reboot, and poweroff.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `yaoshi-dashboard` | Installed tty1 root-owned read-only single-page dashboard with live boot, access, process, pressure, CPU, memory, storage, filesystem, network, thermal, hardware identity, kernel-alert summary status, tty1 VT activation, nonblocking ttyS0 diagnostics, and one-second refresh cadence.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `yaoshi-test`      | Unit tests, fixtures, QEMU harness, serial frame extraction, keyboard driving, deterministic renderer checks, payload round-trip checks, virtual target graph checks, sparse/zero-write checks, Debian artifact checks, SSH functional test driving, `real-dashboard-check` developer harness, and static musl tty/framebuffer capture helper for physical-machine Dashboard observation.                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

External Rust dependency resolution is fixed by `Cargo.lock`. The Askama template engine and the zstd Rust binding are part of the workspace dependency graph and are locked by `Cargo.lock`. Runtime binary cache hits skip Cargo invocations even when Cargo, rustc, or sccache would otherwise be available.

### 2.6 Product configuration file

The bare build command performs instance bootstrap before product configuration loading. `--version` performs no instance bootstrap and no workspace mutation.

The only Yaoshi product configuration file is:

```text
./.yaoshi/yaoshi.toml
```

Yaoshi does not search parent directories, home directories, XDG directories, `/etc`, command-line paths, or environment variables for product configuration.

When this file is missing during the bare build command, Yaoshi renders `config/yaoshi.toml.askama` to this path before parsing it. An existing file is never overwritten by bootstrap.

Accepted root tables:

```text
system
sources
root
installed
scripts
```

Unknown root tables fail with `CONFIG`. A root table named `build` fails with `CONFIG`.

Product build reads and validates exactly these fields:

| Field                       | Type              | Value rule                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| --------------------------- | ----------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `system.hostname`           | string            | Default `yaoshi`. ASCII hostname label, 1-63 characters, letters, digits, hyphen, not starting or ending with hyphen.                                                                                 |
| `sources.debian`            | HTTP or HTTPS URL | Default `https://mirrors.aliyun.com/debian`. Debian mirror root accepted by `mmdebstrap`. Suite and components are fixed.                                                                             |
| `sources.debian_security`   | HTTP or HTTPS URL | Default `https://mirrors.aliyun.com/debian-security`. Debian security mirror root accepted by `mmdebstrap`. Suite and components are fixed.                                                           |
| `root.ssh_public_key_files` | path array        | Required. Non-empty array of existing regular files. Relative paths resolve against repository root. Absolute paths resolve as written.                                                               |
| `installed.packages`        | string array      | Default common installed-system package list defined below. User-editable. Package names validate by the package-name grammar in this section and are deduplicated in order.                          |
| `scripts.build_system`      | path string       | Default `./.yaoshi/scripts/build-system.sh`. Runs inside the extracted frozen Debian package root during the customized-root layer after configured packages are installed. Nonzero exit fails build. |
| `scripts.first_boot`        | path string       | Default `./.yaoshi/scripts/first-boot.sh`. Staged to the installed ESP and executed once on the installed system after prepare. Failure is logged and does not block access.                          |

The local build-system asset tree path is fixed. When the directory exists, it is consumed by the customized-root layer:

```text
./.yaoshi/vendor/
```

It is not a configuration field. When present, it must be a directory tree with
ASCII paths and only directories, regular files, and symlinks. During
`resolve-customized-root` cache misses Yaoshi stages it into the
temporary customized root as:

```text
/YAOSHI-CUSTOM/assets/
```

When `./.yaoshi/vendor/` is absent, `/YAOSHI-CUSTOM/assets/` exists as an empty
directory. The canonical fs-tree manifest digest for `./.yaoshi/vendor/`, or the
fixed absent state, is part of the customized-root input digest and the
installed-root current-image fingerprint. Build-system scripts reference local
assets only through `/YAOSHI-CUSTOM/assets/`.

Unknown fields inside accepted root tables fail with `CONFIG`.

The default product configuration template renders this product configuration:

```toml
[system]
hostname = "yaoshi"

[sources]
debian = "https://mirrors.aliyun.com/debian"
debian_security = "https://mirrors.aliyun.com/debian-security"

[root]
ssh_public_key_files = ["./keys/root.pub"]

[installed]
packages = [
  "curl",
  "git",
  "sudo",
  "less",
  "nano",
  "iproute2",
  "procps",
  "psmisc",
  "iputils-ping",
  "nftables",
  "openssh-client",
  "rsync",
]

[scripts]
build_system = "./.yaoshi/scripts/build-system.sh"
first_boot = "./.yaoshi/scripts/first-boot.sh"
```

The minimal product configuration is:

```toml
[root]
ssh_public_key_files = ["./keys/root.pub"]
```

When `installed.packages` is absent, its effective value is the default package list shown above. When either script field is absent from an existing user configuration, its effective script is an empty script with the behavior defined in Section 5.1.1 and Section 5.2.1.

Instance bootstrap rules:

1. `bootstrap-instance-files` runs only for the bare build command.
2. `bootstrap-instance-files` creates `./.yaoshi/` when it is absent.
3. `bootstrap-instance-files` creates `./.yaoshi/scripts/` when it is absent.
4. If `./.yaoshi/yaoshi.toml` is absent, `bootstrap-instance-files` renders `config/yaoshi.toml.askama` to `./.yaoshi/yaoshi.toml`.
5. If `./.yaoshi/scripts/build-system.sh` is absent, `bootstrap-instance-files` renders `config/build-system.sh.askama` to `./.yaoshi/scripts/build-system.sh`.
6. If `./.yaoshi/scripts/first-boot.sh` is absent, `bootstrap-instance-files` renders `config/first-boot.sh.askama` to `./.yaoshi/scripts/first-boot.sh`.
7. Bootstrap writes each file through a temporary sibling path, fsyncs the file, renames it into place, and fsyncs the parent directory.
8. Bootstrap never overwrites an existing `yaoshi.toml`, `build-system.sh`, or `first-boot.sh` file.
9. A bootstrap path that exists as a directory, symlink, device, socket, FIFO, or other non-regular file returns `CONFIG`.
10. A bootstrap render failure, write failure, fsync failure, or rename failure returns `CONFIG`.
11. Bootstrap does not create `./keys/root.pub`. If the generated default configuration points to `./keys/root.pub` and that file is absent, `load-config` returns `CONFIG` under the normal key-file validation rules.
12. Bootstrap does not create `out/`, `cache/`, `work/`, `check/`, or `sccache/`; those directories are created only by their resolver stages.
13. Bootstrap-rendered script executable bits are not product state. Both scripts are executed through `/bin/bash`.

Rules:

1. `root.ssh_public_key_files` is present and non-empty.
2. `sources.debian`, when present, has scheme `http` or `https`, contains no query string, and contains no fragment.
3. `sources.debian_security`, when present, has scheme `http` or `https`, contains no query string, and contains no fragment.
4. Each public key file contains one OpenSSH public key per non-empty line.
5. Each non-empty public key line parses through the `ssh-key` crate in `yaoshi-build`.
6. Leading and trailing ASCII whitespace are trimmed from each non-empty key line.
7. Duplicate key lines are removed after trimming.
8. Duplicate removal preserves first occurrence order across the configured file list.
9. The effective key set is non-empty after duplicate removal.
10. `installed.packages` is an array of strings.
11. Each configured package name matches `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9+.-]{0,127}`.
12. Duplicate package names are removed after validation.
13. Package duplicate removal preserves first occurrence order.
14. `scripts.build_system` and `scripts.first_boot`, when present, are path strings ending with `.sh`.
15. Relative script paths resolve against repository root. Absolute script paths resolve as written.
16. A configured script path must resolve to an existing regular file.
17. Each script file is at most `1048576` bytes.
18. Script bytes contain no NUL.
19. Script executable bits are ignored; both scripts are executed through `/bin/bash`.
20. Shell expansion and shell-variable interpolation do not occur in configuration values.

The effective product configuration contains exactly:

```text
hostname
debian_mirror
debian_security_mirror
ssh_authorized_keys
installed_packages
build_system_script_bytes
first_boot_script_bytes
```

The `config/build-system.sh.askama` template renders the default `./.yaoshi/scripts/build-system.sh` bytes exactly as:

```bash
#!/bin/bash
set -euo pipefail
export LC_ALL=C
export DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive
export PATH=/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin

# Runs inside the extracted Debian package root after [installed].packages are installed.
# Files written here become part of the installed root image.
# Nonzero exit stops image construction with BUILD.
# Put packages in [installed].packages; keep this script for configuration and files.
# Do not generate machine-unique identity here. First boot owns that.

mkdir -p /opt/yaoshi-local
printf '%s\n' 'customized during Yaoshi build' > /opt/yaoshi-local/README

chmod 0644 /opt/yaoshi-local/README
```

The `config/first-boot.sh.askama` template renders the default `./.yaoshi/scripts/first-boot.sh` bytes exactly as:

```bash
#!/bin/bash
set -euo pipefail
export LC_ALL=C
export PATH=/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin

# Runs once on the first installed boot after Yaoshi prepare.
# Failure is written to /var/log/yaoshi/first-boot.log and does not stop dashboard, tty2, or SSH.
# Keep the identity block unless this machine is intentionally being cloned with shared identity.
# It creates machine-unique runtime identity. It does not create root login keys.

if [ ! -s /etc/machine-id ]; then
  rm -f /etc/machine-id
  systemd-machine-id-setup
fi

mkdir -p /var/lib/dbus
ln -sf /etc/machine-id /var/lib/dbus/machine-id

# SSH host private keys must be unique per installed machine.
# Root authorized_keys is configured separately from .yaoshi/yaoshi.toml and is not changed here.
rm -f /etc/ssh/ssh_host_*_key /etc/ssh/ssh_host_*_key.pub
ssh-keygen -A

# Add user first-boot commands below this line.
# Keep commands bounded. Long-running daemons do not belong in this script.

mkdir -p /var/lib/yaoshi/local
printf '%s\n' 'first boot completed' > /var/lib/yaoshi/local/first-boot.txt
chmod 0644 /var/lib/yaoshi/local/first-boot.txt
```

## 3. State, cache, and artifact graph

### 3.1 State root

`StateRoot` is:

```text
./.yaoshi/
```

Instance state layout:

```text
.yaoshi/
  yaoshi.toml
  scripts/
    build-system.sh
    first-boot.sh

  out/
    yaoshi.img
    yaoshi.img.current

  cache/
    index/
      foundation/
        debian-package-root/
          <intent-sha256>
        installer-module-closure/
          <intent-sha256>
        installer-base-initramfs/
          <intent-sha256>
      root/
        customized-root/
          <intent-sha256>
        root-bridge-overlay/
          <intent-sha256>
        installed-root-source/
          <intent-sha256>
        installed-root-ext4/
          <intent-sha256>
      runtime/
        build-yaoshi-installer-musl/
          <intent-sha256>
        build-yaoshi-dashboard-musl/
          <intent-sha256>
        installer-app-initramfs/
          <intent-sha256>
        installed-esp-fat32/
          <intent-sha256>
        installer-boot-fat32/
          <intent-sha256>
      image/
        installed-system-payload/
          <intent-sha256>
        final-installer-composite/
          <intent-sha256>
    object/
      file/
        <output-sha256>
      tree/
        <output-sha256>/
      composite-file/
        <logical-output-sha256>
    extent/
      payload-encoding/
        <extent-encoding-sha256>
    apt-archives/

  sccache/

  work/
    bin/
      yaoshi-installer
      yaoshi-dashboard

    debian/
      package-root/
        root.tar
        packages.index
        boot/
          vmlinuz
          initrd.img
          config
          systemd-bootx64.efi.signed
          KERNEL-RELEASE
          modules/<kernel-release>/
      customized-root/
        root.tar
        packages.index
        build-system-script.log
      root-bridge-overlay.tree/
      root-bridge-overlay.tar
      installed-root-source/
        installed-root.tar
      installed-root-ext4/
        installed-root.ext4
        packages.index
      installer-modules/
        lib/modules/<kernel-release>/
        YAOSHI-MODULES

    initramfs/
      base.tree/
      BASE.CPIO
      INSTALLER-BASE.CPIO.ZST
      app.tree/
      APP.CPIO
      INSTALLER-APP.CPIO.ZST

    image/
      installed-esp.tree/
      installed-esp.fat32
      installed-target.graph.json
      installed-system.ypayload
      installer-boot.tree/
      installer-boot.fat32
      yaoshi.img.candidate

  check/
    qemu/
    real-dashboard/
```

`work/image/yaoshi.img.candidate` is a publish-time materialization target for the composite final image. It is not a cache object and is not created on a valid current-image fast path.

`.yaoshi/yaoshi.toml` and `.yaoshi/scripts/*.sh` are local instance inputs after bootstrap. `.yaoshi/out/`, `.yaoshi/cache/`, `.yaoshi/work/`, `.yaoshi/check/`, and `.yaoshi/sccache/` are generated state. No path under `.yaoshi/` is a repository source template or committed default.

The following generated paths are invalid and must not exist as Yaoshi-generated directories or files:

```text
.yaoshi/artifacts/
.yaoshi/templates/
.yaoshi/template/
.yaoshi/defaults/
.yaoshi/askama/
.yaoshi/work/kernel/
.yaoshi/work/installed-root-tree/
.yaoshi/work/installed-root-overlay-tree/
.yaoshi/work/initramfs/tree/
.yaoshi/work/initramfs/INSTALLER.CPIO
.yaoshi/work/initramfs/INSTALLER.CPIO.ZST
.yaoshi/work/image/installed-target.raw
.yaoshi/work/image/installed-target.write-plan
.yaoshi/work/image/installed-root.base.ext4
.yaoshi/work/image/installed-root.composed.ext4
.yaoshi/work/image/installed-root.minimized.ext4
```

Build state rules:

1. The product build runs `bootstrap-instance-files` before loading configuration.
2. The product build computes the current-image stamp fingerprints after instance bootstrap and configuration loading.
3. When the current-image fast path succeeds, the product build does not create, delete, or recreate `./.yaoshi/work/`.
4. When the current-image fast path misses, the resolver computes all cacheable-output intent documents before running producer bodies.
5. `resolve-cache-index-graph` reads cache index files and validates referenced objects only. It never creates staging trees, never deletes work, never invokes producer tools, and never writes cache indexes.
6. `prepare-work-root-on-first-producer-miss` removes the previous `./.yaoshi/work/` tree and creates a fresh `./.yaoshi/work/` tree exactly once before the first producer body that needs work.
7. A build that resolves every required artifact by cache hit creates no root, Debian, initramfs, image, or binary staging tree before publish.
8. `prepare-cache-root-on-first-cache-write` creates missing directories under `./.yaoshi/cache/` before the first cache object or index write.
9. `prepare-work-root-on-first-producer-miss` does not remove `./.yaoshi/yaoshi.toml`.
10. `prepare-work-root-on-first-producer-miss` does not remove `./.yaoshi/scripts/`.
11. `prepare-work-root-on-first-producer-miss` does not remove `./.yaoshi/cache/`.
12. `prepare-work-root-on-first-producer-miss` does not remove `./.yaoshi/sccache/`.
13. `prepare-work-root-on-first-producer-miss` does not remove `./target/`.
14. When an expected directory path exists as a non-directory, the active resolver stage fails with its resolver-stage error name.
15. Yaoshi has no cleanup command.
16. A developer resets Yaoshi artifact reuse by deleting `./.yaoshi/cache/` outside Yaoshi.
17. A developer resets Rust compiler reuse by deleting `./target/` or `./.yaoshi/sccache/` outside Yaoshi.
18. Product build never materializes a whole installed target raw image.
19. Product build constructs a virtual installed target graph in memory and writes `./.yaoshi/work/image/installed-target.graph.json` only on payload cache miss for deterministic development validation.
20. Host filesystem ownership, host sparse allocation, host inode numbers, host mtimes, and host directory enumeration order are never product state.
21. Virtual installed target graph logical bytes are product state only through the emitted payload extent table and hashes.
22. `cache/extent/payload-encoding/` is an internal payload producer acceleration cache. Entries are addressed by extent encoding digest and contain one validated encoded raw or zstd extent blob plus a small ASCII header. They are not `OutputRef` objects and are not referenced by cache indexes, payload manifests, product media, installer runtime, QEMU release acceptance artifacts, or final summaries.

### 3.2 Local content-addressed cache

Yaoshi uses content-addressed cache lookup for every heavy artifact. Cache lookup is always active. There is no product flag and no environment variable to disable or enable it.

Cache is an internal build accelerator, not a product feature.

The following cache operations are absent:

```text
cache list
cache clean
cache verify
cache export
cache import
remote cache
cache server
cache promotion
cache approval
cache signing
cache provenance
cache attestation
cache audit report
```

Each cacheable output is a function:

```text
cacheable-output(intent refs and scalar inputs) -> OutputRef
```

`OutputRef` is exact:

```text
OutputRef {
  kind = file | tree | composite-file
  digest = <lowercase sha256 hex>
}
```

Object meanings are exact:

| Kind             | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ---------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `file`           | One byte-for-byte regular file object.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `tree`           | One deterministic tree object whose digest covers sorted relative paths, node type, file mode, symlink target bytes, and regular-file bytes. It is used for bundles, root layers, module closure trees, and exported boot artifact trees.                                        |
| `composite-file` | One logical regular file described by a canonical segment manifest. The `digest` is the SHA-256 of the logical byte stream, not the SHA-256 of the manifest bytes. The object stores no duplicate full raw image bytes. It is used only for the final published installer image. |

Cache index path:

```text
.yaoshi/cache/index/<layer>/<cacheable-output>/<intent-sha256>
```

Cache index content is exactly two ASCII lines in this order:

```text
kind=<file|tree|composite-file>
digest=<lowercase sha256 hex>
```

with one final LF after the second line and no other byte.

Object paths are derived only from `OutputRef`:

```text
.yaoshi/cache/object/file/<output-sha256>
.yaoshi/cache/object/tree/<output-sha256>/
.yaoshi/cache/object/composite-file/<output-sha256>
```

`intent-sha256` is the SHA-256 digest over a canonical JSON document containing exactly the cacheable output's consumer-visible functional intent. The canonical JSON document uses sorted object keys, UTF-8 strings, decimal integer byte sizes, lowercase hex digests, and no insignificant whitespace. A cacheable output grammar identifier is included when a grammar, command contract, validation rule, or writer rule can change output semantics. Product version is included only in cacheable output intents whose output bytes contain or derive from the version value.

Tool path, build timestamp, user name, host name, reason, provenance, approval, attestation, audit, report, explanation, cache freshness, and rebuild mode fields are never cache index fields, are never output-object files, and are never cacheable-output intent inputs.

Producer tool version output and mirror URL are miss-time execution inputs for Debian package-root, root customization, installed-root ext4, module closure, FAT32, payload, and final image producers. They do not invalidate an existing valid cache object and do not enter the current-image fingerprints. Runtime binary cacheable outputs include the Rust compiler, Cargo, musl linker, sccache, Cargo manifest, lockfile, source closure, and build flags because those values define the produced ELF bytes.

Askama template source digests and Askama renderer context grammar identifiers enter only the cacheable outputs whose rendered bytes consume those templates. Generated helper rendered byte digests enter only the cacheable outputs that execute or stage those rendered bytes. Builder implementation source files that do not change a cacheable-output grammar identifier, rendered template bytes, runtime binary source closure, or explicit functional input do not invalidate image artifacts.

File object digest is SHA-256 of the file bytes. `tree` object digest is the canonical tree SHA-256 over sorted relative paths, file type, mode, symlink target bytes, and regular-file SHA-256 values; it never hashes host inode numbers, host mtimes, host sparse-file allocation state, or directory enumeration order.

A `composite-file` object is a regular file containing canonical JSON with exactly these root keys:

```text
grammar
kind
logical_sha256
logical_size
segments
```

Composite manifest rules:

1. `grammar` is `yaoshi.composite-file.v1`.
2. `kind` is `composite-file`.
3. `logical_sha256` equals the `OutputRef.digest` and is the SHA-256 of the materialized logical byte stream.
4. `logical_size` is the byte length of the materialized logical byte stream.
5. `segments` sort by `offset` ascending, do not overlap, and cover exactly `[0, logical_size)`.
6. Segment sources are exactly `inline-bytes`, `zero`, and `file-ref`.
7. `inline-bytes` segments contain `bytes_hex` and `sha256`; the decoded byte length must equal `len` and its SHA-256 must match `sha256`.
8. `zero` segments contain no byte payload and materialize as `len` zero bytes.
9. `file-ref` segments contain `digest`, `source_offset`, and `len`; the referenced object is `.yaoshi/cache/object/file/<digest>` and the referenced byte range must be inside that file object.
10. The final-installer composite object is the only v0.0.1 producer of `composite-file`.
11. Composite manifests contain no path to `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img` and no host-specific materialization field.

A cache hit is valid only when all of these conditions are true:

```text
index exists as a regular file
index content is exactly kind and digest lines in the order specified above
object exists at the path derived from the output digest and expected OutputRef kind
file object path basename equals the index output digest and the object is a regular file when kind is file
tree object path basename equals the index output digest and the object is a directory when kind is tree
composite-file object path basename equals the index output digest and the object is a regular file when kind is composite-file
```

A valid cache hit returns `OutputRef` and skips the producer body. It does not materialize a work output and does not run the cacheable output's miss-time validation subroutine. It does not parse large payload blobs, does not replay composite manifests, and does not check producer-local producer tools. Invalid indexes are treated as misses when all rebuild inputs are present. Invalid indexes are `BUILD` when a required external input is absent or invalid.

On cache miss, the producer body writes a temporary output under `./.yaoshi/work/` or a temporary composite manifest under `./.yaoshi/cache/object/composite-file/`, runs the full cacheable-output validation subroutine, computes the output digest before CAS entry creation, stores the validated output under the digest-derived CAS path, fsyncs the object and object parent directory, writes the index through a temporary file, fsyncs the index file and index parent directory, renames the index into place, and fsyncs the index parent directory after rename. Any fsync failure returns `BUILD` for build artifacts and `IMAGE` for image artifacts.

Cache reuse rules for development speed are exact:

1. A valid `build-yaoshi-installer-musl` cache hit prevents Cargo from building `yaoshi-installer`.
2. A valid `build-yaoshi-dashboard-musl` cache hit prevents Cargo from building `yaoshi-dashboard`.
3. When both runtime binary cache indexes miss, Yaoshi invokes Cargo once for both `yaoshi-installer` and `yaoshi-dashboard`, validates both produced ELF files, and writes two separate file objects and two separate cache indexes.
4. When exactly one runtime binary cache index misses, Yaoshi invokes Cargo for that package only.
5. A valid `resolve-debian-package-root` cache hit prevents `mmdebstrap` and Debian mirror access.
6. A valid `resolve-customized-root` cache hit prevents extraction of the package root and prevents the build-system script from running.
7. A valid `resolve-root-bridge-overlay` cache hit prevents root bridge overlay tree staging and overlay tar generation.
8. A valid `resolve-installed-root-source` cache hit prevents root tar merge work.
9. A valid `resolve-installed-root-ext4` cache hit prevents `mke2fs`, `e2fsck`, `e2label`, and `debugfs` from running.
10. A valid installer module closure cache hit prevents `xz` module decompression.
11. A valid installer base initramfs zstd cache hit prevents base cpio generation and base initramfs zstd encoding.
12. A valid installer app initramfs zstd cache hit prevents app cpio generation and app initramfs zstd encoding.
13. A valid installed ESP FAT32 cache hit prevents installed ESP tree staging and FAT32 packing.
14. A valid installed system payload cache hit prevents virtual target graph extent iteration and payload extent encoding.
15. A valid installer boot FAT32 cache hit prevents installer boot tree staging and FAT32 packing.
16. A valid final installer composite cache hit prevents final MBR/composite manifest generation and prevents creation of a duplicated full raw image cache object.
17. A hostname, root SSH key, dashboard binary, ESP runtime prepare, or first-boot script change invalidates only installed ESP FAT32, installed system payload, final installer composite, published-image stamp, and publish materialization. It does not invalidate Debian package root, customized root, root bridge overlay, installed-root source, installed-root ext4, Debian boot artifacts, installer module closure, installer base initramfs, installer app initramfs, installer boot FAT32, or unrelated runtime binary objects.
18. On an installed system payload cache miss, unchanged nonzero extents reuse builder-internal encoded extent objects from the local extent encoding cache when the current virtual target graph bytes at that extent match the cached uncompressed SHA-256.
