This cache is an internal producer accelerator only; it does not change `YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1`, does not share blob ranges inside one payload, and does not create payload-level blob deduplication.

### 3.2.1 Image reuse principle

Yaoshi development flow is reuse-first. Product build and QEMU release acceptance do not expose cache modes, reuse flags, freshness flags, rebuild flags, or image-selection flags. Developer-visible switches such as `--reuse-image`, `--no-build`, `--cache`, and `--fresh` are absent. The only developer-visible model is:

```text
current product inputs -> current-image fast path or automatic layered cache graph resolution -> published .yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img
```

A valid published installer image is preferred before resolving the build graph. A valid cache hit is preferred over rebuilding when the current-image fast path cannot be used. A valid frozen foundation object is preferred over recreating that foundation object, even when Debian mirrors, tool versions, or unrelated builder implementation bytes changed.

`ensure-current-installer-image` treats `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img` as the primary product artifact. `qemu::flow` treats the same file as its primary test subject and requires it to be current before VM launch. The current-image check reads `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img.current` and performs lightweight media checks: MBR shape, `YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1` metadata, target partition metadata, payload extent table hashes, and current published-image fingerprint equality. When these checks pass, product build and `qemu::flow` do not execute the product build graph and do not rebuild, repack, or republish the installer image.

Local build resource use is allowed to parallelize independent work units, such as payload extent encoding and independent file digest checks, while preserving byte-for-byte deterministic outputs. Cache-hit validation must stay lightweight and must not repeatedly recompute GB-scale object digests merely to restate a CAS object whose path is already derived from that digest. It is not valid to split one SHA-256 logical stream into a different tree hash merely to occupy more cores, because that would change cache keys, payload metadata, or final image logical digests.

When the current-image fast path fails in the product build command, `ensure-current-installer-image` executes the same layered functional build graph used by the product build command and uses cache hits for every unchanged cacheable output. It rebuilds only missing, changed, or invalidated outputs and their downstream dependents. If the final image must be republished, Yaoshi materializes the composite final image to `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img` through the publish path rather than creating a full raw final image cache object. When the current-image fast path fails in `qemu::flow`, QEMU release acceptance fails before VM launch and reports the exact miss reason; it does not build, rebuild, repack, or republish.

It is invalid for `qemu::flow` to force image-producing producer bodies to run. It is invalid for `qemu::flow` to define a second image construction path. It is invalid for product or test code to rebuild the same installer image bytes merely to obtain a QEMU source image.

### 3.3 Cache layers and invalidation

Yaoshi has four local reuse layers:

```text
Cargo target directory and local sccache
  -> fast Rust compilation for product, tests, and runtime binaries
```

```text
Debian package archive cache
  -> mmdebstrap apt archive reuse on debian-package-root cache miss
```

```text
Yaoshi artifact CAS foundation and root layers
  -> frozen Debian package root, customized root, root bridge overlay, installed-root source tar, installed-root ext4, module closure, and base initramfs reused by intent
```

```text
Yaoshi artifact CAS runtime and image layers
  -> runtime binaries, ESP images, installer boot image, payload, final composite, and payload extent encodings reused by intent or content block hash
```

The installed-target artifact graph is exact:

```text
configured installed package list
  -> debian-package-root
  -> customized-root
  -> installed-root-source
  -> installed-root-ext4
  -> installed system payload
  -> final installer composite
```

```text
scripts.build_system bytes
  -> customized-root
  -> installed-root-source
  -> installed-root-ext4
  -> installed system payload
  -> final installer composite
```

```text
.yaoshi/vendor tree digest or absent state
  -> customized-root
  -> installed-root-source
  -> installed-root-ext4
  -> installed system payload
  -> final installer composite
```

```text
Askama root bridge templates
  -> root-bridge-overlay
  -> installed-root-source
  -> installed-root-ext4
  -> installed system payload
  -> final installer composite
```

```text
Debian package-root boot export
  -> installed ESP FAT32
  -> installed system payload
  -> final installer composite
```

```text
yaoshi-dashboard musl binary
  -> installed ESP FAT32
  -> installed system payload
  -> final installer composite
```

```text
ESP runtime prepare source
  -> installed ESP FAT32
  -> installed system payload
  -> final installer composite
```

```text
scripts.first_boot bytes
  -> installed ESP FAT32
  -> installed system payload
  -> final installer composite
```

```text
product configuration values stored on ESP
  -> installed ESP FAT32
  -> installed system payload
  -> final installer composite
```

The installer-envelope artifact graph is exact:

```text
debian-package-root boot export
  -> installer module closure
  -> installer base initramfs zstd
  -> installer boot FAT32
  -> final installer composite
```

```text
yaoshi-installer musl binary
  -> installer app initramfs zstd
  -> installer boot FAT32
  -> final installer composite
```

```text
Askama installer boot templates
  -> installer boot FAT32
  -> final installer composite
```

The final composition graph is exact:

```text
installed system payload
  + installer boot FAT32
  -> final installer composite
  -> published .yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img
```

Invalidation rules are exact:

| Change                                                                                                                                                                                          | Rebuilt cacheable outputs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Unaffected cacheable outputs                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `yaoshi-installer` TUI, state machine, or write flow                                                                                                                                            | `build-yaoshi-installer-musl`, installer app initramfs, installer boot FAT32, final installer composite, publish materialization, current-image stamp                                                                                                                        | Debian package root, customized root, root bridge overlay, installed-root source, installed-root ext4, installed ESP, installed payload, installer module closure, installer base initramfs, dashboard binary |
| Installer module seed list, closure rule, or selected Debian kernel module tree                                                                                                                 | installer module closure, installer base initramfs, installer boot FAT32, final installer composite, publish materialization, current-image stamp                                                                                                                            | customized root, root bridge overlay, installed-root ext4, installed ESP, installed payload, runtime binaries                                                                                                 |
| `yaoshi-dashboard` TUI, sampler, reducer, or renderer dependency                                                                                                                                | `build-yaoshi-dashboard-musl`, installed ESP FAT32, installed system payload, final installer composite, publish materialization, current-image stamp                                                                                                                        | Debian package root, customized root, root bridge overlay, installed-root source, installed-root ext4, installer module closure, installer base initramfs, installer app initramfs, installer boot FAT32      |
| ESP runtime prepare template or rendered ESP runtime prepare                                                                                                                                    | installed ESP FAT32, installed system payload, final installer composite, publish materialization, current-image stamp                                                                                                                                                       | Debian package root, customized root, root bridge overlay, installed-root source, installed-root ext4, installer envelope                                                                                     |
| First-boot script bytes                                                                                                                                                                         | installed ESP FAT32, installed system payload, final installer composite, publish materialization, current-image stamp                                                                                                                                                       | Debian package root, customized root, root bridge overlay, installed-root source, installed-root ext4, Debian boot artifacts, installer envelope, runtime binaries                                            |
| Hostname or root SSH key bytes                                                                                                                                                                  | installed ESP FAT32, installed system payload, final installer composite, publish materialization, current-image stamp                                                                                                                                                       | Debian package root, customized root, root bridge overlay, installed-root source, installed-root ext4, Debian boot artifacts, installer envelope, runtime binaries                                            |
| Build-system script bytes                                                                                                                                                                       | customized root, installed-root source, installed-root ext4, installed system payload, final installer composite, publish materialization, current-image stamp                                                                                                               | Debian package root, Debian boot artifacts, installer module closure, installer base initramfs, installed ESP, installer boot FAT32, runtime binaries                                                         |
| `.yaoshi/vendor/` tree digest or absent state                                                                                                                                                   | customized root, installed-root source, installed-root ext4, installed system payload, final installer composite, publish materialization, current-image stamp                                                                                                               | Debian package root, Debian boot artifacts, installer module closure, installer base initramfs, installed ESP, installer boot FAT32, runtime binaries                                                         |
| Root bridge launcher template, Yaoshi systemd unit template, fstab template, network template, sshd config template, repart template, root shell service template, or root bridge metadata rule | root bridge overlay, installed-root source, installed-root ext4, installed system payload, final installer composite, publish materialization, current-image stamp                                                                                                           | Debian package root, customized root, Debian boot artifacts, installer module closure, installer base initramfs, installed ESP, installer boot FAT32, runtime binaries                                        |
| Debian hard package list, configured installed package list, Debian suite, Debian security suite, architecture, components, or package-root normalization rule                                  | Debian package root, customized root, installed-root source, installed-root ext4, installer module closure, installer base initramfs, installed ESP, installed system payload, installer boot FAT32, final installer composite, publish materialization, current-image stamp | Runtime binaries whose source closures did not change                                                                                                                                                         |
| Debian mirror URL or Debian security mirror URL                                                                                                                                                 | No existing valid cache object is invalidated; the new URL is used on a future debian-package-root cache miss                                                                                                                                                                | All existing valid cache objects                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `mmdebstrap`, `mke2fs`, `e2fsck`, `e2label`, `debugfs`, or `xz` version output                                                                                                                  | No existing valid cache object is invalidated; the active tool is used on a future cache miss requiring that tool                                                                                                                                                            | All existing valid cache objects                                                                                                                                                                              |
| mke2fs image contract, mke2fs.conf rendered bytes, installed-root ext4 size rule, UUID, label, or ext4 validation rule                                                                          | installed-root ext4, installed system payload, final installer composite, publish materialization, current-image stamp                                                                                                                                                       | Debian package root, customized root, root bridge overlay, installer module closure, installer base initramfs, installed ESP, installer boot FAT32                                                            |
| FAT32 writer grammar or affected FAT32 tree template                                                                                                                                            | Affected FAT32 image and downstream images                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Unrelated root and runtime branches                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Virtual target graph, GPT writer, payload writer/parser, or payload zstd setting                                                                                                                | installed system payload, final installer composite, publish materialization, current-image stamp                                                                                                                                                                            | Debian package root, customized root, root bridge overlay, installed-root ext4, installer boot FAT32 unless directly affected                                                                                 |
| Final MBR writer or composite-file writer/parser                                                                                                                                                | final installer composite, publish materialization, current-image stamp                                                                                                                                                                                                      | All upstream artifacts                                                                                                                                                                                        |

Runtime source changes never enter foundation intents. Installer UI, dashboard UI, `yaoshi-screen`, ESP runtime prepare, first-boot script, hostname, and authorized keys are invalid inputs to `resolve-debian-package-root`, `resolve-customized-root`, `resolve-root-bridge-overlay`, `resolve-installer-module-closure`, and `pack-installer-base-initramfs-zstd`, except where a changed file is explicitly part of that cacheable output's source closure or template inventory.

Each cacheable output computes its intent digest immediately before its own cache lookup, after all upstream `OutputRef` digests are known. There is no global artifact-key computation phase.

Image and layout cacheable output intents are exact:

```text
resolve-debian-package-root intent =
  cacheable output grammar identifier
  Debian suite
  Debian security suite
  architecture
  components
  mmdebstrap mode
  mmdebstrap format
  mmdebstrap variant
  hard required package list
  configured installed package list after validation and deduplication against hard required packages
  effective explicit package include list
  package request normalization rule identifier
  package index contract identifier
  Debian boot export contract identifier
  root tar normalization rule identifier
  SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH value
```

`resolve-debian-package-root` intent excludes Debian mirror URL, Debian security mirror URL, mirror current package state, `mmdebstrap --version`, e2fsprogs versions, `xz` version, Askama root bridge template digests, build-system script bytes, vendor tree digest, mke2fs.conf digest, runtime binary source digests, ESP runtime prepare bytes, first-boot script bytes, hostname, and SSH key bytes.

```text
resolve-customized-root intent =
  cacheable output grammar identifier
  debian-package-root output digest
  build_system_script_sha256
  vendor tree digest or absent state
  customization runner contract identifier
  identity cleanup contract identifier
  root tar normalization rule identifier
```

```text
resolve-root-bridge-overlay intent =
  cacheable output grammar identifier
  root bridge ABI contract identifier
  Askama root bridge template source digests
  Askama renderer context grammar identifiers
  rendered root bridge metadata rule identifier
```

```text
resolve-installed-root-source intent =
  cacheable output grammar identifier
  customized-root output digest
  root-bridge-overlay output digest
  root bridge overlay merge contract identifier
  root tar normalization rule identifier
```

```text
resolve-installed-root-ext4 intent =
  cacheable output grammar identifier
  installed-root-source output digest
  mke2fs image contract identifier
  mke2fs.conf digest
  installed_root_size_rule_identifier
  installed_root_ext4_uuid
  installed_root_ext4_label
  SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH value
```

```text
pack-installed-esp-fat32 intent =
  installed_esp_size_rule_identifier
  installed_esp_size_bytes
  FAT32 writer grammar identifier
  installed ESP runtime grammar identifier
  first_boot_runtime_grammar_identifier
  Askama installed ESP template source digest
  debian-package-root boot export digest
  yaoshi-dashboard output digest
  ESP runtime prepare source digest
  first_boot_script_sha256
  effective hostname
  effective authorized keys bytes
```

`pack-installed-esp-fat32` intent uses the Debian package-root boot export digest. It does not use installed-root ext4, customized-root, root-bridge-overlay, build-system script, vendor tree, or mke2fs configuration digests.

```text
pack-installed-system-payload-from-target-graph intent =
  YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1 grammar identifier
  virtual installed target graph grammar identifier
  GPT writer grammar identifier
  logical_sector_size
  required_block_size
  leading_gap_bytes
  trailing_gap_bytes
  installed_esp_size_bytes
  installed_root_size_bytes
  installed ESP FAT32 output digest
  installed root ext4 output digest
  installed target partition GUID constants
  zstd level/window/dictionary/LDM/checksum settings
  payload_extent_max_uncompressed_bytes
  payload_container_alignment_bytes
```

`installed_root_size_bytes` in the payload intent is the byte length of the installed-root-ext4 output object's `installed-root.ext4`; it is a downstream output-derived scalar and is not part of the Debian package-root or customized-root cache keys.

```text
resolve-installer-module-closure intent =
  cacheable output grammar identifier
  debian-package-root boot export digest
  module seed list
  module closure contract identifier
  selected kernel release
```

```text
pack-installer-base-initramfs-zstd intent =
  installer base initramfs grammar identifier
  cpio newc writer grammar identifier
  zstd level/window/dictionary/LDM/checksum settings
  installer module closure output digest
  selected kernel release
```

```text
pack-installer-app-initramfs-zstd intent =
  installer app initramfs grammar identifier
  cpio newc writer grammar identifier
  zstd level/window/dictionary/LDM/checksum settings
  yaoshi-installer output digest
```

```text
pack-installer-boot-fat32 intent =
  installer_boot_size_rule_identifier
  installer_boot_size_bytes
  FAT32 writer grammar identifier
  Askama installer boot template source digest
  debian-package-root boot export digest
  installer base initramfs zstd output digest
  installer app initramfs zstd output digest
```

`pack-installer-boot-fat32` intent uses the Debian package-root boot export digest. It does not use installed-root ext4, customized-root, root-bridge-overlay, build-system script, vendor tree, dashboard binary, installed ESP, or installed payload digests.

```text
pack-final-installer-mbr-composite intent =
  final MBR image grammar identifier
  composite-file grammar identifier
  leading_gap_bytes
  installer_boot_size_bytes
  trailing_gap_bytes
  installer_boot_fat32 output digest
  installed_system_payload output digest
  payload_container_bytes
```

### 3.4 Debian package foundation and root artifact reuse

Debian package archive reuse path:

```text

```

Debian package-root cache index path:

```text
.yaoshi/cache/index/foundation/debian-package-root/<debian-package-root-intent-sha256>
```

Debian package-root object path:

```text
.yaoshi/cache/object/tree/<debian-package-root-output-sha256>/
```

The Debian package-root tree contains exactly:

```text
root.tar
packages.index
boot/
  vmlinuz
  initrd.img
  config
  systemd-bootx64.efi.signed
  KERNEL-RELEASE
  modules/<kernel-release>/
```

`packages.index` is a Debian package-root validation index. It contains one line per installed package in ascending ASCII order by package name:

```text
<package> <version> <architecture> installed
```

Rules:

1. When the Debian package-root cache index is valid, Yaoshi returns the package-root `OutputRef` and does not invoke `mmdebstrap`.
2. When the package-root index is absent or invalid, Yaoshi invokes `mmdebstrap` once for that build to materialize a Debian package root tar artifact and Debian boot export.
3. `mmdebstrap` uses `.yaoshi/cache/apt-archives/` through sync-in and sync-out hooks for `/var/cache/apt/archives`.
4. Debian archive payload downloads happen only during a Debian package-root cache miss whose required archive files are not already present in `.yaoshi/cache/apt-archives/`.
5. A subsequent build with the same Debian package-root intent digest and valid package-root object performs no Debian mirror access because it does not invoke `mmdebstrap`.
6. Yaoshi does not automatically refresh a valid Debian package-root object when a mirror publishes newer packages under the same suite name.
7. A maintainer refreshes Debian package content by deleting the relevant Debian package-root cache index and, when disk space reclamation is desired, deleting unreferenced content objects or archive files outside Yaoshi.
8. Downstream cache keys use the Debian package-root output digest, Debian package-root boot export digest, or both as defined in Section 3.3.
9. A Debian package-root object is not accepted unless `root.tar`, `packages.index`, boot artifacts, package index validation, and boot artifact validation all pass.
10. The Debian package-root object is the only v0.0.1 mechanism for freezing Debian package content under moving suite names.
11. Host ownership of temporary root build directories is not product state. The root tar bytes and exported files in the package-root object are product state.
12. No `root-build.json`, exported `dpkg.status`, build report, provenance file, attestation file, or audit file exists in the Debian package-root object.

Customized root cache index path:

```text
.yaoshi/cache/index/root/customized-root/<customized-root-intent-sha256>
```

Customized root object path:

```text
.yaoshi/cache/object/tree/<customized-root-output-sha256>/
```

The customized root tree contains exactly:

```text
root.tar
packages.index
```

Rules:

1. `resolve-customized-root` extracts the frozen Debian package-root `root.tar`, stages `/YAOSHI-CUSTOM/BUILD-SYSTEM.sh`, stages `/YAOSHI-CUSTOM/assets/`, runs the build-system script through Yaoshi's fixed customization runner, removes `/YAOSHI-CUSTOM`, runs fixed identity cleanup, validates that package and boot content contracts remain unchanged, emits a normalized `root.tar`, and emits `packages.index`.
2. `resolve-customized-root` never invokes `mmdebstrap`.
3. The customized root package index must equal the Debian package-root package index byte-for-byte.
4. The customized root layer must not change the Debian package-root boot export bytes.
5. Build-system script logs are work-only diagnostics under `.yaoshi/work/debian/customized-root/build-system-script.log` and do not enter CAS.

Root bridge overlay cache index path:

```text
.yaoshi/cache/index/root/root-bridge-overlay/<root-bridge-overlay-intent-sha256>
```

Root bridge overlay object path:

```text
.yaoshi/cache/object/tree/<root-bridge-overlay-output-sha256>/
```

Installed-root source cache index path:

```text
.yaoshi/cache/index/root/installed-root-source/<installed-root-source-intent-sha256>
```

Installed-root source object path:

```text
.yaoshi/cache/object/file/<installed-root-source-output-sha256>
```

Installed-root source rules:

1. `resolve-installed-root-source` merges the customized root tar and root bridge overlay tree into one normalized `installed-root.tar` file object.
2. Root bridge overlay paths overwrite customized-root paths on conflict.
3. The merged tar must contain every fixed root bridge path from Section 5.2.
4. The merged tar must contain `/var/lib/dpkg/status`.
5. The merged tar must not contain `/root/.ssh/authorized_keys`.
6. The merged tar must not contain `/boot/YAOSHI/`.

Installed-root ext4 cache index path:

```text
.yaoshi/cache/index/root/installed-root-ext4/<installed-root-ext4-intent-sha256>
```

Installed-root ext4 object path:

```text
.yaoshi/cache/object/tree/<installed-root-ext4-output-sha256>/
```

The installed-root ext4 tree contains exactly:

```text
installed-root.ext4
packages.index
```

Rules:

1. When the installed-root ext4 cache index is valid, Yaoshi returns the installed-root ext4 `OutputRef` and does not invoke `mke2fs`, `e2fsck`, `e2label`, or `debugfs`.
2. When the installed-root ext4 index is absent or invalid, Yaoshi computes `installed_root_size_bytes` from `installed-root.tar`, invokes `mke2fs`, validates the image with read-only e2fsprogs tools, and stores the validated ext4 tree.
3. The installed-root ext4 object contains no boot artifact directory because boot artifacts belong to the frozen Debian package-root object.
4. The installed-root ext4 object contains no build-system script log, exported `dpkg.status`, build report, provenance file, attestation file, or audit file.

### 3.5 Debian boot artifact reuse

Debian boot artifacts are exported by `resolve-debian-package-root`. There is no installed-root-ext4 extraction phase.

The selected `<kernel-release>` is the lexically greatest directory basename under the exported `boot/modules/` after ASCII byte comparison. The corresponding exported `vmlinuz`, `initrd.img`, and `config` must exist. If any selected artifact is absent, Yaoshi returns `BUILD`.

Boot artifact validation rules:

1. `vmlinuz` is a regular file and begins with bytes `4d 5a`.
2. `initrd.img` is a regular file and is non-empty.
3. `config` is a regular ASCII text file and contains a line `CONFIG_BLK_DEV_INITRD=y`.
4. `config` contains a line `CONFIG_RD_ZSTD=y` because the Yaoshi installer initramfs is zstd-compressed.
5. `config` contains a line `CONFIG_DEVTMPFS=y`.
6. `config` contains a line `CONFIG_MODULES=y`.
7. `config` contains a line `CONFIG_VT=y`.
8. `config` contains a line `CONFIG_VT_CONSOLE=y`.
9. `config` contains a line `CONFIG_FB=y`.
10. `config` contains a line `CONFIG_FB_EFI=y`.
11. `config` contains a line `CONFIG_FRAMEBUFFER_CONSOLE=y`.
12. `config` contains a line `CONFIG_SERIAL_8250=y`.
13. `config` contains a line `CONFIG_SERIAL_8250_CONSOLE=y`.
14. The first-frame local display path is the built-in EFI framebuffer plus built-in framebuffer console. Yaoshi does not rely on DRM, GPU, or framebuffer-console modules before the first Prepare frame.
15. `systemd-bootx64.efi.signed` is a regular file, begins with bytes `4d 5a`, and is exported from `/usr/lib/systemd/boot/efi/systemd-bootx64.efi.signed` in the Debian package root.
16. The selected module tree contains `modules.dep` and `modules.builtin`.
17. The selected module tree belongs to the same `<kernel-release>` as `vmlinuz`, `initrd.img`, and `config`.
18. No boot artifact is modified by Yaoshi.
19. Yaoshi does not run `update-initramfs`, `mkinitramfs`, `kernel-install`, `bootctl`, `depmod`, or `objcopy`.

## 4. Build graph and image formats

### 4.1 Build resolver order

The product build is a cache-first resolver. A resolver stage means "return the current valid output for this product state". It does not mean that the producer body must run. A resolver stage completes by current-image hit, cache hit, or cache miss rebuild. The product build never executes work solely to restate an already-current artifact.

The bare product resolver executes in this exact order:

```text
bootstrap-instance-files
load-config
resolve-current-image
resolve-build-intents
resolve-cache-index-graph
prepare-work-root-on-first-producer-miss
resolve-foundation-branch
resolve-root-branch
resolve-runtime-binaries
resolve-installed-runtime-branch
resolve-installer-envelope-branch
resolve-final-installer-image
publish-current-image
print-summary
```

Resolver-stage meanings are exact:

| Resolver stage                             | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `bootstrap-instance-files`                 | Create missing `.yaoshi/yaoshi.toml`, `.yaoshi/scripts/build-system.sh`, and `.yaoshi/scripts/first-boot.sh` from Askama templates; never overwrite existing instance files.                                                                                           |
| `load-config`                              | Parse `.yaoshi/yaoshi.toml`, resolve configured key file paths, validate configuration, and compute effective product configuration.                                                                                                                                   |
| `resolve-current-image`                    | Compute the current stamp fingerprints and attempt the current-image fast path from `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img.current` and `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img`.                                                                                                                     |
| `resolve-build-intents`                    | Resolve scalar build constants, Debian suite values, package list values, cacheable-output grammar identifiers, source closures, Askama template source digests, ESP runtime prepare bytes, generated helper byte digests, and every cacheable-output intent document. |
| `resolve-cache-index-graph`                | Read cache indexes, validate referenced objects, and mark every cacheable output as hit or miss without creating work staging trees.                                                                                                                                   |
| `prepare-work-root-on-first-producer-miss` | Remove and recreate `./.yaoshi/work/` exactly once before the first producer body that needs work. This stage is skipped when no producer body needs work.                                                                                                             |
| `resolve-foundation-branch`                | Resolve `debian-package-root`, installer module closure, and installer base initramfs. Producer bodies run only for misses.                                                                                                                                            |
| `resolve-root-branch`                      | Resolve customized root, root bridge overlay, installed-root source tar, and installed-root ext4. Producer bodies run only for misses.                                                                                                                                 |
| `resolve-runtime-binaries`                 | Resolve `build-yaoshi-installer-musl` and `build-yaoshi-dashboard-musl` file objects. Producer bodies run only for misses.                                                                                                                                             |
| `resolve-installed-runtime-branch`         | Resolve installed ESP FAT32 and installed system payload. Producer bodies run only for misses.                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `resolve-installer-envelope-branch`        | Resolve installer app initramfs and installer boot FAT32. Producer bodies run only for misses.                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `resolve-final-installer-image`            | Resolve the final installer MBR composite-file object. Producer body runs only on miss.                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `publish-current-image`                    | Materialize the final installer composite into `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img` only when the current-image fast path did not already prove the published image current.                                                                                                       |
| `print-summary`                            | Print the product image summary. On current-image hit, summary values come from lightweight media metadata.                                                                                                                                                            |

A successful `resolve-current-image` returns directly to `print-summary`. It has already completed instance bootstrap and configuration loading. It does not execute `resolve-build-intents`, does not create missing cache directories, does not remove or recreate `./.yaoshi/work/`, does not check cache-miss-only tools, and does not rewrite `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img`.

`resolve-cache-index-graph` reads existing cache index files and validates referenced objects. It never invokes `cargo`, `mmdebstrap`, `mke2fs`, `e2fsck`, `e2label`, `debugfs`, `xz`, the musl linker, QEMU, or SSH. It never deletes work. It never materializes a large image back into work.

A failed resolver stage stops the graph. Subsequent resolver stages do not run after failure. The bare product resolver performs static artifact and image checks only. It does not execute QEMU and does not execute nextest.

Cacheable heavy outputs are exactly:

```text
build-yaoshi-installer-musl -> file
build-yaoshi-dashboard-musl -> file
resolve-debian-package-root -> tree
resolve-customized-root -> tree
resolve-root-bridge-overlay -> tree
resolve-installed-root-source -> file
resolve-installed-root-ext4 -> tree
resolve-installer-module-closure -> tree
pack-installer-base-initramfs-zstd -> file
pack-installer-app-initramfs-zstd -> file
pack-installed-esp-fat32 -> file
pack-installed-system-payload-from-target-graph -> file
pack-installer-boot-fat32 -> file
pack-final-installer-mbr-composite -> composite-file
```

Resolver-to-cacheable-output mapping is exact:

```text
resolve-foundation-branch
  -> resolve-debian-package-root
  -> resolve-installer-module-closure
  -> pack-installer-base-initramfs-zstd

resolve-root-branch
  -> resolve-customized-root
  -> resolve-root-bridge-overlay
  -> resolve-installed-root-source
  -> resolve-installed-root-ext4

resolve-runtime-binaries
  -> build-yaoshi-installer-musl
  -> build-yaoshi-dashboard-musl

resolve-installed-runtime-branch
  -> pack-installed-esp-fat32
  -> pack-installed-system-payload-from-target-graph

resolve-installer-envelope-branch
  -> pack-installer-app-initramfs-zstd
  -> pack-installer-boot-fat32

resolve-final-installer-image
  -> pack-final-installer-mbr-composite
```

Work-only generated material is not a cache object:

```text
Debian package-root extraction directory
customized-root extraction directory
root-bridge-overlay.tree
root-bridge-overlay.tar
installed-root.tar staging copy
staging trees
uncompressed BASE.CPIO
uncompressed APP.CPIO
installed-target.graph.json
publish-time yaoshi.img.candidate
```

Work-only substeps are internal implementation steps. They are not top-level resolver stages, are not public build phases, and are not product progress names. The internal substep set is exact:

```text
stage-debian-package-root-work
run-mmdebstrap-package-root
export-debian-boot-artifacts
stage-customized-root-work
run-build-system-customization
validate-customized-root-contract
stage-root-bridge-overlay-tree
pack-root-bridge-overlay-tar
merge-installed-root-source-tar
compute-installed-root-size
pack-installed-root-ext4-with-mke2fs
stage-installer-base-initramfs-tree
pack-installer-base-initramfs-newc
compress-installer-base-initramfs-zstd
stage-installer-app-initramfs-tree
pack-installer-app-initramfs-newc
compress-installer-app-initramfs-zstd
stage-installed-esp-tree
iterate-target-graph
encode-payload-extents
stage-installer-boot-tree
emit-final-mbr-sector
emit-final-composite-manifest
materialize-final-composite-for-publish
```

Cacheable output execution rules:

1. A cacheable output computes its intent digest only after all upstream `OutputRef` values and scalar inputs required by that output are known.
2. A valid cache hit returns `OutputRef` and does not execute the producer body.
3. A cache miss executes the producer body in `./.yaoshi/work/`, runs the producer's full validation subroutine, stores the validated output in CAS, writes the two-line cache index, and returns `OutputRef`.
4. Downstream resolver stages consume upstream `OutputRef` values and resolve them to CAS object paths only when they need a real path.
5. A cache hit never copies a large image back into `./.yaoshi/work/` solely for downstream lookup.
6. A cache hit never invokes cache-miss-only external tools.
7. `resolve-debian-package-root` is the only cacheable output that invokes `mmdebstrap`.
8. `resolve-installed-root-ext4` is the only cacheable output that invokes `mke2fs`, `e2fsck`, `e2label`, and read-only `debugfs`.
9. `resolve-installer-module-closure` is the only cacheable output that invokes `xz`.
10. Runtime binary resolution, foundation resolution, root resolution, installed runtime resolution, and installer envelope resolution run concurrently when their unresolved cache misses are independent and all required upstream `OutputRef` values are known.
11. `pack-installed-system-payload-from-target-graph` compresses independent nonzero payload extents concurrently and reuses valid encoded extent cache objects for unchanged nonzero extents. The emitted extent table, blob order, manifest, prelude, and payload bytes remain deterministic and sorted by logical offset.
12. `pack-final-installer-mbr-composite` emits a composite-file object whose logical byte stream is the final installer raw image. It does not store a duplicate full raw final image in `.yaoshi/cache/object/file/`.
13. No phase named `materialize-debian-installed-root-tree`, `merge-installed-root-tree`, `pack-installed-root-ext4-from-tree`, `compose-installed-root-ext4`, `minimize-installed-root-ext4`, `build-installed-target-raw`, `derive-target-write-plan`, `compute-installed-target-required-map`, or `writer-emitted-required-extent-graph` exists.

### 4.2 Lazy environment preconditions

Yaoshi checks only the preconditions required for the path it actually executes. There is no global environment check before the current-image fast path. A valid current image is usable even when cache-miss-only producer tools are absent. The only work before the current-image fast path is instance bootstrap and configuration loading.

`bootstrap-instance-files` requires write access to `StateRoot` only when one of the three instance bootstrap files is missing. When all three instance files already exist as regular files, bootstrap performs no write. `resolve-current-image` requires only the ability to read `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img.current`, read `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img`, and parse the lightweight media metadata described in Section 4.9. It does not require cache directory write access, work directory write access, `mmdebstrap`, e2fsprogs, `xz`, `sccache`, or the musl linker.

`resolve-build-intents` and `resolve-cache-index-graph` run only after current-image miss and after instance bootstrap and configuration loading have succeeded. They verify readable configured source files and validate cache indexes, CAS path naming, object type, composite manifest shape, and referenced object existence when those paths are required to compute intents or validate cache hits. They do not create work directories and do not check producer tools.

`prepare-work-root-on-first-producer-miss` runs only after a cache miss has selected a producer body that needs work. It verifies:

```text
repository root is writable
StateRoot parent directory is writable
.yaoshi/out can be created or reused as a directory
.yaoshi/work can be removed and recreated
```

`prepare-cache-root-on-first-cache-write` runs only before the first cache object or index write. It verifies:

```text
.yaoshi/cache can be created or reused as a directory
.yaoshi/cache/index can be created or reused as a directory
.yaoshi/cache/object can be created or reused as a directory
.yaoshi/cache/extent can be created or reused as a directory
.yaoshi/cache/apt-archives can be created or reused as a directory
```

All command checks are producer-local and cache-miss-only. A command that is needed only to rebuild a missing artifact is not checked when a valid current image or a valid cache hit supplies that artifact.

Producer-local command and host-precondition checks are exact:

| Condition                           | Checks before the producer body runs                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ----------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| runtime musl binary cache miss      | `cargo`, `sccache`, `x86_64-unknown-linux-musl-gcc`                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Debian package-root cache miss      | `mmdebstrap`; `.yaoshi/cache/apt-archives/` writable                                                                                                                                                                              |
| installed-root ext4 cache miss      | `mke2fs`, `e2fsck`, `e2label`, `debugfs`; `mke2fs -d` tarball input must work with the active e2fsprogs build                                                                                                                     |
| installer module closure cache miss | `xz`                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| QEMU nextest profile                | `cargo-nextest`, `qemu-system-x86_64`, `ssh`, x86_64 Linux host, `/dev/kvm` character device, current user read-write access to `/dev/kvm`, at least `8` logical host CPUs, at least `16 GiB` host RAM, and writable QEMU scratch |

QEMU release acceptance is hardware-virtualized. The `qemu` profile does not use TCG, WHPX, HVF, Hyper-V acceleration, emulation fallback, nested software emulation, or a low-resource compatibility path. Failure to satisfy the QEMU host-precondition row returns `ENVIRONMENT` before launching a VM.

Rules:

1. Product image generation runs without host root privileges.
2. `mmdebstrap` is invoked with `--mode=unshare` and `--format=tar` only on a Debian package-root cache miss.
3. `mke2fs`, `e2fsck`, `e2label`, and read-only `debugfs` are invoked only on an installed-root ext4 cache miss.
4. `xz` is invoked only on installer module closure cache miss for `.ko.xz` module decompression.
5. A valid current image or cache hit is never rejected solely because a cache-miss-only command is absent from `PATH`.
6. A cache miss that requires an absent command returns `ENVIRONMENT` before mutating the producer output path.
7. Product build preconditions never include these command or input surfaces:

```text
make
Linux source directory
Kconfig
Kbuild output directory
host zstd
host cpio
host FAT filesystem tools
host GPT partitioning tools
ext4 mutation libraries
```

8. Yaoshi's FAT32 writer, GPT writer, virtual target graph iterator, payload writer, and initramfs writer are Rust product code and are not shell-out tool surfaces.

### 4.3 Runtime binaries

Runtime binaries included in images are built as separate cacheable outputs:

```text
build-yaoshi-installer-musl -> work/bin/yaoshi-installer
build-yaoshi-dashboard-musl -> work/bin/yaoshi-dashboard
```

Runtime binary cache indexes remain separate. Miss execution is coalesced:

```text
if installer binary cache miss and dashboard binary cache miss:
  cargo build --locked --target x86_64-unknown-linux-musl -p yaoshi-installer -p yaoshi-dashboard
  validate both produced binaries
  store two separate file objects
  write two separate cache indexes

if only one runtime binary cache index misses:
  cargo build --locked --target x86_64-unknown-linux-musl -p <package>
  validate the produced binary
  store one file object
  write one cache index
```

The produced runtime binaries are ELF64 x86-64 executables and contain no `PT_INTERP` program header. Checks are performed through the `object` crate.

Runtime binary cacheable output intents include the target triple, Cargo profile, `Cargo.lock`, root `Cargo.toml`, the package source closure, relevant workspace crate source closures, `rustc -Vv`, `cargo -V`, `sccache --version`, `x86_64-unknown-linux-musl-gcc --version`, `.cargo/config.toml`, and relevant `RUSTFLAGS`. Runtime binary cache hits skip Cargo and rely on the cached ELF object.

### 4.4 Shared image constants and size rules

All generated raw disk images use:

```text
logical_sector_size = 512
required_block_size = 4096
leading_gap_bytes = 1048576
trailing_gap_bytes = 1048576
payload_extent_max_uncompressed_bytes = 67108864
payload_container_alignment_bytes = 512
ext4_block_size_bytes = 4096
ext4_inode_size_bytes = 256
ext4_inode_ratio_bytes = 8192
ext4_journal_size_bytes = 67108864
target_head_scrub_bytes = 16777216
target_tail_scrub_bytes = 16777216
source_date_epoch = 1704067200
```

Internal image size rules are exact and are not configuration fields:

```text
installer_boot_tree_regular_bytes = sum of regular-file sizes in installer-boot.tree
installer_boot_size_bytes = round_up_to_multiple(
  max(67108864, installer_boot_tree_regular_bytes + 16777216),
  1048576
)
```

```text
installed_esp_tree_regular_bytes = sum of regular-file sizes in installed-esp.tree
installed_esp_size_bytes = round_up_to_multiple(
  max(134217728, installed_esp_tree_regular_bytes + 33554432),
  1048576
)
```

```text
root_tar_regular_file_bytes = sum of regular-file sizes in installed-root.tar
root_safety_bytes = max(536870912, ceil(root_tar_regular_file_bytes * 0.25))
installed_root_size_bytes = round_up_to_multiple(
  root_tar_regular_file_bytes + root_safety_bytes,
  67108864
)
```

Size rule invariants:

1. `installer_boot_size_bytes` is a positive multiple of `1048576`.
2. `installed_esp_size_bytes` is a positive multiple of `1048576`.
3. `installed_root_size_bytes` is a positive multiple of `67108864`.
4. `installed_root_size_bytes` has no 1 GiB floor and no 2 GiB floor.
5. `installed_root_size_bytes` is large enough for `mke2fs -d installed-root.tar` to complete and pass `e2fsck`.
6. No size rule is user-configurable in v0.0.1.

Zstd payload and initramfs parameters are fixed:

```text
zstd_format = RFC8878-compatible zstd frame
payload_zstd_compression_level = 1
initramfs_zstd_compression_level = 3
zstd_window_log = 22
zstd_window_bytes = 4194304
zstd_long_distance_matching = false
zstd_dictionary = none
zstd_content_checksum = false
zstd_frame_independence = one frame per zstd extent
```

Installed target GPT partition type GUIDs:

| Name                 | GUID                                   |
| -------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
| EFI System Partition | `c12a7328-f81f-11d2-ba4b-00a0c93ec93b` |
| x86-64 Linux root    | `4f68bce3-e8cd-4db1-96e7-fbcaf984b709` |

Installed GPT identifier constants:

| Name                            | Value                                  |
| ------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
| `installed_disk_guid`           | `f67a39da-9f7a-4b92-a558-7127212cf1f4` |
| `installed_esp_partition_guid`  | `c6b0b0ea-d3c2-4e84-90da-9514010d9f34` |
| `installed_root_partition_guid` | `5c5c9f71-bc23-4f8d-80b2-6d4bb64a0f33` |
| `installed_root_ext4_uuid`      | `4d2cfe3a-96cb-43f4-b3b2-2b519d3c0f01` |

### 4.5 Virtual installed target graph

Installed target graph path on payload cache miss:

```text
work/image/installed-target.graph.json
```

The installed target graph is an internal logical byte graph. It is not embedded into the final installer image. It is never materialized as a whole raw file. It is never written to a host block device during build.

Installed target image byte span:

```text
installed_target_image_bytes = leading_gap_bytes
  + installed_esp_size_bytes
  + installed_root_size_bytes
  + trailing_gap_bytes
```

Partition 1:

```text
number = 1
name = YAOSHI_ESP
type_guid = c12a7328-f81f-11d2-ba4b-00a0c93ec93b
unique_partition_guid = installed_esp_partition_guid
start_byte_offset = leading_gap_bytes
byte_size = installed_esp_size_bytes
filesystem = FAT32
filesystem_label = YAOSHI_ESP
mountpoint = /boot
```

Partition 2:

```text
number = 2
name = YAOSHI_ROOT
type_guid = 4f68bce3-e8cd-4db1-96e7-fbcaf984b709
unique_partition_guid = installed_root_partition_guid
start_byte_offset = leading_gap_bytes + installed_esp_size_bytes
byte_size = installed_root_size_bytes
filesystem = ext4
filesystem_label = YAOSHI_ROOT
mountpoint = /
```

The installed ESP tree under `work/image/installed-esp.tree/` is exact. Its text files are rendered from the installed ESP Askama templates in Section 2.1.1:

```text
EFI/
  BOOT/
    BOOTX64.EFI
loader/
  loader.conf
  entries/
    yaoshi.conf
YAOSHI/
  BOOT/
    VMLINUZ
    INITRD.IMG
    KERNEL-RELEASE
  CONFIG/
    HOSTNAME
    AUTHKEYS
  RUNTIME/
    PREPARE
    FIRST-BOOT
  DASHBOARD/
    YAOSHI-DASHBOARD
```

`EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI` equals the Debian package-root `boot/systemd-bootx64.efi.signed` byte-for-byte.

`YAOSHI/BOOT/VMLINUZ` equals the Debian package-root `boot/vmlinuz` byte-for-byte.

`YAOSHI/BOOT/INITRD.IMG` equals the Debian package-root `boot/initrd.img` byte-for-byte.

`YAOSHI/BOOT/KERNEL-RELEASE` contains the selected Debian kernel release followed by one LF.

`YAOSHI/CONFIG/HOSTNAME` contains the effective hostname followed by one LF.

`YAOSHI/CONFIG/AUTHKEYS` contains the effective deduplicated root SSH public keys, one key per line, with one final LF. The filename is intentionally FAT 8.3-safe so early installed-boot prepare does not depend on vfat long-filename/NLS readiness before SSH access is configured.

`YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE` contains the ESP runtime prepare script defined in Section 5.2 and rendered from `esp/runtime/PREPARE.askama`. It is interpreted by `/bin/bash` through the fixed root bridge launcher and does not depend on vfat executable permission semantics.

`YAOSHI/RUNTIME/FIRST-BOOT` contains the configured first-boot script bytes from `scripts.first_boot`. It is interpreted by `/bin/bash` through `/usr/lib/yaoshi/first-boot-launcher` and does not depend on vfat executable permission semantics. Changing this file invalidates the installed ESP and downstream images, not the installed-root ext4 object.

`YAOSHI/CONFIG` is generated only during product build. The installed system mounts the ESP read-only at `/boot` and has no Yaoshi runtime path for editing hostname, root SSH keys, loader entries, boot artifacts, or dashboard bytes on the ESP. Changing hostname or root SSH keys means changing `./.yaoshi/yaoshi.toml` or the configured key files and building a new installer image.

`real-dashboard-check` is the only v0.0.1 development harness that remounts `/boot` read-write on an already-installed physical Yaoshi machine. It replaces only `/boot/YAOSHI/DASHBOARD/YAOSHI-DASHBOARD`, restarts only `yaoshi-dashboard.service`, captures the real tty1 cell and attribute surfaces, captures a framebuffer artifact only when `/dev/fb0` is readable and supported, and does not create a product runtime configuration path for editing ESP content.

`YAOSHI/DASHBOARD/YAOSHI-DASHBOARD` equals `work/bin/yaoshi-dashboard` byte-for-byte.

`loader/loader.conf` is rendered from `esp/loader/loader.conf.askama` and contains exactly:

```text
default yaoshi.conf
timeout 0
editor no
```

with one final LF.

`loader/entries/yaoshi.conf` is rendered from `esp/loader/yaoshi.conf.askama` and contains exactly:

```text
title Yaoshi Debian trixie
linux /YAOSHI/BOOT/VMLINUZ
initrd /YAOSHI/BOOT/INITRD.IMG
options quiet loglevel=3 root=PARTUUID=5c5c9f71-bc23-4f8d-80b2-6d4bb64a0f33 rootwait rw rootfstype=ext4 systemd.unit=multi-user.target psi=1 console=ttyS0,115200n8 console=tty1 consoleblank=0
```

with one final LF.

Target graph content is canonical JSON with sorted object keys and no insignificant whitespace. It contains exactly:

```text
grammar
logical_sector_size
required_block_size
installed_target_image_bytes
partitions
gpt_extents
byte_sources
semantic_required_sha256
```

Rules:

1. `grammar` is `yaoshi.virtual-installed-target-graph.v1`.
2. `logical_sector_size` is `512`.
3. `required_block_size` is `4096`.
4. `installed_target_image_bytes` equals the formula above.
5. `partitions` contains exactly the installed ESP and installed root partition entries defined in this section.
6. `gpt_extents` describes protective MBR, primary GPT header, primary GPT partition-entry array, backup GPT partition-entry array, and backup GPT header bytes emitted by the Rust GPT writer.
7. `byte_sources` contains exactly `gpt`, `installed-esp-fat32`, `installed-root-ext4`, and `zero`.
8. `semantic_required_sha256` is computed from the payload extent table offsets, extent kinds, lengths, and uncompressed bytes in the same order used by `YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1`.
9. No field in the target graph references an ext4 allocation graph or writer-emitted filesystem extent graph.
10. No field in the target graph references a standalone installed-target raw digest or standalone write-plan digest.

Virtual target graph byte source rules:

1. GPT bytes are emitted by Yaoshi Rust GPT code at their declared logical offsets.
2. Installed ESP FAT32 bytes are read from the installed ESP FAT32 object at partition 1 start.
3. Installed root ext4 bytes are read from the installed root ext4 object at partition 2 start.
4. Every logical gap byte not covered by GPT, ESP, or root ext4 is zero.
5. The graph iterator yields 4096-byte-aligned logical blocks over `[0, installed_target_image_bytes)`.
6. A block whose 4096 bytes are all zero contributes to a zero extent.
7. A block whose 4096 bytes are not all zero contributes to a data extent.
8. Adjacent blocks of the same kind are coalesced until coalescing would exceed `67108864` bytes.
9. The graph iterator does not inspect ext4 allocation metadata, FAT allocation metadata, host sparse allocation state, `SEEK_HOLE`, `SEEK_DATA`, `FIEMAP`, or `FIBMAP`.
10. The generated payload extent table covers every byte in `[0, installed_target_image_bytes)`.
11. Because the generated extent table covers the complete installed target byte span, `omitted_target_bytes` is always `0` in v0.0.1.
12. Zero extents are encoded without blob bytes and are written by the installer as zeros.
13. Data extents are encoded as zstd when zstd saves at least `33` bytes; otherwise they are encoded raw.
14. The target head and tail scrub ranges are still written before payload extent processing to remove stale boot records and stale backup partition tables outside the installed target span.

### 4.6 YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1 container

Installed-system payload path:

```text
work/image/installed-system.ypayload
```

The payload container is a regular file whose size is a multiple of `512` bytes. It contains a 512-byte prelude, one canonical JSON manifest, one binary extent table, and one blob area.

On-media order:

```text
512-byte prelude
manifest JSON bytes
manifest padding to 512-byte boundary
extent table bytes
blob area bytes
final padding to 512-byte boundary
```

The prelude is exactly 512 bytes. All integer fields are unsigned little-endian except `zstd_level`, which is signed little-endian `i32`. Reserved bytes are zero.

| Offset | Size | Field                      | Value                                                                    |
| -----: | ---: | -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
|      0 |   16 | `magic`                    | ASCII bytes `YAOSHI_PAYLOAD1\0`                                          |
|     16 |    4 | `format_version`           | `1`                                                                      |
|     20 |    4 | `prelude_len`              | `512`                                                                    |
|     24 |    8 | `manifest_offset`          | `512`                                                                    |
|     32 |    8 | `manifest_len`             | byte length of canonical JSON manifest, not including padding            |
|     40 |    8 | `manifest_padded_len`      | `round_up(manifest_len, 512)`                                            |
|     48 |    8 | `extent_table_offset`      | `512 + manifest_padded_len`                                              |
|     56 |    8 | `extent_table_len`         | `extent_count * 128`                                                     |
|     64 |    8 | `blob_area_offset`         | `extent_table_offset + extent_table_len`                                 |
|     72 |    8 | `blob_area_len`            | encoded blob byte length, not including final padding                    |
|     80 |    8 | `total_payload_bytes`      | complete payload file size including final padding                       |
|     88 |    8 | `target_image_bytes`       | `installed_target_image_bytes`                                           |
|     96 |    8 | `target_minimum_bytes`     | `installed_target_image_bytes`                                           |
|    104 |    4 | `logical_sector_size`      | `512`                                                                    |
|    108 |    4 | `required_block_size`      | `4096`                                                                   |
|    112 |    4 | `extent_entry_size`        | `128`                                                                    |
|    116 |    4 | `compression_id`           | `1` for zstd                                                             |
|    120 |    4 | `zstd_level`               | `1`                                                                      |
|    124 |    4 | `zstd_window_log`          | `22`                                                                     |
|    128 |   32 | `manifest_sha256`          | SHA-256 of manifest bytes                                                |
|    160 |   32 | `extent_table_sha256`      | SHA-256 of extent table bytes                                            |
|    192 |   32 | `blob_area_sha256`         | SHA-256 of blob area bytes                                               |
|    224 |   32 | `semantic_required_sha256` | SHA-256 over payload extent logical offsets and uncompressed bytes       |
|    256 |    8 | `extent_count`             | number of extent table entries                                           |
|    264 |    8 | `blob_count`               | number of raw and zstd extent blob ranges                                |
|    272 |    8 | `planned_extent_bytes`     | sum of all extent `uncompressed_len` values; equals `target_image_bytes` |
|    280 |    8 | `planned_zero_bytes`       | sum of all zero-extent `uncompressed_len` values                         |
|    288 |    8 | `encoded_blob_bytes`       | same value as `blob_area_len`                                            |
|    296 |    8 | `omitted_target_bytes`     | `0`                                                                      |
|    304 |  208 | `reserved`                 | all zero                                                                 |

Manifest root object contains exactly these keys:

```text
format
version
target
payload
zstd
partitions
hashes
```

Manifest rules:

1. The manifest is canonical JSON with sorted object keys, UTF-8 strings, decimal integer byte sizes, lowercase hex digests, and no insignificant whitespace.
2. `format` is `yaoshi.payload.v1`.
3. `version` is `0.0.1`.
4. `target.image_bytes` equals `target_image_bytes` in the prelude.
5. `target.minimum_bytes` equals `target_minimum_bytes` in the prelude.
6. `target.logical_sector_size` is `512`.
7. `target.required_block_size` is `4096`.
8. `payload.extent_count`, `payload.blob_count`, `payload.planned_extent_bytes`, `payload.planned_zero_bytes`, `payload.encoded_blob_bytes`, and `payload.omitted_target_bytes` equal the corresponding prelude values.
9. `payload.planned_extent_bytes` equals `target.image_bytes`.
10. `payload.omitted_target_bytes` is `0`.
11. `zstd.level` is `1`.
12. `zstd.window_log` is `22`.
13. `zstd.dictionary` is `none`.
14. `zstd.long_distance_matching` is `false`.
15. `zstd.content_checksum` is `false`.
16. `partitions` contains exactly the installed ESP and installed root partition entries defined in Section 4.5.
17. `hashes.semantic_required_sha256`, `hashes.extent_table_sha256`, and `hashes.blob_area_sha256` equal the corresponding prelude hashes.
18. No field named `components`, `installed_kernel_sha256`, `installed_esp_fat32_sha256`, `installed_root_ext4_sha256`, `installed_target_raw_sha256`, `target_write_plan_sha256`, `installed_target_layout_graph_sha256`, or `layout_graph_sha256` exists.
19. Unknown manifest keys return `IMAGE`.

Extent table entry format is exactly 128 bytes:

| Offset | Size | Field                 | Value                                                                  |
| -----: | ---: | --------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
|      0 |    8 | `logical_offset`      | target disk byte offset                                                |
|      8 |    4 | `uncompressed_len`    | extent length in bytes                                                 |
|     12 |    4 | `encoded_len`         | blob byte length, zero for zero extents                                |
|     16 |    8 | `blob_offset`         | offset inside blob area; `u64::MAX` for zero extents                   |
|     24 |   32 | `uncompressed_sha256` | SHA-256 of uncompressed extent bytes                                   |
|     56 |   32 | `encoded_sha256`      | SHA-256 of encoded blob bytes; SHA-256 of empty bytes for zero extents |
|     88 |    1 | `kind`                | `0` raw, `1` zstd, `2` zero                                            |
|     89 |    1 | `flags`               | `0`                                                                    |
|     90 |   38 | `reserved`            | all zero                                                               |

Extent table rules:

1. Entries sort by `logical_offset` ascending.
2. Entries do not overlap.
3. Entries cover exactly `[0, target_image_bytes)`.
4. `logical_offset` is a multiple of `4096`.
5. `uncompressed_len` is a positive multiple of `4096`.
6. `uncompressed_len <= 67108864`.
7. `logical_offset + uncompressed_len <= target_image_bytes`.
8. Raw entries have `encoded_len == uncompressed_len`.
9. Zstd entries have `encoded_len < uncompressed_len - 32`.
10. Zero entries have `encoded_len == 0`, `blob_offset == u64::MAX`, and `kind == 2`.
11. Raw and zstd entries have `blob_offset + encoded_len <= blob_area_len`.
12. Every raw or zstd entry owns one blob range.
13. Blob ranges are ascending in extent-table order and are never shared by two entries.
14. A referenced blob range must match `encoded_sha256`.
15. Decompressed or raw output must match `uncompressed_sha256`.
16. A zstd frame must decompress to exactly `uncompressed_len` bytes and then return end-of-frame.
17. A zstd frame header value is not trusted as a target length. The manifest and extent table are authoritative.
18. The installer sets zstd decoder `ZSTD_d_windowLogMax` to `22` before decoding zstd extents.
19. Full producer/parser payload validation fails on any malformed prelude, manifest, extent table, blob reference, zstd frame, digest mismatch, offset overflow, uncovered target range, or unsupported value.
20. Installer write-time payload validation is structural and hot-path bounded: it validates prelude and manifest shape, manifest hash, extent-table hash, entry ordering, coverage, blob bounds, offsets, kinds, zstd window constraints, and decoded output length. It does not replay `encoded_sha256` or `uncompressed_sha256` for every extent before writing.
21. Each extent's uncompressed bytes are read from the virtual installed target graph at the declared logical offset, or are generated zero bytes for zero extents.
22. Payload encoding does not inspect ext4 allocation metadata.

Semantic required hash is computed as:

```text
for each extent in extent-table order:
  append little-endian u64 logical_offset
  append little-endian u32 uncompressed_len
  append one byte kind
  append uncompressed extent bytes
sha256(all appended bytes)
```

### 4.7 Final installer MBR image

Final installer image candidate path:

```text
work/image/yaoshi.img.candidate
```

Published image path:

```text
.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img
```

`work/image/yaoshi.img.candidate` is created only by publish materialization after a final composite object is resolved. It is not a cache object.

Final installer image byte size:

```text
installer_image_bytes = leading_gap_bytes
  + installer_boot_size_bytes
  + payload_container_bytes
  + trailing_gap_bytes
```

The final installer image has two MBR partition entries.

Partition 1:

```text
number = 1
mbr_type = 0xEF
boot_indicator = 0x00
start_lba = leading_gap_bytes / 512
sector_count = installer_boot_size_bytes / 512
start_byte_offset = leading_gap_bytes
byte_size = installer_boot_size_bytes
filesystem = FAT32
filesystem_label = YAOSHI_BOOT
content = systemd-boot fallback EFI, Debian kernel, installer base initramfs, and installer app initramfs
```

Partition 2:

```text
number = 2
mbr_type = 0x83
boot_indicator = 0x00
start_lba = (leading_gap_bytes + installer_boot_size_bytes) / 512
sector_count = payload_container_bytes / 512
start_byte_offset = leading_gap_bytes + installer_boot_size_bytes
byte_size = payload_container_bytes
filesystem = none
content = YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1 installed-system payload
```

The final two bytes of LBA 0 are:

```text
55 aa
```

The installer boot partition tree under `work/image/installer-boot.tree/` is exact. Its text files are rendered from the installer boot Askama templates in Section 2.1.1:

```text
EFI/
  BOOT/
    BOOTX64.EFI
loader/
  loader.conf
  entries/
    yaoshi-installer.conf
YAOSHI/
  BOOT/
    VMLINUZ
    KERNEL-RELEASE
  INSTALLER-BASE.CPIO.ZST
  INSTALLER-APP.CPIO.ZST
```

`EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI` equals the Debian package-root `boot/systemd-bootx64.efi.signed` byte-for-byte.

`YAOSHI/BOOT/VMLINUZ` equals the Debian package-root `boot/vmlinuz` byte-for-byte.

`YAOSHI/BOOT/KERNEL-RELEASE` contains the selected Debian kernel release followed by one LF.

`YAOSHI/INSTALLER-BASE.CPIO.ZST` equals `work/initramfs/INSTALLER-BASE.CPIO.ZST` byte-for-byte.

`YAOSHI/INSTALLER-APP.CPIO.ZST` equals `work/initramfs/INSTALLER-APP.CPIO.ZST` byte-for-byte.

`loader/loader.conf` is rendered from `installer-boot/loader/loader.conf.askama` and contains exactly:

```text
default yaoshi-installer.conf
timeout 0
editor no
```

with one final LF.

`loader/entries/yaoshi-installer.conf` is rendered from `installer-boot/loader/yaoshi-installer.conf.askama` and contains exactly:

```text
title Yaoshi Installer
linux /YAOSHI/BOOT/VMLINUZ
initrd /YAOSHI/INSTALLER-BASE.CPIO.ZST
initrd /YAOSHI/INSTALLER-APP.CPIO.ZST
options quiet loglevel=3 yaoshi.mode=installer psi=1 console=ttyS0,115200n8 consoleblank=0 rdinit=/init
```

with one final LF.

The two `initrd` lines are required in the shown order. The base initramfs layer is loaded before the app initramfs layer. The app layer supplies `/init`, and the kernel executes that `/init` as PID 1.

The installer image contains no ISO9660 structures. The installer boot partition contains none of these paths:

```text
/boot/
/EFI/Linux/
/EFI/systemd/
/YAOSHI/INSTALLER.CPIO
/YAOSHI/INSTALLER.CPIO.ZST
/YAOSHI/INSTALLER-BASE.CPIO
/YAOSHI/INSTALLER-APP.CPIO
/vmlinuz
/initrd.img
/grub/
/boot/grub/
```

### 4.8 Image writers, checks, and publish

`yaoshi-image` owns MBR, GPT, deterministic FAT32, virtual installed target graph construction, graph byte-source iteration, composite-file manifest generation, composite-file materialization, and image parsers. `yaoshi-payload` owns `YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1`. Yaoshi does not invoke host disk layout tools for MBR, GPT, FAT32 construction, payload construction, composite final image materialization, or sparse payload installation. No loop device or host mount is used for image construction.

MBR writer rules for the final installer image:

1. Sector size is `512` bytes.
2. LBA `0` contains one normal MBR partition table.
3. Partition entry count is `4`.
4. Entries 1 and 2 are nonzero.
5. Entries 3 and 4 are zero.
6. Entry 1 and entry 2 match Section 4.7.
7. The writer rejects an installer image whose partition 2 sector count exceeds the MBR `u32` sector-count field.
8. CHS fields in used entries are zero.
9. The final two bytes of LBA `0` are `55 aa`.

GPT writer rules for the virtual installed target graph:

1. Sector size is `512` bytes.
2. Protective MBR is at LBA `0`.
3. Primary GPT header is at LBA `1`.
4. Primary partition entry array starts at LBA `2`.
5. Partition entry count is `128`.
6. Partition entry size is `128` bytes.
7. Backup partition entry array and backup GPT header are placed at the end of the installed target image byte span.
8. Header CRC32 and partition-array CRC32 are computed by Rust code.
9. Partition names are UTF-16LE and round-trip exactly.
10. Partition 1 and partition 2 match Section 4.5.
11. The GPT writer is not invoked by a host partitioning command.

FAT32 writer rules for installer boot and installed ESP images:

1. The image is a regular file created and sized through Rust file APIs.
2. Logical sector size is `512` bytes.
3. FAT type is FAT32.
4. File content bytes are copied exactly.
5. FAT32 volume label is the producer-required label.
6. Directory entries are emitted deterministically from sorted ASCII path order.
7. The produced image is reopened and compared against the expected tree.
8. Yaoshi does not invoke FAT host tools.

Installed root ext4 rules:

1. `installed-root.ext4` is created only by `mke2fs` from the installed-root source tar artifact.
2. Yaoshi passes `-t ext4`, `-b 4096`, `-I 256`, `-i 8192`, `-L YAOSHI_ROOT`, `-U installed_root_ext4_uuid`, and an explicit block count equal to `installed_root_size_bytes / 4096`.
3. Yaoshi sets `MKE2FS_CONFIG` to the generated Yaoshi mke2fs configuration file rendered from `debian/mke2fs.conf.askama`; the template source digest and rendered file digest are part of the installed-root ext4 intent.
4. Yaoshi passes extended options that disable lazy inode table initialization, disable lazy journal initialization, disable discard at image creation, set root owner to `0:0`, and set root permissions to `0755`.
5. Yaoshi does not run `debugfs -w` and does not mutate the ext4 image after `mke2fs` creation.
6. `e2fsck -fn installed-root.ext4` must exit with status `0` before cache index creation. Any nonzero status is `BUILD`; status `1` or `2` is not accepted because it means the filesystem required correction.
7. `e2label installed-root.ext4` must print `YAOSHI_ROOT` exactly.
8. Read-only `debugfs` validation must confirm the required fixed root bridge paths, `/var/lib/dpkg/status`, package database paths, and absence of `/root/.ssh/authorized_keys`.
9. Yaoshi does not invoke libext2fs, lwext4, fs-ext4, fstool, ext4plus, or ext4_rs as product backends.

`resolve-debian-package-root` creates on cache miss:

```text
work/debian/package-root/root.tar
work/debian/package-root/packages.index
work/debian/package-root/boot/
```

`resolve-customized-root` creates on cache miss:

```text
work/debian/customized-root/root.tar
work/debian/customized-root/packages.index
work/debian/customized-root/build-system-script.log
```

`resolve-root-bridge-overlay` creates on cache miss:

```text
work/debian/root-bridge-overlay.tree/
work/debian/root-bridge-overlay.tar
```

`resolve-installed-root-source` creates on cache miss:

```text
work/debian/installed-root-source/installed-root.tar
```

`resolve-installed-root-ext4` creates on cache miss:

```text
work/debian/installed-root-ext4/installed-root.ext4
work/debian/installed-root-ext4/packages.index
```

`pack-installed-esp-fat32` creates on cache miss:

```text
work/image/installed-esp.tree/
work/image/installed-esp.fat32
```

`pack-installed-system-payload-from-target-graph` creates on cache miss:

```text
work/image/installed-target.graph.json
work/image/installed-system.ypayload
```

from GPT bytes, installed ESP FAT32 bytes, installed root ext4 bytes, and zero ranges.

Payload extent encoding cache rules:

1. The cache root is `.yaoshi/cache/extent/payload-encoding/`.
2. An extent encoding cache key is the SHA-256 digest over canonical JSON containing exactly the extent encoding grammar identifier, byte source identity, logical offset, uncompressed length, uncompressed SHA-256, extent kind before encoding, zstd level, zstd window log, dictionary setting, long-distance-matching setting, content-checksum setting, and raw-encoding threshold rule identifier.
3. Byte source identity is one of `gpt`, `installed-esp-fat32`, or `installed-root-ext4`. Zero extents are never stored in this cache.
4. The key contains no byte source object digest, installed ESP FAT32 output digest, installed root ext4 output digest, payload output digest, or final image digest.
5. A valid extent encoding cache entry contains an ASCII header with `kind=<raw|zstd>`, `encoded-sha256=<lowercase sha256 hex>`, and `encoded-len=<decimal bytes>`, one blank line, then the encoded blob bytes.
6. A valid extent encoding cache hit requires the path to be a regular file, header grammar to parse exactly, encoded length to match the following blob byte length, encoded SHA-256 to match the blob bytes, raw entries to have `encoded_len == uncompressed_len`, zstd entries to have `encoded_len < uncompressed_len - 32`, and zstd entries to decompress to exactly the current virtual target graph extent bytes under `windowLogMax 22`.
