7. A valid extent encoding cache hit reads the current virtual target graph bytes at `logical_offset` for `uncompressed_len` and verifies that their SHA-256 equals the key's `uncompressed_sha256` before reusing the encoded blob.
8. An invalid extent encoding cache entry is treated as a miss and is replaced after a successful re-encode.
9. The payload writer always appends one blob range per nonzero extent into the new payload blob area, even when the encoded bytes came from the extent encoding cache.
10. Extent encoding cache reuse must not change the extent table order, blob order, manifest bytes, prelude bytes, payload hashes, semantic required hash, or installer write behavior.
11. The extent encoding cache is never used by the installer and is never required for payload validation.
12. Deleting `.yaoshi/cache/extent/payload-encoding/` outside Yaoshi only removes this internal acceleration layer and does not invalidate any existing CAS object by itself.

`pack-installer-boot-fat32` creates on cache miss:

```text
work/image/installer-boot.tree/
work/image/installer-boot.fat32
```

with size `installer_boot_size_bytes`, label `YAOSHI_BOOT`, and the exact tree in Section 4.7.

`pack-final-installer-mbr-composite` creates on cache miss one `composite-file` object under:

```text
.yaoshi/cache/object/composite-file/<logical-output-sha256>
```

The logical byte stream of this composite object is the final raw installer image. The segment map is exact:

```text
0..512                                                        inline-bytes final MBR sector
512..leading_gap_bytes                                       zero
leading_gap_bytes..leading_gap_bytes+installer_boot_size     file-ref installer boot FAT32 object
leading_gap_bytes+installer_boot_size..payload_end           file-ref installed system payload object
payload_end..installer_image_bytes                           zero
```

where:

```text
payload_end = leading_gap_bytes + installer_boot_size_bytes + payload_container_bytes
installer_image_bytes = leading_gap_bytes + installer_boot_size_bytes + payload_container_bytes + trailing_gap_bytes
```

The inline MBR sector is emitted by the Yaoshi Rust MBR writer. It is stored in the composite manifest as lowercase hex bytes and must have length `512`. The installer boot FAT32 and installed system payload are referenced by their file object digests and source offset `0`. The composite object stores no duplicate copy of the installer boot FAT32 object, no duplicate copy of the installed system payload object, and no full raw final image bytes.

Miss-time validation subroutines are exact:

```text
Debian package-root root.tar is a regular file
Debian package-root packages.index contains hard required packages and configured installed packages
Debian package-root boot artifacts validate by Section 3.5
customized-root packages.index equals Debian package-root packages.index
customized-root boot contract equals Debian package-root boot export contract
installed-root source tar is a regular file
installed-root source tar contains required fixed root bridge paths and /var/lib/dpkg/status
installed-root source tar contains no /root/.ssh/authorized_keys and no /boot/YAOSHI/
installed root ext4 output is a regular file
installed root ext4 output byte size equals installed_root_size_bytes
read-only e2fsck validates installed root ext4 without correction
installed root ext4 label is YAOSHI_ROOT
read-only debugfs validates required fixed root bridge paths and Debian package database paths
packages.index contains hard required packages and configured installed packages, and excluded package prefixes are absent
installer base initramfs zstd parses and matches Section 5.5
installer app initramfs zstd parses and matches Section 5.5
combined installer initramfs layers contain /init, /dev/console, /proc, /sys, /run, YAOSHI-MODULES, and selected modules
installed ESP FAT32 output is a regular file
installed ESP FAT32 output byte size equals installed_esp_size_bytes
installed ESP FAT32 label is YAOSHI_ESP
installed ESP FAT32 tree exactly matches Section 4.5
virtual installed target graph parses
virtual installed target graph partitions match Section 4.5
virtual installed target graph extent iteration covers the complete target image byte span
installed system payload parses and matches Section 4.6
installed system payload extent table matches virtual installed target graph bytes
installer boot FAT32 output is a regular file
installer boot FAT32 output byte size equals installer_boot_size_bytes
installer boot FAT32 tree exactly matches Section 4.7
final installer composite logical byte size equals installer_image_bytes
final installer composite segment map exactly matches this section
final installer composite inline MBR contains two partitions
final installer composite partition 2 segment begins with YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1
```

On final-installer composite cache hit, Yaoshi verifies only index existence, digest-addressed composite object naming, and composite object file type. It does not parse the composite manifest until publish materialization requires its segment map. It does not repeat payload decode, extent-table replay, FAT tree deep comparison, ext4 validation, virtual target graph validation, or full final-installer media validation.

Before `publish`, Yaoshi performs a lightweight current media shape check against the materialized candidate image:

```text
MBR parses
partition 1 offset and size match Section 4.7
partition 2 offset and size match Section 4.7
partition 2 begins with YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1 prelude magic
payload manifest and extent table hashes validate
published output logical digest target equals final composite OutputRef digest
```

`publish` is exact:

```text
if resolve-current-image already succeeded:
  perform no write
else:
  materialize the final composite-file object to work/image/yaoshi.img.candidate
  perform the lightweight current media shape check on work/image/yaoshi.img.candidate
  fsync work/image/yaoshi.img.candidate
  atomically rename work/image/yaoshi.img.candidate to .yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img
  fsync .yaoshi/out
  write .yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img.current with the current stamp fingerprints
```

Composite materialization order is exact:

```text
for inline-bytes segment: pwrite decoded bytes at segment offset
for zero segment: create sparse range by seek/truncate when possible; otherwise pwrite from one 1048576-byte zero buffer
for file-ref segment: try FICLONERANGE/reflink; then copy_file_range; then read/pwrite fallback
truncate output to logical_size
```

All materialization fallbacks must produce the same byte stream. Filesystems without reflink or range-clone support remain valid; they use the deterministic fallback and may copy the referenced payload bytes during publish. This fallback affects publish speed only and never changes cache keys, object digests, media layout, or installer behavior.

The installed published image must equal the final composite logical byte stream byte-for-byte.

Successful final summary labels, in order:

```text
Image:
Version:
Size:
Output:
Payload:
Target:
Planned:
Zeroed:
```

Successful final summary values:

```text
Image: YaoshiInstallerImage
Version: 0.0.1
Size: <decimal byte length of .yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img>
Output: .yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img
Payload: <decimal byte length of embedded YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1>
Target: <decimal byte length of installed target image span>
Planned: <decimal byte length of payload extent table planned bytes>
Zeroed: <decimal byte length of zero extents in the payload>
```

### 4.9 Current installer image resolver

`ensure-current-installer-image` is the only current-image resolver used by product build publish. `qemu::flow` uses the same current-image stamp and lightweight media checks in require-current mode before launching QEMU release acceptance; require-current mode returns the published image only on current-image hit and fails on any miss reason.

Current-image stamp path:

```text
.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img.current
```

The current-image stamp is an internal development accelerator. Its content is exactly five ASCII lines in this order:

```text
foundation-fingerprint=<lowercase sha256 hex>
installed-root-fingerprint=<lowercase sha256 hex>
installed-runtime-fingerprint=<lowercase sha256 hex>
installer-envelope-fingerprint=<lowercase sha256 hex>
published-image-fingerprint=<lowercase sha256 hex>
```

with one final LF after the fifth line and no other byte.

`foundation-fingerprint` is the SHA-256 digest over the canonical sorted object containing exactly:

```text
resolve-debian-package-root intent
resolve-installer-module-closure intent
pack-installer-base-initramfs-zstd intent
```

`foundation-fingerprint` excludes Debian mirror URL, Debian security mirror URL, mirror current package state, `mmdebstrap --version`, e2fsprogs versions, `xz` version, hostname, SSH keys, build-system script bytes, first-boot script bytes, ESP runtime prepare bytes, dashboard binary source, installer binary source, and unrelated builder implementation bytes.

`installed-root-fingerprint` is the SHA-256 digest over the canonical sorted object containing exactly:

```text
resolve-customized-root intent
resolve-root-bridge-overlay intent
resolve-installed-root-source intent
resolve-installed-root-ext4 intent
```

`installed-runtime-fingerprint` is the SHA-256 digest over the canonical sorted object containing exactly:

```text
build-yaoshi-dashboard-musl intent
pack-installed-esp-fat32 intent
pack-installed-system-payload-from-target-graph intent
```

`installer-envelope-fingerprint` is the SHA-256 digest over the canonical sorted object containing exactly:

```text
build-yaoshi-installer-musl intent
pack-installer-app-initramfs-zstd intent
pack-installer-boot-fat32 intent
```

`published-image-fingerprint` is the SHA-256 digest over this canonical object:

```text
foundation-fingerprint
installed-root-fingerprint
installed-runtime-fingerprint
installer-envelope-fingerprint
pack-final-installer-mbr-composite intent
final MBR image grammar identifier
composite-file grammar identifier
leading_gap_bytes
trailing_gap_bytes
logical_sector_size
```

Generated paths under `.yaoshi/` except configured instance configuration, configured script source files, QEMU test outputs, real-dashboard capture artifacts, logs, test-only harness code, and source files that explicitly enter the active cacheable-output intents above are not stamp inputs.

Current-image fast-path checks are exact:

```text
.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img.current exists as a regular file
stamp content is exactly the five fingerprint lines above plus final LF
stamp foundation-fingerprint equals the current foundation-fingerprint
stamp installed-root-fingerprint equals the current installed-root-fingerprint
stamp installed-runtime-fingerprint equals the current installed-runtime-fingerprint
stamp installer-envelope-fingerprint equals the current installer-envelope-fingerprint
stamp published-image-fingerprint equals the current published-image-fingerprint
.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img exists as a regular file
.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img byte length is at least the minimum final installer image size
MBR parses
partition 1 offset and size are 512-byte aligned and match the installer boot partition shape
partition 2 offset and size are 512-byte aligned and contain a YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1 prelude
payload prelude magic, version, offsets, lengths, and manifest hash validate
payload extent table hash validates
payload target metadata and partition metadata match v0.0.1 constants
```

The current-image fast path deliberately does not recompute a full SHA-256 digest of `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img`, does not decode the full payload blob area, does not replay the virtual target graph, does not deep-compare FAT32 trees, and does not validate the installed ext4 filesystem. Those checks belong to cache-miss producer validation, static tests, installer-side structural payload validation, and QEMU functional validation.

Current-image miss reasons are exact:

```text
missing-stamp
malformed-stamp
stale-stamp
missing-image
media-check-failed
```

Resolver rules:

1. Product build calls `ensure-current-installer-image`; `qemu::flow` calls require-current image verification.
2. When the current-image fast path succeeds, the resolver returns `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img` without executing `resolve-build-intents`, without executing `resolve-cache-index-graph`, without preparing work, without rebuilding, without repacking, and without republishing.
3. When the current-image fast path misses during product build, the resolver executes the cache-first build resolver in Section 4.1.
4. When the current-image fast path misses during `qemu::flow`, the test fails before VM launch with the exact miss reason and requires the developer to run the product build command first.
5. For every unchanged input whose cache index and object are valid, resolve by cache hit and skip the producer body.
6. For every changed, missing, or invalidated output, execute only the required cache-miss producer bodies and their downstream dependents.
7. Resolve the final installer `OutputRef` from `.yaoshi/cache/object/composite-file/<final-installer-logical-sha256>`.
8. Run `publish-current-image` with the composite materialization rule in Section 4.8.
9. After a successful publish, write or replace `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img.current` with the current stamp fingerprints.
10. Return `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img`.

After one complete successful build, a subsequent `cargo run --locked -p yaoshi --` with identical product inputs executes the current-image fast path, invokes no producer tools, leaves runtime binary producer bodies skipped, leaves image-producing producer bodies skipped, leaves `./.yaoshi/work/` untouched, and leaves `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img` untouched.

## 5. Debian rootfs, boot artifacts, installer initramfs, and Yaoshi overlay

### 5.1 Debian installed system

The installed system root content uses Debian 13 `trixie` for `amd64`.

Source contract:

```text
suite = trixie
security_suite = trixie-security
architecture = amd64
components = main, non-free-firmware
mmdebstrap_mode = unshare
mmdebstrap_format = tar
mmdebstrap_variant = minbase
```

The hard required installed-system package list is internal and fixed in this order:

```text
systemd,systemd-sysv,udev,ca-certificates,debian-archive-keyring,bash,systemd-repart,systemd-resolved,openssh-server,e2fsprogs,linux-image-amd64,initramfs-tools,systemd-boot-efi-amd64-signed
```

The default user-editable installed-system package list comes from `installed.packages` and is fixed in the generated default configuration in this order:

```text
curl,git,sudo,less,nano,iproute2,procps,psmisc,iputils-ping,nftables,openssh-client,rsync
```

The effective explicit `mmdebstrap --include` package list is exact:

```text
effective_include =
  hard_required_packages in fixed Yaoshi order
  + installed.packages in configured order after deduplication against hard_required_packages
```

The hard required package list is the only package set forced by Yaoshi source code. `installed.packages` is product configuration and exists to make the installed target immediately usable without softening the installer. Removing a package from `installed.packages` removes Yaoshi's explicit request for that package on the next build.

Yaoshi does not include these packages by default:

```text
rust
cargo
go
golang-*
nodejs
npm
python3-pip
build-essential
make
gcc
g++
linux-headers-*
dkms
```

Runtime installation performs no package download and no package installation.

`resolve-debian-package-root` invokes `mmdebstrap` only on a Debian package-root cache miss. On a cache miss it invokes mmdebstrap with these semantics:

```text
SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH=1704067200 \
mmdebstrap \
  --mode=unshare \
  --format=tar \
  --variant=minbase \
  --architectures=amd64 \
  --components=main,non-free-firmware \
  --include=<effective_include_as_comma_separated_list> \
  --skip=essential/unlink \
  --aptopt='Acquire::Retries "3"' \
  --aptopt='Acquire::http::Timeout "30"' \
  --aptopt='Acquire::https::Timeout "30"' \
  --aptopt='Apt::Install-Recommends "false"' \
  --aptopt='Apt::Install-Suggests "false"' \
  --setup-hook='mkdir -p <apt-archives> "$1/var/cache/apt/archives"' \
  --setup-hook='sync-in <apt-archives> /var/cache/apt/archives' \
  --customize-hook='<root-export-helper> "$1"' \
  --customize-hook='copy-out /YAOSHI-EXPORT <work-root-export>' \
  --customize-hook='rm -rf "$1/YAOSHI-EXPORT"' \
  --customize-hook='sync-out /var/cache/apt/archives <apt-archives>' \
  trixie \
  <work-debian-package-root.tar> \
  "deb [arch=amd64] <effective-debian-mirror> trixie main non-free-firmware" \
  "deb [arch=amd64] <effective-debian-security-mirror> trixie-security main non-free-firmware"
```

`<apt-archives>` is `.yaoshi/cache/apt-archives/`. `<root-export-helper>`, `<work-root-export>`, and `<work-debian-package-root.tar>` are resolved absolute paths. The root export helper is rendered by `yaoshi-build` from `debian/root-export-helper.sh.askama`; its template source digest and rendered byte digest are part of the Debian package-root intent.

The Debian package-root tar contains Debian package content and fixed build-time identity cleanup only. It does not contain the Yaoshi root bridge overlay, `/boot/YAOSHI/`, dashboard binary, hostname from product configuration, root authorized keys, ESP runtime prepare script, first-boot script, or build-system customization output.

The root export helper performs fixed identity cleanup before exporting boot artifacts. The cleanup is exact:

```bash
rm -f /etc/ssh/ssh_host_*_key /etc/ssh/ssh_host_*_key.pub
: > /etc/machine-id
rm -f /var/lib/dbus/machine-id
```

The helper creates `/YAOSHI-EXPORT` inside the temporary root and copies exactly:

```text
/var/lib/dpkg/status
/boot/vmlinuz-<kernel-release>
/boot/initrd.img-<kernel-release>
/boot/config-<kernel-release>
/usr/lib/systemd/boot/efi/systemd-bootx64.efi.signed
/lib/modules/<kernel-release>/
```

The selected `<kernel-release>` is the lexically greatest basename under `/lib/modules/` by ASCII byte comparison.

#### 5.1.1 Build-system script execution

`scripts.build_system` is the only build-time user script hook. It runs during the `resolve-customized-root` cacheable output after the frozen Debian package root is available. It does not run inside `mmdebstrap`, does not change the Debian package-root object, and does not change Debian boot artifacts.

The generated build-system runner is rendered from `debian/build-system-runner.sh.askama` and has exactly this behavior:

```bash
#!/bin/bash
set -euo pipefail
export LC_ALL=C
export DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive
export PATH=/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin
cd /

if [ -r /YAOSHI-CUSTOM/BUILD-SYSTEM.sh ]; then
  /bin/bash -euo pipefail /YAOSHI-CUSTOM/BUILD-SYSTEM.sh
fi
```

Customized-root execution order is exact:

```text
extract debian-package-root root.tar to work/debian/customized-root/root/
stage build-system script at /YAOSHI-CUSTOM/BUILD-SYSTEM.sh
stage generated build-system runner at /YAOSHI-CUSTOM/RUN-BUILD-SYSTEM.sh
stage .yaoshi/vendor/ at /YAOSHI-CUSTOM/assets/ or create an empty /YAOSHI-CUSTOM/assets/ directory when absent
execute /bin/bash /YAOSHI-CUSTOM/RUN-BUILD-SYSTEM.sh through Yaoshi's fixed root customization runner
remove /YAOSHI-CUSTOM
run fixed identity cleanup
emit normalized root.tar
emit packages.index
```

Build-system script rules:

1. An absent `scripts.build_system` field produces an empty script file and the runner exits `0`.
2. A configured script is copied into the temporary customized root as `/YAOSHI-CUSTOM/BUILD-SYSTEM.sh`.
3. The script runs as root in the extracted Debian package root.
4. The script observes the installed packages requested by the effective include list.
5. The script must not generate machine-unique identity. Fixed identity cleanup removes SSH host keys, truncates `/etc/machine-id`, and removes `/var/lib/dbus/machine-id` after the script runs.
6. The script's stdout and stderr are written to `.yaoshi/work/debian/customized-root/build-system-script.log`.
7. A zero exit status continues customized-root validation.
8. A nonzero exit status returns `BUILD`, prints the last `120` lines of `.yaoshi/work/debian/customized-root/build-system-script.log` to stderr after the `error[BUILD:40]` line, creates no customized-root cache index, and publishes no downstream image.
9. Build-system script bytes enter only the customized-root intent as `build_system_script_sha256`.
10. Build-system script logs do not enter CAS, do not enter product media, do not enter the dashboard, and are work-only diagnostics.
11. The customized-root `packages.index` must equal the Debian package-root `packages.index` byte-for-byte after the script and identity cleanup complete.
12. The customized-root layer must not change the selected Debian kernel release, `boot/vmlinuz`, `boot/initrd.img`, `boot/config`, `boot/systemd-bootx64.efi.signed`, or `boot/modules/<kernel-release>/` bytes exported by the Debian package-root object.
13. A package index mismatch or boot export mismatch returns `BUILD`, creates no customized-root cache index, and publishes no downstream image.
14. Package installation entry is `[installed].packages`. The build-system script is for file and configuration customization.

Package index validation requires these package names to appear exactly once with status `installed`:

```text
systemd
systemd-sysv
udev
ca-certificates
debian-archive-keyring
bash
systemd-repart
openssh-server
e2fsprogs
linux-image-amd64
initramfs-tools
systemd-boot-efi-amd64-signed
```

Package index validation also requires every configured effective `installed.packages` entry to appear exactly once with status `installed`.

Package index validation fails with `BUILD` when any of these packages appear:

```text
package name beginning with grub-
package name exactly dracut
package name exactly dracut-core
package name exactly cloud-init
package name exactly kbd
package name exactly network-manager
package name beginning with shim-
package name exactly dkms
package name beginning with linux-headers-
```

`dracut-install`, `procps`, `sudo`, `make`, `gcc`, `gcc-*-base`, and compiler-support packages pulled by an explicit user package list or by the standard trixie dependency closure are allowed in `packages.index`. They are not Yaoshi command surfaces and are not installed by Yaoshi at runtime.

### 5.2 Installed root bridge overlay

The installed root bridge overlay is a fixed root bridge. It contains stable launch points, systemd service definitions, baseline fstab/network/SSH configuration, root-growth repart definition, the first-boot launcher, and the dashboard symlink. It does not contain high-frequency Yaoshi runtime prepare logic, first-boot script bytes, dashboard binary bytes, hostname, or root SSH authorized key bytes.

`resolve-root-bridge-overlay` renders the installed root bridge overlay Askama templates into a deterministic staging tree and then creates `work/debian/root-bridge-overlay.tar` with this path set:

```text
boot
usr/bin/yaoshi-dashboard
usr/lib/yaoshi/prepare-launcher
usr/lib/yaoshi/first-boot-launcher
etc/hostname
etc/resolv.conf
etc/machine-id
etc/fstab
etc/systemd/network/20-yaoshi-dhcp.network
etc/systemd/system/yaoshi-dashboard.service
etc/systemd/system/yaoshi-root-shell.service
etc/systemd/system/yaoshi-prepare.service
etc/systemd/system/yaoshi-first-boot.service
etc/systemd/system/getty@tty1.service
etc/systemd/system/getty@tty2.service
etc/systemd/system/getty-static.service
etc/systemd/system/serial-getty@ttyS0.service
etc/systemd/system/ssh.service.d/10-yaoshi-order.conf
etc/systemd/system/multi-user.target.wants/yaoshi-dashboard.service
etc/systemd/system/multi-user.target.wants/yaoshi-root-shell.service
etc/systemd/system/multi-user.target.wants/yaoshi-prepare.service
etc/systemd/system/multi-user.target.wants/yaoshi-first-boot.service
etc/systemd/system/multi-user.target.wants/systemd-networkd.service
etc/systemd/system/multi-user.target.wants/systemd-resolved.service
etc/systemd/system/multi-user.target.wants/ssh.service
etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/10-yaoshi.conf
usr/lib/yaoshi/repart.d/20-yaoshi-root.conf
root/.ssh
```

`usr/bin/yaoshi-dashboard` is a symlink:

```text
/usr/bin/yaoshi-dashboard -> /boot/YAOSHI/DASHBOARD/YAOSHI-DASHBOARD
```

`usr/lib/yaoshi/prepare-launcher` is rendered from `overlay/prepare-launcher.sh.askama`, has mode `0755`, and contains exactly:

```bash
#!/bin/bash
set -eu
export LC_ALL=C
if [ ! -r /boot/YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE ]; then
  for _ in {1..120}; do
    /bin/mount /boot 2>/dev/null || true
    /bin/mount -t vfat -o ro,nosuid,nodev,noatime,umask=022,shortname=mixed LABEL=YAOSHI_ESP /boot 2>/dev/null || true
    [ -r /boot/YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE ] && break
    /bin/sleep 0.5
  done
fi
if [ ! -r /boot/YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE ]; then
  printf '%s\n' 'missing Yaoshi runtime prepare' >&2
  exit 1
fi
if [ ! -r /boot/YAOSHI/CONFIG/HOSTNAME ] || [ ! -r /boot/YAOSHI/CONFIG/AUTHKEYS ]; then
  printf '%s\n' 'missing Yaoshi runtime config' >&2
  exit 1
fi
rm -rf /run/yaoshi-prepare
mkdir -p /run/yaoshi-prepare
YAOSHI_PREPARE_HOSTNAME="$(cat /boot/YAOSHI/CONFIG/HOSTNAME)"
YAOSHI_PREPARE_AUTHKEYS="$(cat /boot/YAOSHI/CONFIG/AUTHKEYS)"
export YAOSHI_PREPARE_HOSTNAME YAOSHI_PREPARE_AUTHKEYS
cp /boot/YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE /run/yaoshi-prepare/PREPARE
exec /bin/bash /run/yaoshi-prepare/PREPARE
```

The installed ESP contains the runtime prepare script at:

```text
/boot/YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE
```

`YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE` is staged by `pack-installed-esp-fat32`; changing it does not change the installed-root ext4 object. The file is interpreted by `/bin/bash` through `prepare-launcher`. vfat execute bits are not part of the runtime contract.

`YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE` is rendered from `esp/runtime/PREPARE.askama` and contains exactly:

```bash
#!/bin/bash
set -eu
export LC_ALL=C
mkdir -p /var/lib/yaoshi /root/.ssh

write_state_file() {
  path="$1"
  value="$2"
  printf '%s\n' "${value}" > "${path}.tmp"
  mv "${path}.tmp" "${path}"
}

write_root_growth_state() {
  write_state_file /var/lib/yaoshi/root-growth-state "$1"
}

write_prepare_state() {
  write_state_file /var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state "$1"
}

prepare_done=0
mark_prepare_failed() {
  if [ "${prepare_done}" != 1 ]; then
    write_prepare_state failed || true
  fi
}
trap mark_prepare_failed EXIT

rm -f /var/lib/yaoshi/root-growth-state.tmp /var/lib/yaoshi/config-state.tmp /var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state.tmp
write_prepare_state running

if [ -z "${YAOSHI_PREPARE_HOSTNAME+x}" ]; then
  printf '%s\n' 'missing hostname config' >&2
  exit 1
fi
host="${YAOSHI_PREPARE_HOSTNAME}"
case "${host}" in
  ''|'-'*|*'-'|*[!A-Za-z0-9-]*)
    printf '%s\n' 'invalid hostname config' >&2
    exit 1
    ;;
esac
if [ "${#host}" -gt 63 ]; then
  printf '%s\n' 'invalid hostname config' >&2
  exit 1
fi
printf '%s\n' "${host}" > /etc/hostname.yaoshi.tmp
cat > /etc/hosts.yaoshi.tmp <<EOF
127.0.0.1 localhost
127.0.1.1 ${host}
::1 localhost ip6-localhost ip6-loopback
ff02::1 ip6-allnodes
ff02::2 ip6-allrouters
EOF

if [ -z "${YAOSHI_PREPARE_AUTHKEYS+x}" ]; then
  printf '%s\n' 'missing authorized_keys config' >&2
  exit 1
fi
: > /root/.ssh/authorized_keys.yaoshi.tmp
while IFS= read -r line || [ -n "${line}" ]; do
  case "${line}" in
    ''|*[![:print:]]*) ;;
    *) printf '%s\n' "${line}" >> /root/.ssh/authorized_keys.yaoshi.tmp ;;
  esac
done <<< "${YAOSHI_PREPARE_AUTHKEYS}"
if [ ! -s /root/.ssh/authorized_keys.yaoshi.tmp ]; then
  printf '%s\n' 'empty authorized_keys config' >&2
  exit 1
fi

if [ ! -e /var/lib/yaoshi/root-expanded ]; then
  if /usr/bin/systemd-repart --dry-run=no --empty=refuse --discard=no --definitions=/usr/lib/yaoshi/repart.d && /usr/lib/systemd/systemd-growfs /; then
    write_root_growth_state expanded
    touch /var/lib/yaoshi/root-expanded
  else
    write_root_growth_state failed
  fi
elif [ -r /var/lib/yaoshi/root-growth-state ]; then
  :
else
  write_root_growth_state expanded
fi

mv /etc/hostname.yaoshi.tmp /etc/hostname
/bin/hostname "${host}" 2>/dev/null || true
mv /etc/hosts.yaoshi.tmp /etc/hosts
chown root:root /root/.ssh /root/.ssh/authorized_keys.yaoshi.tmp /etc/hostname /etc/hosts
chmod 0700 /root/.ssh
chmod 0644 /etc/hosts
chmod 0600 /root/.ssh/authorized_keys.yaoshi.tmp
mv /root/.ssh/authorized_keys.yaoshi.tmp /root/.ssh/authorized_keys

write_state_file /var/lib/yaoshi/config-state applied
write_prepare_state applied
prepare_done=1
trap - EXIT
exit 0
```

`usr/lib/yaoshi/first-boot-launcher` is rendered from `overlay/first-boot-launcher.sh.askama`, has mode `0755`, and contains exactly:

```bash
#!/bin/bash
set -u
export LC_ALL=C
export PATH=/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin

mkdir -p /var/lib/yaoshi /var/log/yaoshi

state=/var/lib/yaoshi/first-boot-state
attempt=/var/lib/yaoshi/first-boot-attempted
log=/var/log/yaoshi/first-boot.log
script=/boot/YAOSHI/RUNTIME/FIRST-BOOT

write_state() {
  printf '%s\n' "$1" > "${state}.tmp"
  mv "${state}.tmp" "${state}"
}

if [ -e "${attempt}" ]; then
  exit 0
fi

: > "${attempt}"
write_state running

{
  printf '%s\n' 'Yaoshi first boot script: start'

  if [ ! -r "${script}" ]; then
    printf '%s\n' 'Yaoshi first boot script: missing'
    write_state skipped
    exit 0
  fi

  /usr/bin/timeout 60s /bin/bash -euo pipefail "${script}"
  rc="$?"

  case "${rc}" in
    0)
      printf '%s\n' 'Yaoshi first boot script: applied'
      write_state applied
      ;;
    124)
      printf '%s\n' 'Yaoshi first boot script: timeout'
      write_state failed
      ;;
    *)
      printf 'Yaoshi first boot script: failed exit=%s\n' "${rc}"
      write_state failed
      ;;
  esac
} >> "${log}" 2>&1

exit 0
```

The installed ESP contains the configured first-boot script at:

```text
/boot/YAOSHI/RUNTIME/FIRST-BOOT
```

`YAOSHI/RUNTIME/FIRST-BOOT` is staged by `pack-installed-esp-fat32`; changing it does not change the installed-root ext4 object. The file is interpreted by `/bin/bash` through `first-boot-launcher`. vfat execute bits are not part of the runtime contract.

`etc/fstab` is rendered from `overlay/fstab.askama` and contains:

```text
LABEL=YAOSHI_ROOT / ext4 defaults,noatime 0 1
LABEL=YAOSHI_ESP /boot vfat ro,nosuid,nodev,noatime,umask=022,shortname=mixed 0 2
```

`etc/resolv.conf` is a symlink:

```text
/etc/resolv.conf -> /run/systemd/resolve/stub-resolv.conf
```

`etc/systemd/network/20-yaoshi-dhcp.network` is rendered from `overlay/network/20-yaoshi-dhcp.network.askama` and contains:

Name=*
Type=ether

[Network]
DHCP=yes
IPv6AcceptRA=yes

[DHCPv4]
ClientIdentifier=mac
```

[Unit]
Description=Yaoshi Dashboard
After=local-fs.target yaoshi-prepare.service yaoshi-root-shell.service
RequiresMountsFor=/boot
Conflicts=getty@tty1.service

[Service]

StandardInput=tty

TTYReset=yes
TTYVHangup=yes
TTYVTDisallocate=no
Restart=always
RestartSec=1
User=root

[Install]

Before=yaoshi-dashboard.service

[Service]

`etc/systemd/system/yaoshi-prepare.service` is rendered from `overlay/systemd/yaoshi-prepare.service.askama` and contains:

`etc/systemd/system/ssh.service.d/10-yaoshi-order.conf` is rendered from `overlay/systemd/ssh-order.conf.askama` and contains:

Requires=yaoshi-prepare.service
```

Wanted symlinks:

```text
etc/systemd/system/multi-user.target.wants/yaoshi-dashboard.service -> ../yaoshi-dashboard.service
etc/systemd/system/multi-user.target.wants/yaoshi-root-shell.service -> ../yaoshi-root-shell.service

etc/systemd/system/multi-user.target.wants/ssh.service -> /lib/systemd/system/ssh.service
etc/systemd/system/getty@tty1.service -> /dev/null
etc/systemd/system/getty@tty2.service -> /dev/null

```

`etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/10-yaoshi.conf` is rendered from `overlay/ssh/10-yaoshi.conf.askama` and contains:

```text
PermitRootLogin yes
PasswordAuthentication no
KbdInteractiveAuthentication no
PubkeyAuthentication yes
```

`usr/lib/yaoshi/repart.d/20-yaoshi-root.conf` is rendered from `overlay/repart/20-yaoshi-root.conf.askama` and contains:

```ini
[Partition]
Type=root-x86-64
Label=YAOSHI_ROOT
Weight=1000000
PaddingWeight=0
```

`etc/hostname` is rendered from `overlay/hostname.askama` and contains exactly:

```text
yaoshi
```

with one final LF. This is a static bootstrap value only. The effective configured hostname is build-staged on the ESP at `/YAOSHI/CONFIG/HOSTNAME` and is applied to `/etc/hostname` by `YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE` before SSH, root shell, first-boot script, and dashboard readiness.

`root/.ssh` exists as a directory with mode `0700`. `root/.ssh/authorized_keys` is not present in the packed root ext4 image. It is created from `/boot/YAOSHI/CONFIG/AUTHKEYS` by `YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE` before SSH starts. Root SSH key changes therefore do not invalidate the installed root tree or installed root ext4.

`etc/machine-id` is a zero-byte regular file. The build-system script is not allowed to provide persistent machine identity because fixed identity cleanup runs after it. The first-boot script owns installed-machine identity generation.

#### 5.2.1 First-boot script execution

`scripts.first_boot` is the only installed first-boot user script hook. It is staged to the installed ESP as `/boot/YAOSHI/RUNTIME/FIRST-BOOT` and executed once by `yaoshi-first-boot.service` through `/usr/lib/yaoshi/first-boot-launcher`.

First-boot execution rules:

1. The script runs after `yaoshi-prepare.service` succeeds.
2. The script runs before SSH service ordering allows `ssh.service` to start.
3. `ssh.service` is ordered after `yaoshi-first-boot.service` and requires only `yaoshi-prepare.service`, not `yaoshi-first-boot.service`.
4. Script stdout and stderr append to `/var/log/yaoshi/first-boot.log`.
5. State is written to `/var/lib/yaoshi/first-boot-state` with one of `running`, `applied`, `failed`, or `skipped`.
6. The attempt marker is `/var/lib/yaoshi/first-boot-attempted`.
7. The script is never retried automatically after the first attempt.
8. The script runtime is bounded to `60` seconds by `/usr/bin/timeout`.
9. `yaoshi-first-boot.service` has `TimeoutStartSec=75`.
10. The launcher exits `0` after recording `applied`, `failed`, `skipped`, or timeout failure.
11. Script failure never fails `yaoshi-prepare.service`.
12. Script failure never fails `yaoshi-dashboard.service`.
13. Script failure never fails `yaoshi-root-shell.service`.
14. Script failure never prevents `ssh.service` from starting after the first-boot launcher exits.
15. Script logs and first-boot state do not appear on the dashboard.
16. Script bytes enter only the installed ESP cache input as `first_boot_script_sha256`.

All text files in the overlay are rendered from the Askama templates listed in Section 2.1.1 and contain the shown bytes plus one final LF, except `etc/machine-id`, which is zero bytes and is generated by the overlay metadata writer. The ESP runtime prepare and first-boot files are stored in the installed ESP FAT32 object, not in the overlay tar.

Normalized installed-root ownership and modes:

1. `/`, every overlay path, and every directory ancestor required by the overlay are owned by UID `0` and GID `0` in the deterministic overlay tar.
2. Directory modes are `0755`, except `/root` and `/root/.ssh`, which are `0700`.
3. Regular-file modes are `0644`, except `/usr/lib/yaoshi/prepare-launcher` and `/usr/lib/yaoshi/first-boot-launcher`, which are `0755`.
4. Symlink targets are unchanged.
5. Metadata normalization failure returns `BUILD`.

### 5.3 Installed root ext4 packing through e2fsprogs

The installed root ext4 image is created from the merged installed-root source tar by `mke2fs`. The installed-root source tar is the customized Debian root plus the fixed root bridge overlay. There is no canonical root fs-tree merge phase and no Yaoshi ext4 packer.

`resolve-installed-root-source` creates:

```text
work/debian/installed-root-source/installed-root.tar
```

`resolve-installed-root-ext4` creates:

```text
work/debian/installed-root-ext4/installed-root.ext4
work/debian/installed-root-ext4/packages.index
```

Installed root size calculation:

```text
root_tar_regular_file_bytes = sum of regular-file sizes in work/debian/installed-root-source/installed-root.tar
root_safety_bytes = max(536870912, ceil(root_tar_regular_file_bytes * 0.25))
installed_root_size_bytes = round_up_to_multiple(
  root_tar_regular_file_bytes + root_safety_bytes,
  67108864
)
```

`mke2fs` invocation semantics are exact:

```text
MKE2FS_CONFIG=<generated-mke2fs.conf> \
mke2fs \
  -q \
  -F \
  -t ext4 \
  -b 4096 \
  -I 256 \
  -i 8192 \
  -L YAOSHI_ROOT \
  -U 4d2cfe3a-96cb-43f4-b3b2-2b519d3c0f01 \
  -E lazy_itable_init=0,lazy_journal_init=0,nodiscard,root_owner=0:0,root_perms=0755 \
  -d <work-installed-root-source.tar> \
  <work-installed-root.ext4> \
  <installed_root_size_bytes / 4096>
```

`mke2fs` tarball input support is a required miss-time precondition. If the active e2fsprogs build cannot accept a tarball through `-d`, `resolve-installed-root-ext4` returns `ENVIRONMENT` before root image creation.

Root image validation command semantics are exact:

```text
e2fsck -fn <work-installed-root.ext4>
e2label <work-installed-root.ext4>
debugfs -R 'stat /etc/fstab' <work-installed-root.ext4>
debugfs -R 'stat /usr/lib/yaoshi/prepare-launcher' <work-installed-root.ext4>
debugfs -R 'stat /usr/lib/yaoshi/first-boot-launcher' <work-installed-root.ext4>
debugfs -R 'stat /usr/bin/yaoshi-dashboard' <work-installed-root.ext4>
debugfs -R 'stat /var/lib/dpkg/status' <work-installed-root.ext4>
```

`e2fsck -fn` is a read-only validation command. Yaoshi accepts only exit status `0`. Exit status `1`, `2`, `4`, `8`, `16`, `32`, or `128` returns `BUILD` and prevents cache index creation.

Packing rules:

1. Yaoshi computes `installed_root_size_bytes` on installed-root ext4 cache miss after `resolve-installed-root-source` has produced `installed-root.tar` and before invoking `mke2fs`.
2. Yaoshi creates `installed-root.ext4` at exactly `installed_root_size_bytes` through `mke2fs`.
3. Yaoshi writes the ext4 label `YAOSHI_ROOT` through `mke2fs -L`.
4. Yaoshi writes the ext4 UUID through `mke2fs -U`.
5. Yaoshi validates the image through `e2fsck -fn`; exit status must be exactly `0`.
6. Yaoshi validates the label through `e2label`.
7. Yaoshi validates required content through read-only `debugfs` commands.
8. Yaoshi never writes `work/image/installed-root.composed.ext4`.
9. Yaoshi never writes `work/image/installed-root.base.ext4`.
10. Yaoshi never writes `work/image/installed-root.minimized.ext4`.
11. Yaoshi never invokes `resize2fs -M` or any shrink/minimize pass during product build.
12. `installed_root_size_bytes` is not rounded up to 1 GiB or 2 GiB.
13. The validated installed root image and package index are cached by the installed-root ext4 intent digest. That intent digest does not include mirror URLs, tool versions, hostname, root SSH keys, dashboard binary, ESP runtime prepare, first-boot script, or installer TUI source.
14. Changing hostname, root SSH keys, dashboard binary, ESP runtime prepare, or first-boot script does not invalidate this phase because those values are build-staged on the ESP.
15. Changing the fixed root bridge launcher, systemd units, fstab, network files, sshd config, root shell service, or root bridge metadata rules invalidates `resolve-root-bridge-overlay`, `resolve-installed-root-source`, and `resolve-installed-root-ext4`.
16. Changing the build-system script or `.yaoshi/vendor/` invalidates `resolve-customized-root`, `resolve-installed-root-source`, and `resolve-installed-root-ext4` without invalidating Debian package root, Debian boot export, installer module closure, installer base initramfs, installed ESP, or installer boot.

The root ext4 image is a product artifact. The root tar artifact is a producer-local construction input and is not used by the installer.

### 5.4 Installer module closure

The installer runs the Debian kernel from the Debian package-root `boot/vmlinuz`. The kernel module tree comes from the matching Debian package-root module directory:

```text
work/debian/package-root/boot/modules/<kernel-release>/
```

Because the installer does not run udev or modprobe, Yaoshi builds a bounded static module closure and records the load order.

Installer module closure path:

```text
work/debian/installer-modules/
```

The installer module closure contains exactly:

```text
lib/modules/<kernel-release>/<selected .ko files>
YAOSHI-MODULES
```

`YAOSHI-MODULES` contains one module path per line, relative to initramfs root, in load order, with one final LF.

When the selected Debian module file is stored as `.ko.xz`, Yaoshi decompresses it at build time with `xz -dc` and emits the corresponding `.ko` path into the installer module closure. The installer initramfs never contains `.ko.xz` files.

The module selection seed names are exact. USB keyboard input seeds are intentionally ordered before block and storage seeds so real-device Enter input can become available before later storage probing. `required` seeds fail the build when absent and not built in; `optional` seeds are included when present or built in and skipped when absent:

```text
xhci_pci required
xhci_hcd required
ehci_pci optional
ehci_hcd optional
uhci_hcd optional
ohci_pci optional
ohci_hcd optional
hid_generic required
usbhid required
virtio_pci required
virtio_blk required
virtio_scsi required
nvme required
sd_mod required
ahci required
usb_storage required
uas required
```

The installer module closure contains no network module seed and no filesystem module seed. The installer does not mount FAT32 or ext4. The source FAT32 filesystem, target GPT, target ext4 allocation map, and payload partition are parsed from raw block devices by Yaoshi Rust code.

Closure algorithm:

1. Parse `modules.dep` from the selected Debian module tree.
2. Resolve each seed module name to exactly one module file path by basename without compression suffix.
3. For each seed, recursively include dependency modules before the seed.
4. Deduplicate by resolved module file path while preserving first load order.
5. Copy each included uncompressed `.ko` module byte-for-byte into `work/debian/installer-modules/lib/modules/<kernel-release>/`.
6. Decompress each included `.ko.xz` module with `xz -dc`, write the output as the corresponding `.ko` file under `work/debian/installer-modules/lib/modules/<kernel-release>/`, and fail with `BUILD` if `xz` exits non-zero or writes stderr.
7. Emit `YAOSHI-MODULES` in the final load order, using the emitted `.ko` paths.
8. Fail with `BUILD` when a required seed cannot be resolved and is not proven built-in by static built-in module data.
9. Skip an optional seed when it cannot be resolved and is not proven built-in by static built-in module data.
10. The built-in source file is the Debian module tree `modules.builtin`. If `modules.builtin` lists the seed module, the seed is not copied and no load entry is emitted.
11. Fail with `BUILD` when no loadable module path is emitted.
12. The closure fails with `BUILD` when any copied module path contains `/kernel/net/`, `/kernel/fs/`, `/kernel/sound/`, `/kernel/drivers/gpu/`, `/kernel/drivers/net/`, `/kernel/drivers/media/`, `/kernel/drivers/bluetooth/`, or `/kernel/drivers/wireless/`.
13. The installer module closure has no separate manifest file; the installer initramfs tree digest includes `YAOSHI-MODULES` and every copied module byte.
14. The installer module closure `OutputRef` digest is part of the installer base initramfs cache key.

The installer PID 1 loads modules before installation-media discovery:

```text
emit module_loading_started line when ttyS0 is open
fail startup when /YAOSHI-MODULES contains no loadable module entries
for each line in /YAOSHI-MODULES:
  render Prepare with input and storage drivers active and loaded count updated
  emit module_loading_module_started with the module path when ttyS0 is open
  open module file read-only
  call finit_module(fd, "", 0) through a bounded module-loader worker
  poll tty1 input while waiting for the module-loader worker
  accept Enter as controlled poweroff whenever input is available
  continue when finit_module returns EEXIST
  emit module_loading_module_complete with the module path when ttyS0 is open
  fail module loading when the worker does not return within 30 seconds
  emit module_loading_module_failed with the module path and reason when ttyS0 is open
  fail startup for every other errno
emit module_loading_complete line when ttyS0 is open
```

The installer does not execute `modprobe`, `insmod`, `depmod`, or a shell.

### 5.5 Installer initramfs

The installer initramfs is split into two zstd-compressed `newc` layers. The boot loader entry loads them in this exact order:

```text
/YAOSHI/INSTALLER-BASE.CPIO.ZST
/YAOSHI/INSTALLER-APP.CPIO.ZST
```

The base layer is stable with respect to installer app changes. It contains device nodes, mountpoint directories, `YAOSHI-MODULES`, and the bounded module closure. It does not contain `/init` and does not contain the installer app binary.

The app layer is the high-frequency installer development layer. It contains `/init` as the static musl `yaoshi-installer` binary and no module closure. When the kernel finishes unpacking both initramfs layers, `/init` exists and is executed as process ID 1.

`pack-installer-base-initramfs-zstd` creates a rootless staging tree under `work/initramfs/base.tree/`:

```text
dev/
proc/
run/
sys/
lib/
lib/modules/
lib/modules/<kernel-release>/
YAOSHI-MODULES
```

The copied module files are the exact installer module closure from Section 5.4.

The base cpio entry set contains:

```text
dev/ directory mode 0755
dev/console character device mode 0600 major 5 minor 1
proc/ directory mode 0755
run/ directory mode 0755
sys/ directory mode 0755
lib/ directory mode 0755
lib/modules/ directory mode 0755
lib/modules/<kernel-release>/ directory mode 0755
each selected module file regular file mode 0644
YAOSHI-MODULES regular file mode 0644
```

`pack-installer-app-initramfs-zstd` creates a rootless staging tree under `work/initramfs/app.tree/`:

```text
init
```

The app cpio entry set contains:

```text
init regular file mode 0755
```

`init` is the static musl `yaoshi-installer` binary. The combined installer userspace contains no package manager, no service manager, no shell, no dynamically linked userspace program, no Debian rootfs, no login, no SSH, no network daemon, and no dashboard.

`pack-installer-base-initramfs-zstd` creates on cache miss:

```text
work/initramfs/BASE.CPIO
work/initramfs/INSTALLER-BASE.CPIO.ZST
```

`pack-installer-app-initramfs-zstd` creates on cache miss:

```text
work/initramfs/APP.CPIO
work/initramfs/INSTALLER-APP.CPIO.ZST
```

Archive rules:

1. The archive format is `newc` cpio.
2. The archive is generated by Rust code in `yaoshi-initramfs`.
3. Archive entry uid is `0`.
4. Archive entry gid is `0`.
5. Archive entry mtime is `0`.
6. Directory link count is `2`.
7. Regular-file and character-device link count is `1`.
8. Entries are emitted in lexical byte order, except `TRAILER!!!` is emitted last.
9. Yaoshi does not invoke a host `cpio` command.

Compression rules:

1. Each compressed initramfs layer is one RFC 8878-compatible zstd frame.
2. The initramfs zstd compression level is `3`.
3. The zstd window log is `22`.
4. The frame uses no dictionary.
5. The frame uses no long-distance matching.
6. The frame content checksum flag is disabled.
7. Compression is performed by Rust code through the workspace zstd dependency.
8. Yaoshi does not invoke a host `zstd` command for initramfs compression.
9. Each compressed file begins with bytes `28 b5 2f fd`.
10. Decompression of `INSTALLER-BASE.CPIO.ZST` yields exactly the bytes of `work/initramfs/BASE.CPIO`.
11. Decompression of `INSTALLER-APP.CPIO.ZST` yields exactly the bytes of `work/initramfs/APP.CPIO`.
12. The cache objects for these phases are only `INSTALLER-BASE.CPIO.ZST` and `INSTALLER-APP.CPIO.ZST`; staging trees and uncompressed cpio files are work-only files.

Installer minimality validation is exact:

```text
base CPIO contains /dev/console
base CPIO contains /proc, /sys, and /run mountpoint directories
base CPIO contains only the module closure copied under /lib/modules/<kernel-release>/
base CPIO contains YAOSHI-MODULES
base CPIO contains no /init
base CPIO contains no /bin directory
base CPIO contains no /sbin directory
base CPIO contains no /usr directory
base CPIO contains no /etc directory
base CPIO contains no /var directory
base CPIO contains no /root directory
base CPIO contains no /home directory
base CPIO contains no /tmp directory
base CPIO contains no dynamic linker path
base CPIO contains no package database
base CPIO contains no shell binary
base CPIO contains no network daemon
base CPIO contains no dashboard binary
app CPIO contains /init
app CPIO contains no module file
app CPIO contains no /bin directory
app CPIO contains no /sbin directory
app CPIO contains no /usr directory
app CPIO contains no /etc directory
app CPIO contains no /var directory
app CPIO contains no /root directory
app CPIO contains no /home directory
app CPIO contains no /tmp directory
app CPIO contains no dynamic linker path
app CPIO contains no package database
app CPIO contains no shell binary
app CPIO contains no network daemon
app CPIO contains no dashboard binary
combined initramfs layers contain /init, /dev/console, /proc, /sys, /run, YAOSHI-MODULES, and the selected module files
combined initramfs layers contain no Debian rootfs, package manager, systemd, udev, SSH, dashboard, documentation, locales, or package database
INSTALLER-BASE.CPIO.ZST, INSTALLER-APP.CPIO.ZST, and installer boot artifacts fit inside installer_boot_size_bytes with at least 1048576 bytes of unused FAT32 data-cluster capacity
```

The installer is small by construction. The size boundary is the exact file set plus the required local-block-device module closure and the single static app binary, not a tunable optimization pass. Yaoshi does not run stripping, pruning, package removal, filesystem deduplication, or post-build minimization commands on the installer initramfs layers.

## 6. Installer runtime and sparse payload installation

### 6.1 TTY ownership and startup

Installer runtime owns:

| TTY     | Owner              | Behavior                                           |
| ------- | ------------------ | -------------------------------------------------- |
| `tty1`  | `yaoshi-installer` | Borderless installer transaction screen and input. |
| `ttyS0` | `yaoshi-installer` | QEMU synchronization lines when available.         |

No getty and no shell run in the installer runtime.

`yaoshi-installer` runs as process ID 1.

Startup order:

```text
install panic handler
open /dev/console best-effort
mount devtmpfs at /dev
mount procfs at /proc
mount sysfs at /sys
mount tmpfs at /run
create /run/yaoshi
open /dev/tty1 for installer screen
apply installer display negotiation on tty1
open /dev/ttyS0 for QEMU synchronization when the device node exists
emit display marker when ttyS0 is open
render and flush first Prepare screen on tty1 with modules not-started, media not-started, and payload not opened
emit first_frame_flushed marker when ttyS0 is open
load modules from /YAOSHI-MODULES with count-based Prepare rendering, per-module serial markers, bounded finit_module workers, and tty1 Enter polling
enter installer state machine at DiscoverSource
```

A required startup failure before tty1 rendering writes:

```text
Yaoshi installer startup failure: <reason>
```

to `/dev/console` when `/dev/console` is writable and then enters a halted failure loop. A required startup failure after the first frame has been flushed enters `Failure` and renders `Stopped` on tty1.

Module loading is startup work after the first frame. A module-loading failure enters `Failure` with reason `module-load-failed` and `failure_write_state = no-target-writes`.

### 6.2 Installer phases and transition graph

Installer phases:

```text
Start
DiscoverSource
InspectDisks
SelectDisk
ConfirmDisk
WriteTarget
Complete
Failure
ControlledExit
Halted
```

Transition graph:

```text
Start -> DiscoverSource | ControlledExit
DiscoverSource -> InspectDisks | Failure | ControlledExit
InspectDisks -> SelectDisk | Failure | ControlledExit
SelectDisk -> ConfirmDisk | InspectDisks | ControlledExit
ConfirmDisk -> WriteTarget | SelectDisk | Failure
WriteTarget -> Complete | Failure
Complete -> ControlledExit
Failure -> ControlledExit
ControlledExit -> Halted
```

There is no transition from `WriteTarget` to Back, Cancel, Retry, Force, Shell, Manual Command, Account, Password, Network, Settings, or another operator-interrupt state.

### 6.3 First frame rule

The installer renders and flushes a complete `Prepare` screen on tty1 before:

```text
installer module loading
installation-media discovery
payload partition inspection
payload manifest parsing
target disk discovery
opening a target for writing
starting target writes
```

The first frame contains these semantic facts:

```text
surface = installer
phase = Prepare
safe state = no disk will be changed
action = Power off
```

When `/dev/ttyS0` is open, the first-frame synchronization line is:

```text
YAOSHI_MARK v=1 kind=installer event=first_frame_flushed
```

Module-loading synchronization lines are:

```text
YAOSHI_MARK v=1 kind=installer event=module_loading_started
YAOSHI_MARK v=1 kind=installer event=module_loading_complete
```

`module_loading_started` is emitted only after `first_frame_flushed`.

The Debian kernel, systemd-boot fallback binary, zstd-compressed installer initramfs, devtmpfs/procfs/sysfs/tmpfs mounts, tty1 open, built-in EFI framebuffer, built-in framebuffer console, and display negotiation are part of the boot path before this rule. The installer module closure, DRM modules, GPU modules, framebuffer-console modules, installed-system sparse payload, installation-media discovery, payload parsing, target discovery, and target-write opens are not part of the first-frame path.

### 6.4 Disk identity and source discovery

The installer does not depend on udev. It reads device identity from sysfs and opens block devices through devtmpfs names under `/dev`.

`KernelDiskRef` contains:

```text
sysfs_path
major_minor
kernel_name
dev_path
logical_block_size
byte_size
model
serial
stable_disk_id
```

`stable_disk_id` resolution is exact:

```text
read /sys/block/<disk>/device/wwid when present and non-empty
else read /sys/block/<disk>/device/eui when present and non-empty
else read /sys/block/<disk>/device/uuid when present and non-empty
else read /sys/block/<disk>/device/serial when present and non-empty
else stable_disk_id is unavailable
sanitize selected value through AsciiCellString
render stable_disk_id as sysfs:<selected-value>
```

`DiscoverSource` performs repeated installation-media discovery for `10.000` seconds. It starts the first discovery pass after installer module loading completes. It sleeps `200` milliseconds between discovery passes when no valid source disk is found.

Each discovery pass performs this order through Rust code:

```text
list whole disks under /sys/block
exclude kernel names starting with ram, loop, dm-, md, zram, sr, or fd
read /sys/block/<disk>/queue/logical_block_size
exclude disks whose logical block size is not 512
open each remaining whole disk read-only through /dev/<kernel_name>
parse installer MBR
find disks whose MBR partition 1 and partition 2 match this document
open partition 2 as YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1
parse payload prelude
parse payload manifest
parse payload extent table
verify payload manifest and extent-table hashes
verify payload target_minimum_bytes is nonzero
verify payload manifest partitions contain YAOSHI_ESP and YAOSHI_ROOT and contain no artifact-only component hashes
```

The runtime source-discovery path is deliberately lightweight: it does not read
partition 1 FAT32 files and does not read the payload blob area while looking for
installer media. Each candidate source probe is bounded by
`source_probe_timeout = 2 s`; a timed-out candidate is ignored for that discovery
pass. Final-image construction and current-image preflight retain the complete
installer boot tree validation, and target writing performs structural payload
validation plus functional target writes without installer-time blob-area or
per-extent digest replay.

When exactly one valid source disk is found, the installer records source disk and payload metadata and emits:

```text
YAOSHI_MARK v=1 kind=installer event=installation_media_discovered
```

when `/dev/ttyS0` is open.

When zero valid source disks exist after `10.000` seconds, the installer enters `Failure` with reason `installation-media-not-found`. When more than one valid source disk appears in any discovery pass, the installer enters `Failure` with reason `installation-media-ambiguous`.

### 6.5 Target disk discovery

`InspectDisks` performs read-only target disk discovery. It reads:

```text
/sys/block whole disk entries
/sys/block partition entries
/sys/block/<disk>/dev
/sys/block/<disk>/size
/sys/block/<disk>/queue/logical_block_size
/sys/block/<disk>/device/model
/sys/block/<disk>/device/serial
/sys/block/<disk>/device/wwid
/sys/block/<disk>/device/eui
/sys/block/<disk>/device/uuid
MBR or GPT partition tables through read-only opens
```

Discovery does not open block devices for writing.
Each read-only partition-table classification is bounded by
`disk_classify_timeout = 2 s`; a timed-out disk is rendered as unreadable rather
than blocking target selection.

Candidate status values:

```text
selectable
installer-media
installed-target
blocked-by-installed-target
too-small
unsupported-sector-size
no-stable-id
read-error
```

A disk is `selectable` when all of these are true:

```text
it is a whole disk under /sys/block
it is not the source disk
its kernel name does not start with ram, loop, dm-, md, zram, sr, or fd
its logical block size is 512
its byte size is at least payload_target_minimum_bytes
its KernelDiskRef is readable
its stable_disk_id is not unavailable
no other Yaoshi installed target disk is attached
```

A disk is `installed-target` when it contains the Yaoshi installed GPT identity. When any non-source installed target is attached, every other non-source disk is `blocked-by-installed-target` and is not selectable. This prevents creating duplicate Yaoshi GPT and PARTUUID identities on the same machine.

`InspectDisks` enters `SelectDisk` after one complete discovery pass. Zero target-action disks is a valid `SelectDisk` state.

### 6.6 Write start rule

Activating the destructive install action enters a locked write transaction. The renderer must show the locked `Write` scene with `verify target` active before the installer re-reads the selected target, revalidates the payload, or opens the target for writing.

The write transaction starts only after this sequence:

```text
phase is ConfirmDisk
operator activates Install Yaoshi to <dev_path>
installer enters WriteTarget
installer renders and flushes Write with verify target active, every later write task pending, and input locked
installer re-reads selected target KernelDiskRef
re-read target matches selected sysfs_path, major_minor, kernel_name, and stable_disk_id
re-read target is not the source disk
re-read target logical block size is 512
re-read target byte size is at least payload_target_minimum_bytes
installer verifies no Yaoshi installed target disk is attached
installer validates source payload prelude, manifest, extent table, zstd bounds, and target bounds structurally
installer opens the selected target write path
installer marks verify target done
installer emits target_write_started when ttyS0 is open
installer renders and flushes Write with prepare disk beginning active
```

Rules:

1. No target is opened for writing before the `verify target` task opens the selected target.
2. No target byte is written before `target_write_started` has been emitted when ttyS0 is available.
3. Back returns from `ConfirmDisk` to `SelectDisk` only before the destructive action is activated.
4. After the destructive action is activated, the write transaction is locked; Back, Cancel, Retry, Force, Shell, Manual Command, Account, Password, Network, Settings, and operator-interrupt states are absent.
5. Yaoshi does not restore previous disk contents after target writes start.
6. The selected target write path opened by `verify target` is the only target disk used by `WriteTarget`; the runtime may duplicate the selected target file descriptor for bounded parallel offset writes.
7. Selected-target identity mismatch enters `Failure` with reason `selected-disk-changed` and `failure_write_state = no-target-writes`.
8. Source payload structural validation failure enters `Failure` with reason `payload-validation-failed` and `failure_write_state = no-target-writes`.
9. Target write open failure enters `Failure` with reason `target-open-failed` and `failure_write_state = no-target-writes`.
10. `failure_write_state` is `no-target-writes` before the `target_write_started` serial event is emitted.
11. `failure_write_state` is `target-write-started` after the `target_write_started` serial event is emitted.

### 6.7 Target write operation

`WriteTarget` is rendered and executed as five product tasks. These tasks are the only task names used by the Write scene:

```text
verify target
prepare disk beginning
prepare disk end
copy Yaoshi image
finalize writes
```

The task token set used by ttyS0 synchronization is exact:

```text
verify-target
prepare-disk-beginning
prepare-disk-end
copy-yaoshi-image
finalize-writes
```

Task token order is exact:

```text
verify-target -> prepare-disk-beginning -> prepare-disk-end -> copy-yaoshi-image -> finalize-writes
```

After the Write Start Rule enters `WriteTarget`, the runtime performs this order:

```text
emit write_task task=verify-target state=active line when ttyS0 is open
render and flush Write with verify target active
re-read selected target KernelDiskRef
validate source payload prelude, manifest, extent table, zstd bounds, and target bounds structurally
open the selected target write path
emit write_task task=verify-target state=done line when ttyS0 is open
emit target_write_started line when ttyS0 is open

emit write_task task=prepare-disk-beginning state=active line when ttyS0 is open
zero byte range [0, min(target_head_scrub_bytes, target_size)) on selected target
emit write_task task=prepare-disk-beginning state=done line when ttyS0 is open

emit write_task task=prepare-disk-end state=active line when ttyS0 is open
zero byte range [target_size - target_tail_scrub_bytes, target_size) on selected target
emit write_task task=prepare-disk-end state=done line when ttyS0 is open

emit write_task task=copy-yaoshi-image state=active line when ttyS0 is open
process payload entries in extent-table order with offset reads and offset writes; do not use shared seek state
for each raw extent: read encoded bytes and write them to logical_offset
for each zstd extent: read encoded frame, decompress with windowLogMax 22, verify exact output length, and write decoded bytes to logical_offset
for each zero extent: clear the declared target range through the target zero writer without materializing a zero data buffer when the kernel supports a zeroing primitive
write target data with offset writes at declared logical target offsets
emit write_progress at bounded intervals and after the final planned-byte count reaches payload_planned_extent_bytes when ttyS0 is open
verify written planned bytes equals payload_planned_extent_bytes
emit write_task task=copy-yaoshi-image state=done line when ttyS0 is open

emit write_task task=finalize-writes state=active line when ttyS0 is open
sync the selected target file, sync the target filesystem, and invoke global sync
close selected target write descriptors
emit write_task task=finalize-writes state=done line when ttyS0 is open
emit Complete
```

Write-target serial synchronization lines are exact:

```text
YAOSHI_MARK v=1 kind=installer event=target_write_started
YAOSHI_MARK v=1 kind=installer event=write_task task=<write_task> state=<active|done>
YAOSHI_MARK v=1 kind=installer event=write_progress task=copy-yaoshi-image extent=<processed_extent_count>/<payload_extent_count> written=<planned_written_bytes>/<payload_planned_extent_bytes> source=<source_read_bytes>/<payload_source_bytes> zeroed=<zero_written_bytes>/<payload_zero_extent_bytes> percent=<progress_percent>
```

`write_progress` is emitted at bounded intervals, at task transitions, and after the final planned-byte count reaches `payload_planned_extent_bytes`. It is not emitted once per extent. `<zero_written_bytes>` is the accumulated uncompressed byte length of processed payload zero extents. It is a ttyS0 diagnosis contract only and does not add product-visible text to tty1.

Write task progress rules:

1. `verify target` has no gauge and no percentage.
2. `prepare disk beginning` has its own task progress values: `head_scrub_written_bytes`, `target_head_scrub_bytes`, and `head_scrub_percent`.
3. `prepare disk end` has its own task progress values: `tail_scrub_written_bytes`, `target_tail_scrub_bytes`, and `tail_scrub_percent`.
4. For a successful `BLKZEROOUT` scrub, the scrub task remains at `0.000%` while the ioctl is in progress and then advances directly to `100.000%` when the ioctl returns success.
5. For a fallback buffered scrub, the scrub task advances after each successful `pwrite_all` chunk.
6. `copy Yaoshi image` has its own task progress values: `planned_written_bytes`, `payload_planned_extent_bytes`, and `progress_percent`.
7. `finalize writes` has no gauge and no percentage.
8. A task is `active` only while its runtime operation is current.
9. A task becomes `done` only after its runtime operation has returned success.
10. A later task remains `pending` until every earlier task is `done`.
11. A failure marks the current task `failed` and enters the `Stopped` scene.

Target zero writer order is exact:

```text
validate offset and length are multiples of 512
invoke ioctl(fd, BLKZEROOUT, &range) with range = [u64 offset, u64 length]
when BLKZEROOUT returns 0, treat the range as written zero bytes
when BLKZEROOUT returns ENOTTY, EOPNOTSUPP, or EINVAL after alignment validation, write the range with repeated pwrite_all calls from one 1048576-byte zero buffer
when BLKZEROOUT returns any other errno, enter Failure with reason target-write-failed
when zero-buffer pwrite_all returns any error or short write, enter Failure with reason target-write-failed
```

Payload zero extents are cleared through the target zero writer and are not copied as zero-filled payload data when a target zeroing primitive is available. The target head and tail scrub ranges remove stale partition tables, boot records, and backup GPT tails from the physical target. The payload extent table covers the complete installed target byte span.

Any failure after `target_write_started` enters `Failure` with reason `target-write-failed` and `failure_write_state = target-write-started`, except payload structural validation or zstd decoding failures, which use reason `payload-validation-failed`.

## 7. Terminal screen architecture

The product terminal screen layer is a renderer for fixed semantic screens. It is not a menu system, not a terminal UI framework exposed to operators, not a general-purpose terminal abstraction layer, and not a shell wrapper.

Runtime terminal screens use exactly this direct product dependency set:

```text
ratatui
crossterm
```

Allowed Ratatui render primitives for product screen code:

```text
Layout
Rect
Buffer
Style
Span
Line
Text
Paragraph
Table
List
```

Product screen code does not use Ratatui `Block` with borders. Product screen code does not use Ratatui Gauge, Sparkline, Tabs, Scrollbar, Calendar, Canvas, Chart, BarChart, LineGauge, popup overlays, modal dialogs, mouse hit testing, editable text input, command palettes, command prompts, scrollback browsing, theme selectors, terminal hyperlink affordances, or widget-provided non-ASCII glyphs.

All product-visible text is ASCII-only. A product-visible byte is valid only when it is one of:

```text
0x20 through 0x7E
LF in validation artifact line endings only
```

`AsciiCellString` sanitization is exact:

1. Bytes in `0x20..=0x7E` are retained.
2. Tab, CR, LF, ESC, C0 controls, and DEL are replaced by one ASCII space.
3. Every non-ASCII UTF-8 scalar or invalid byte sequence is replaced by `?`.
4. Consecutive spaces introduced by sanitization are collapsed to one space.
5. Leading and trailing spaces are trimmed unless the caller is rendering an intentionally padded fixed-width field.
6. The sanitizer does not preserve ANSI control sequences.
7. The sanitizer does not preserve Unicode width, Unicode normalization, Unicode direction, or Unicode grapheme semantics.

Byte length is the product cell width. Layout, table column sizing, gauge placement, truncation, alignment, padding, centering, and artifact validation use byte counts only.

`truncate_ascii(s, max_width)` is exact:

1. If `max_width == 0`, return the empty string.
2. If `len(s) <= max_width`, return `s` unchanged.
3. If `max_width <= 3`, return `.` repeated `max_width` times.
4. Otherwise return the first `max_width - 3` bytes of `s` followed by `...`.

Product layout regions that render host-controlled strings are at least `4` cells wide. A region narrower than `4` for a truncatable host-controlled field is an internal renderer error.

Textual tokens:

```text
Selected row marker          >
Unselected row marker        <single ASCII space>
Focused action               [ <label> ]
Unfocused action             <label>
Disabled action              <label>
Action separator             four ASCII spaces
Active gauge fill            #
Inactive gauge fill          -
Unavailable field            unavailable
Configuring field            configuring
Measuring field              measuring
Title separator              " - "
Field separator              " - "
Temperature suffix           " C"
```

The following visible patterns are absent from every product frame:

```text
+- border corners
horizontal ruler rows
vertical ruler columns
boxed panels
Unicode box drawing
Unicode bullets
Unicode arrows
Unicode ellipsis
emoji
```

### 7.1 Product font and display density

Yaoshi uses Terminus as the only product console font family. Product display density uses exactly two cell sizes:

```text
10x20
12x24
```

The repository vendors exactly these uncompressed PSF font files and license files:

```text
crates/yaoshi-screen/fonts/terminus/TerminusRegular10x20.psf
crates/yaoshi-screen/fonts/terminus/TerminusBold10x20.psf
crates/yaoshi-screen/fonts/terminus/TerminusRegular12x24.psf
crates/yaoshi-screen/fonts/terminus/TerminusBold12x24.psf
crates/yaoshi-screen/fonts/terminus/OFL-1.1.txt
```

Compiled-in display profiles are exact:

| Profile name    | Cell size | Regular face source path                                       | Bold face source path                                       |
| --------------- | --------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Terminus10x20` | `10x20`   | `crates/yaoshi-screen/fonts/terminus/TerminusRegular10x20.psf` | `crates/yaoshi-screen/fonts/terminus/TerminusBold10x20.psf` |
| `Terminus12x24` | `12x24`   | `crates/yaoshi-screen/fonts/terminus/TerminusRegular12x24.psf` | `crates/yaoshi-screen/fonts/terminus/TerminusBold12x24.psf` |

Profile rules:

1. A display profile is one cell size plus one regular face and one matching bold face.
2. The regular face is the Linux virtual-console font applied by font ioctl.
3. The matching bold face is a required product asset for the selected cell size and must parse, carry the same cell dimensions, and pass glyph-count validation.
4. Product body text uses the selected regular face with no bold modifier.
5. Product emphasis uses the selected regular face plus terminal bold modifier and foreground hierarchy defined in Section 7.2.
6. No product path globally applies a bold PSF face to render a whole screen in bold.
7. No `14x28`, `16x32`, downloaded, generated, converted, or host-provided console font is accepted.

Installer display envelope:

```text
installer_minimum_supported_grid = 96x28
installer_maximum_content_frame  = 160x44
```

Dashboard display envelope:

```text
dashboard_minimum_supported_grid = 80x24
dashboard_maximum_content_frame  = 160x44
```

Display-density selection is deterministic and surface-local:

```text
if framebuffer_height_pixels >= 1440:
  candidate order = Terminus12x24, then Terminus10x20
else:
  candidate order = Terminus10x20, then Terminus12x24
```

The runtime evaluates candidates in the candidate order above and selects the first profile whose applied-font effectiveness check passes and whose observed grid satisfies the active surface minimum. If no profile is eligible, the display is unsupported.

There is no canonical-grid closeness scoring, no larger-cell tie break, no attempt to maximize physical text size, and no high-density fallback profile beyond `10x20`. A `3840x2160` framebuffer therefore selects `Terminus12x24`; a framebuffer below `1440` pixels in height selects `Terminus10x20` when the surface floor is satisfied.

Root-region sizing is exact:

```text
root_columns = min(observed_terminal_columns, surface_maximum_content_frame.columns)
root_rows    = min(observed_terminal_rows, surface_maximum_content_frame.rows)
```

The root region is centered inside the observed terminal grid when either observed dimension exceeds the active surface maximum content frame. Extra physical pixels or extra terminal rows never force a larger font and never force long product rows to remain single-line.

Applied-font effectiveness check:

```text
observed_cell_width_floor  = floor(framebuffer_width_pixels / terminal_columns)
observed_cell_height_floor = floor(framebuffer_height_pixels / terminal_rows)
font effective when observed_cell_width_floor >= profile_cell_width
                and observed_cell_height_floor >= profile_cell_height
```

When the framebuffer size cannot be read, the display is unsupported. When a font ioctl succeeds but the effectiveness check fails, the display is unsupported with reason `font-application-not-effective`.

Installer blocking-stop fallback:

```text
blocking_stop_poweroff_timeout = 15 s
```

Blocking-stop states are installer startup fatal errors, unsupported installer display, and Stopped failure scenes. These states never wait forever. Startup fatal errors before tty1 is available write the fallback notice to `/dev/console`. When keyboard input is available, `Enter` activates immediate controlled poweroff. Independently of input availability, the installer powers off automatically after `blocking_stop_poweroff_timeout`. The Stopped scene renders the remaining automatic poweroff time. The unsupported-display page renders both the immediate Enter action and the automatic poweroff timeout in plain ASCII text. The automatic fallback emits the same `controlled_exit action=poweroff` marker as manual poweroff when ttyS0 is available.

After successful display negotiation, the runtime emits this serial marker when `/dev/ttyS0` is open:

```text
YAOSHI_MARK v=1 kind=display surface=<installer|dashboard> profile=<Terminus10x20|Terminus12x24> fb=<width>x<height> cell=<observed-cell-width>x<observed-cell-height> grid=<columns>x<rows> root=<columns>x<rows>
```

Installer ttyS0 output is best-effort diagnostics. The installer opens ttyS0 with nonblocking/no-controlling-tty flags. Marker writes are bounded by `serial_mark_timeout = 1 s`; marker write failure disables later serial diagnostics for the current boot. Screen-frame writes are bounded per line by `serial_frame_line_timeout = 250 ms`; screen-frame write failure abandons only the current screen-frame block and keeps later marker writes eligible. Serial output must never block module loading, media discovery, target selection, or controlled exit.

For QEMU screen diagnosis, each production-rendered installer or dashboard frame also emits one complete serial screen frame block when `/dev/ttyS0` is open:

```text
YAOSHI_SCREEN_FRAME_BEGIN v=1 screen=<screen-id> columns=<columns> rows=<rows>
YAOSHI_SCREEN_FRAME_ROW v=1 screen=<screen-id> row=<row-index> text=<ascii-cell-row>
YAOSHI_SCREEN_FRAME_STYLE v=1 screen=<screen-id> row=<style-row-index> text=<style-span-row>
YAOSHI_SCREEN_FRAME_END v=1 screen=<screen-id>
```

`<ascii-cell-row>` is the exact ASCII cell row produced by the shared production renderer for the frame written to tty1. The screen-frame `columns` and `rows` fields are the full terminal grid dimensions used to render the frame. The display marker `root=<columns>x<rows>` field is the capped product root region inside that full grid. These values may differ on displays larger than the surface maximum content frame, and that difference is not a product UI mismatch. `screens/*.txt` QEMU screen captures extract complete serial screen frame blocks for named product states and store their cells and style spans with capture metadata. The screen frame block is a ttyS0 validation and diagnosis artifact and does not add product-visible text to tty1.

### 7.2 Visual styles

Color reinforces state and hierarchy and never carries state by itself. A valid installer or dashboard frame remains understandable in monochrome terminal capture.

Every semantic style uses the console default background. Background color is not a product state carrier.

Semantic styles:

| Style              | Foreground     | Background | Modifier |
| ------------------ | -------------- | ---------- | -------- |
| `Canvas`           | `default`      | `default`  | none     |
| `Plain`            | `white`        | `default`  | none     |
| `Header`           | `bright-white` | `default`  | bold     |
| `Section`          | `bright-cyan`  | `default`  | bold     |
| `Muted`            | `gray`         | `default`  | none     |
| `Info`             | `white`        | `default`  | none     |
| `Focus`            | `bright-cyan`  | `default`  | bold     |
| `Action`           | `white`        | `default`  | none     |
| `ActionFocus`      | `bright-cyan`  | `default`  | bold     |
| `DestructiveFocus` | `bright-red`   | `default`  | bold     |
| `Warning`          | `yellow`       | `default`  | bold     |
| `Success`          | `green`        | `default`  | bold     |
| `Disabled`         | `gray`         | `default`  | dim      |
| `Critical`         | `bright-red`   | `default`  | bold     |
| `GaugeFill`        | `cyan`         | `default`  | none     |
| `GaugeSuccess`     | `green`        | `default`  | none     |
| `GaugeWarning`     | `yellow`       | `default`  | none     |
| `GaugeCritical`    | `bright-red`   | `default`  | none     |
| `GaugeRest`        | `gray`         | `default`  | none     |

Typography hierarchy rules:

1. Descriptive field values, explanatory sentences, device model strings, serial strings, stable IDs, numeric details, and inactive rows use `Plain`, `Info`, or `Muted` and therefore render without bold modifier.
2. Page titles use `Header`.
3. Section labels use `Section`.
4. Selected rows and focused actions use bold through `Focus`, `ActionFocus`, or `DestructiveFocus`.
5. Warning, success, and critical state words use bold because they are state summaries, not descriptive detail.
6. No scene renders all text in bold.
7. No product page uses bold to compensate for an oversized font.

Action color invariant:

1. Action foreground color is focus-only.
2. Every unfocused enabled action uses `Action`.
3. A focused enabled normal action uses `ActionFocus`.
4. A focused enabled destructive action uses `DestructiveFocus`.
