5. A disabled action uses `Disabled`.
6. An unfocused destructive action is visually neutral and uses `Action`.
7. No unfocused enabled action uses warning, critical, success, focus, action-focus, or destructive-focus styling.
8. Disk-row selection is not action focus and never changes action styling.
9. Product frames never use background color, inverse video, text-cursor markers, or focus-state words to carry action focus.

The following byte strings never appear in any rendered installer or dashboard frame:

```text
write gate
write-gate
source scan
source-scan
writable fd
bounded reader
syncfs
global sync
writable handle
installer media hidden
stage
open target
clear target start
clear target end
flush target
sync filesystem
close target
hidden candidates
candidate status
[FOCUS]
[DANGER]
[DISABLED]
ActionDock
KeyHints
CommandLine
Events
event-state
History
history strip
Display   <font-profile>
nominal
normal
healthy
```

### 7.3 Numeric formatting and gauges

Numeric formatting rules:

| Data class                 | Rendering rule                                                                      |
| -------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Installer progress percent | Three decimal places, for example `34.038%`.                                        |
| Usage percent              | Three decimal places, for example `1.567%`.                                         |
| Load average               | Three decimal places, for example `0.182`.                                          |
| Pressure average           | Three decimal places, for example `2.500%`.                                         |
| Temperature                | Degrees Celsius with three decimal places and ASCII suffix, for example `61.250 C`. |
| Byte capacity              | Binary units with three decimal places at `>= 1024 B`, for example `931.513 GiB`.   |
| Byte count below `1024 B`  | Decimal bytes with no fractional part, for example `512 B`.                         |
| Byte rate                  | Binary units per second with three decimal places, for example `88.017 MiB/s`.      |
| Packet, error, drop rate   | Decimal units per second with three decimal places, for example `11.000 packets/s`. |
| Duration below `60 s`      | Seconds with three decimal places, for example `28.713 s`.                          |
| Duration `>= 60 s`         | `<minutes>m <seconds>s`, where seconds has three decimal places.                    |
| Counts                     | Decimal integers, for example `8 CPUs`, `2 disks`, `4 interfaces`.                  |
| Unknown value              | `unavailable`.                                                                      |
| First delta-derived sample | `measuring`.                                                                        |
| Converging network value   | `configuring`.                                                                      |

ASCII gauge formula:

```text
gauge_bar_width = clamp(region_width - visible_label_width - 4, 16, 48)
```

A gauge renders exactly:

```text
[<filled><rest>]
```

`<filled>` is `#` repeated `floor(gauge_bar_width * clamped_percent / 100)`. `<rest>` is `-` repeated for the remaining gauge bar cells. The two bracket cells are not counted in `gauge_bar_width`.

Gauge atomicity rules:

1. A rendered gauge is an indivisible ASCII field.
2. A rendered gauge always contains both bracket cells and exactly `gauge_bar_width` interior cells.
3. No product row may contain a dangling `[` or `]`, a partial gauge interior, or a truncated gauge suffix.
4. When a dashboard row budget cannot fit a complete gauge, the row-specific dashboard renderer omits that gauge and keeps the associated numeric percentage.
5. Installer write-scene task gauges are mandatory; if an installer write-scene row cannot fit a mandatory complete gauge at the negotiated display floor, the renderer returns an internal layout error before the frame is emitted.

## 8. Installer transaction screens

The installer screen is a borderless ASCII transaction surface. It presents only the current phase state, selected target data, sparse payload status, available actions, overwrite consequence, write progress, and result. It does not explain the product architecture and does not display implementation checkpoints.

Every supported installer scene uses this root-region grammar:

```text
TitleLine          height 1
StateLine          height 1
BlankLine          height 1
ContentLines       content-driven, maximum 22 rows
BlankLine          height 1
ActionChoiceLine   height 1
ActionMeaningLine  height 1
```

Rules:

1. The renderer does not stretch static content to fill the screen.
2. Remaining rows after `ActionMeaningLine` are canvas.
3. The installer root region is centered when the terminal grid exceeds `160x44`.
4. The installer renders zero border glyphs and zero ASCII border boxes.
5. The installer renders zero bordered cards, step rails, page rails, action docks, key-hint bands, modal overlays, side rails, menu bars, and scrollback browsers.
6. The installer never duplicates the same device, stable disk id, size, image, model, or serial value in two separate content regions of the same scene.
7. Every visible line is ASCII-only.
8. Semantic rows that can contain long host-controlled values use the field wrapping rules in this section before row truncation is allowed.
9. Row truncation is allowed only after the scene-specific row budget has been exhausted.
10. The word `erase` is used only for the explicit rescue action that clears target metadata without installing Yaoshi.

Installer scene titles:

```text
Yaoshi Installer - Prepare
Yaoshi Installer - Target
Yaoshi Installer - Confirm Install
Yaoshi Installer - Write
Yaoshi Installer - Done
Yaoshi Installer - Erase Result
Yaoshi Installer - Stopped
Yaoshi Installer - Poweroff
Yaoshi Installer - Reboot
```

### 8.1 Shared installer row models

#### 8.1.1 FieldRows

A `FieldRow` renders one descriptive field.

```text
<label><spaces><value>
```

Rules:

1. `<label>` is ASCII, lowercase, and at most `10` cells.
2. The label column is exactly `10` cells.
3. The value starts at column `11`.
4. A value that exceeds the remaining row width wraps at ASCII spaces into continuation rows.
5. Continuation rows render `10` spaces followed by the continued value.
6. When one token is longer than the remaining row width, that token is truncated with `truncate_ascii`.
7. Scene definitions state the maximum continuation row count for fields that may wrap.
8. Descriptive `FieldRow` labels and values use `Plain` unless the scene explicitly applies a state style.

#### 8.1.2 TaskRows

A `TaskRow` renders one phase-local task owned by the current scene. It is not a global audit trail and is not reused across scenes.

Task state tokens are exact:

```text
[done]
[active]
[pending]
[failed]
```

Task row format:

```text
<state-token>  <task-label>  <task-detail>
```

Rules:

1. `TaskRows` are ordered exactly as the scene defines them.
2. A scene owns its own task list and progress values.
3. `done` rows use `Success` only on the state token and `Plain` on the label and detail.
4. `active` rows use `Focus` on the state token and task label.
5. `pending` rows use `Muted`.
6. `failed` rows use `Critical` on the state token and task label.
7. Task details are descriptive text and use non-bold style unless they contain a warning or critical state word.
8. Task rows wrap only when the scene explicitly allows wrapping; otherwise they are truncated after the task label is preserved.
9. Implementation names, module paths, file descriptors, serial marker names, ioctl names, cache keys, and resolver-stage names are never task labels.

### 8.2 ActionRows model

Installer operator execution uses `ActionRows`. `ActionRows` consists exactly of:

```text
ActionChoiceLine
ActionMeaningLine
```

Action model fields:

```text
label
kind = normal | destructive
enabled = true | false
meaning
effect
```

Action rendering rules:

1. `ActionChoiceLine` renders all scene actions in scene-defined order.
2. Actions are separated by exactly four ASCII spaces.
3. The focused enabled action renders as `[ <label> ]`.
4. An unfocused enabled action renders as `<label>`.
5. A disabled action renders as `<label>` with `Disabled` style and is never bracketed.
6. Every unfocused enabled action uses `Action`, including destructive actions.
7. A focused normal action uses `ActionFocus`.
8. A focused destructive action uses `DestructiveFocus`.
9. `ActionMeaningLine` renders exactly the `meaning` of the focused enabled action.
10. Scenes with no enabled action render a fixed locked-state sentence in `ActionChoiceLine` and a fixed locked-state explanation in `ActionMeaningLine`.
11. The maximum rendered action label width is `48` cells. Longer labels are truncated with `truncate_ascii` before brackets are added.
12. If the joined action line still exceeds the root width after action-label truncation, the complete line is truncated with `truncate_ascii` to the root width.
13. `ActionMeaningLine` wraps to two rows only when the root width is below `120`; otherwise it is truncated with `truncate_ascii` to the root width.

Input contract:

1. `Tab` changes the focused enabled action.
2. `Enter` activates only the focused enabled action.
3. `ArrowUp` and `ArrowDown` change selected disk only in `Target` when at least one target-action disk exists.
4. The disk list is never a focus target.
5. Selecting a disk is never an action.
6. Disabled actions are skipped by `Tab` and ignored by `Enter`.
7. Locked scenes have no focused action and no enabled action.

### 8.3 Prepare scene

`Prepare` represents internal phases `Start`, post-frame device support loading, `DiscoverSource`, and `InspectDisks`. The product-visible screen describes functional readiness. It does not show first-frame markers, module file paths, font profile, terminal grid, serial state, or block-device implementation names.

`TitleLine`:

```text
Yaoshi Installer - Prepare
```

`StateLine`:

```text
safe - no disk will be changed
```

Prepare owns exactly these task rows:

```text
console ready
input and storage drivers
installer media
payload validation
target disk scan
```

The first rendered Prepare content is exact except for gauge width:

```text
status    preparing installer
current   starting input and storage drivers
[done]    console ready
[active]  input and storage drivers 0 / <module_count>
[pending] installer media 0.000 s / 10.000 s <media-gauge> 0.000%
[pending] payload validation
[pending] target disk scan
```

Prepare task detail rules:

| Task label                  | Active detail format                                       | Done detail format                       | Failed detail format       |
| --------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | -------------------------- |
| `console ready`             | never active after first frame                             | empty                                    | `display unsupported`      |
| `input and storage drivers` | `<loaded_module_count> / <module_count>`                   | `<module_count> / <module_count>`        | `device support failed`    |
| `installer media`           | `<elapsed> / 10.000 s <media-gauge> <media_check_percent>` | `found in <elapsed>`                     | `not found` or `ambiguous` |
| `payload validation`        | `checking image metadata`                                  | `metadata ok`                            | `payload invalid`          |
| `target disk scan`          | `inspecting local disks`                                   | `<selectable_count> usable target disks` | `disk inspection failed`   |

When `payload validation` is `done`, Prepare renders these payload field rows immediately after the task rows and before action rows:

```text
payload
  image       <payload_target_minimum_bytes>
  write       <payload_planned_extent_bytes>
  packed      <payload_container_bytes>
  zero        <payload_zero_extent_bytes>
```

Payload field rows use `Plain` style. They are rendered as separate rows and are never collapsed into one long task-detail string.

`current` line values are exact:

```text
starting input and storage drivers
loading input and storage support
looking for installer media
checking installer image
inspecting target disks
ready for target selection
```

The media gauge visible label is `media`. The media gauge uses `media_check_percent`. The media gauge remains at `0.000%` while the installer media task is `pending`.

Actions:

| Order | Label       | Kind   | Enabled | Meaning                                    | Effect     |
| ----: | ----------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------ | ---------- |
|     1 | `Power off` | normal | true    | `Power off without opening a target disk.` | `poweroff` |

Focused action on entry:

```text
Power off
```

Rendered `ActionRows` on entry:

```text
[ Power off ]
Power off without opening a target disk.
```

### 8.4 Target scene

`Target` renders target-action disks, selected target details, and a compact summary of ineligible disks. It does not render a separate selected-target card. It does not make the disk table executable.

`TitleLine`:

```text
Yaoshi Installer - Target
```

`StateLine` when at least one target-action disk exists:

```text
safe - choose target disk - installer media excluded
```

`StateLine` when zero target-action disks exist:

```text
safe - no writes started - no usable target disk
```

When at least one target-action disk exists, `ContentLines` are:

```text
image
  target size  <payload_target_minimum_bytes>
  write        <payload_planned_extent_bytes>
  packed       <payload_container_bytes>
target disks
<target-list-row>...
selected
  disk         <dev_path>
  size         <size> - sector <sector> - existing <existing>
  identity     <stable_id>
  model        <model_short>
  serial       <serial_short>
  usage        <payload_footprint_percent> before first boot growth
other disks
  <other-disk-summary-row>
```

Target list row format:

```text
<marker> <dev_path>  <size>  <existing>  <action_state>  id <stable_id_short>
```

Column values:

| Field                            | Value                                                                                                                                                        |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `<marker>`                       | `>` for the selected row and one ASCII space for every unselected row.                                                                                       |
| `<dev_path>`                     | `KernelDiskRef.dev_path` after ASCII sanitization.                                                                                                           |
| `<size>`                         | `KernelDiskRef.byte_size` as byte capacity.                                                                                                                  |
| `<sector>`                       | `KernelDiskRef.logical_block_size` as byte count.                                                                                                            |
| `<existing>`                     | `GPT`, `MBR`, `none`, or `unrecognized`, derived from read-only partition-table inspection.                                                                  |
| `<action_state>`                 | `install` for installable targets, `erase-only` for targets that can be selected only for metadata erase, or `blocked` for rows that are not action targets. |
| `<model_short>`                  | Disk model after ASCII sanitization, or `unavailable`; wraps to at most `2` field rows.                                                                      |
| `<serial_short>`                 | Disk serial after ASCII sanitization, or `unavailable`; wraps to at most `2` field rows.                                                                     |
| `<stable_id_short>`              | Basename of `stable_disk_id`, truncated to `32` cells with `truncate_ascii`.                                                                                 |
| `<stable_id>`                    | Full `stable_disk_id` after ASCII sanitization; wraps to at most `2` field rows.                                                                             |
| `<payload_target_minimum_bytes>` | Payload target image size.                                                                                                                                   |
| `<payload_planned_extent_bytes>` | Planned uncompressed extent bytes that will be written or zeroed.                                                                                            |
| `<payload_container_bytes>`      | On-media payload container byte size.                                                                                                                        |
| `<payload_footprint_percent>`    | Three-decimal percentage of target disk occupied by `payload_target_minimum_bytes` relative to the candidate target disk size before first boot root growth. |

Target row rules:

1. A visible disk is a target-action candidate: `selectable`, `installed-target`, or `blocked-by-installed-target`.
2. The source installer disk is never visible.
3. Ineligible disks other than rescue erase targets are never visible as target rows.
4. Payload size information is rendered only in the `image` section.
5. Row order is ascending lexical order by `kernel_name`.
6. On entry with at least one visible disk, the first visible row is selected and `Confirm target` is focused.
7. `ArrowUp` and `ArrowDown` move selection within visible rows and do not wrap.
8. `Enter` never activates a disk row.
9. The selected target list row applies `Focus`.
10. Selected detail fields appear once after the target list and wrap before truncation.
11. When visible target rows exceed available content rows, the renderer shows as many target rows as fit minus one row and renders `... <n> more target disks not shown` as the final target row.

Other-disk summary row:

```text
<installer_media_count> installer media - <installed_target_count> installed target - <blocked_by_installed_target_count> blocked by installed target - <too_small_count> too small - <unsupported_sector_size_count> unsupported sector - <no_stable_id_count> no stable id - <read_error_count> unreadable
```

When zero target-action disks exist, `ContentLines` are exactly:

```text
no usable target disk found
required
  separate from installer media
  stable sysfs disk identity
  512 B sectors
  at least <payload_target_minimum_bytes>
  readable whole disk
other
  <other-disk-summary-row>
```

Actions when at least one target-action disk exists:

| Order | Label            | Kind   | Enabled | Meaning                                                                    | Effect          |
| ----: | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------- |
|     1 | `Confirm target` | normal | true    | `Confirm the selected disk before install or erase; Up/Down changes selected disk.` | `confirm-disk`  |
|     2 | `Refresh`        | normal | true    | `Rescan disks and rebuild the target list.`                                | `inspect-disks` |
|     3 | `Power off`      | normal | true    | `Power off without starting target writes.`                                | `poweroff`      |

Focused action on entry with target-action disks:

```text
Confirm target
```

Actions when zero target-action disks exist:

| Order | Label            | Kind   | Enabled | Meaning                                     | Effect          |
| ----: | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------- | --------------- |
|     1 | `Confirm target` | normal | false   |                                             | none            |
|     2 | `Refresh`        | normal | true    | `Rescan disks and rebuild the target list.` | `inspect-disks` |
|     3 | `Power off`      | normal | true    | `Power off without starting target writes.` | `poweroff`      |

Focused action on entry with zero target-action disks:

```text
Refresh
```

### 8.5 Confirm Install scene

`Confirm Install` represents internal phase `ConfirmDisk` and renders one irreversible sparse overwrite decision. The product-visible title uses `Confirm Install` because target writes have not started while this scene is active.

`TitleLine`:

```text
Yaoshi Installer - Confirm Install
```

`StateLine`:

```text
danger - no writes started - back is still safe
```

`ContentLines`:

```text
target
  disk         <dev_path>
  size         <size>
  sector       <logical_block_size>
  existing     <existing>
identity
  id           <stable_disk_id>
  model        <model>
  serial       <serial>
install plan
  write        <payload_planned_extent_bytes> Yaoshi image
  zero         <payload_zero_extent_bytes> declared empty bytes
  grow         <remaining-target-bytes> available for first boot root growth
  result       replaces boot records and filesystem metadata on the target disk
erase plan
  clear        target head and tail metadata only
  result       removes partition tables and boot records without installing Yaoshi
```

Rules:

1. `id`, `model`, and `serial` fields wrap to at most `2` field rows each before truncation.
2. Missing model or serial renders as `unavailable`.
3. A target with unavailable stable disk id is not a target-action candidate and therefore never reaches this scene.
4. The `install plan` and `erase plan` `result` lines are the only consequence sentences in the scene.
5. No field in this scene renders module names, payload hashes, source-byte counters, file descriptors, or serial marker names.

Actions:

| Order | Label                          | Kind        | Enabled | Meaning                                                            | Effect         |
| ----: | ------------------------------ | ----------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------- |
|     1 | `Back`                         | normal      | true    | `Return to target selection without opening the disk for writing.` | `select-disk`  |
|     2 | `Erase disk only <dev_path>`   | destructive | true    | `Clear boot records and partition metadata on <dev_path>.`         | `erase-target` |
|     3 | `Install Yaoshi to <dev_path>` | destructive | true only for `selectable` targets | `Write the Yaoshi image to <dev_path>.`         | `write-target` |
|     4 | `Power off`                    | normal      | true    | `Power off without starting target writes.`                        | `poweroff`     |

Rules:

1. `Back` is focused every time the scene is entered.
2. The destructive action is never focused on scene entry.
3. The install action label uses exactly `Install Yaoshi to <dev_path>` before action-label truncation.
4. The erase action label uses exactly `Erase disk only <dev_path>` before action-label truncation.
5. Destructive action labels use `Action` when unfocused and `DestructiveFocus` when focused.
6. Activating the install action starts the Write Start Rule.
7. Activating the erase action re-reads the selected target identity, opens only that target for writing, clears the target head scrub range and target tail scrub range, flushes writes, and renders Erase Result. It does not validate or write the payload.
8. Activating `Power off` enters `ControlledExit` with action `poweroff` without opening a target for writing.

### 8.6 Write scene

`Write` renders the locked installation or metadata-erase transaction and its phase-local task progress. Keyboard input is locked while this scene is active. The scene does not render a numeric stage row, an implementation timeline, serial marker names, file descriptor terms, syscall terms, or source-byte counters.

`TitleLine`:

```text
Yaoshi Installer - Write
```

`StateLine`:

```text
locked - keep power connected - target <dev_path>
```

`ContentLines`:

```text
status    <write_status>
target    <dev_path>
<image_or_clear_label> <target_image_bytes>
tasks
[<state>] verify target
[<state>] prepare disk beginning <head_scrub_written_bytes> / <target_head_scrub_bytes> <head-gauge> <head_scrub_percent>
[<state>] prepare disk end <tail_scrub_written_bytes> / <target_tail_scrub_bytes> <tail-gauge> <tail_scrub_percent>
[<state>] copy Yaoshi image <progress_percent> <copy-gauge>
[<state>] finalize writes
progress
written   <planned_written_bytes> / <payload_planned_extent_bytes>
zeroed    <zero_written_bytes> / <payload_zero_extent_bytes>
rate      current <current_rate_bps>
average   <average_rate_bps>
left      <remaining_planned_bytes>
time      <estimated_remaining_seconds>
```

`<write_status>` is `installing Yaoshi` for install and `erasing target metadata` for erase. `<image_or_clear_label>` is `image` for install and `clear` for erase.

Write task rows are exact and always render in this order:

```text
verify target
prepare disk beginning
prepare disk end
copy Yaoshi image
finalize writes
```

Task meanings are exact:

| Task label               | Runtime operation covered                                                                                        | Gauge |
| ------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----- |
| `verify target`          | Re-read selected target identity, revalidate source payload structure, manifest hash, extent-table hash, and open the target write path. | no    |
| `prepare disk beginning` | Write zeros to the target head scrub range.                                                                      | yes   |
| `prepare disk end`       | Write zeros to the target tail scrub range.                                                                      | yes   |
| `copy Yaoshi image`      | Copy raw and zstd data extents and clear zero extents through the high-throughput offset writer.                 | yes   |
| `finalize writes`        | Sync the selected target file, sync the target filesystem, invoke global sync, close target write descriptors, and transition to completion. | no    |

Task state mapping from the current write task is exact:

| Current write task       | Completed tasks                                               | Active task              |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------ |
| `verify target`          | none                                                          | `verify target`          |
| `prepare disk beginning` | `verify target`                                               | `prepare disk beginning` |
| `prepare disk end`       | `verify target`, `prepare disk beginning`                     | `prepare disk end`       |
| `copy Yaoshi image`      | `verify target`, `prepare disk beginning`, `prepare disk end` | `copy Yaoshi image`      |
| `finalize writes`        | through `copy Yaoshi image`                                   | `finalize writes`        |

The `prepare disk beginning` gauge visible label is `beginning`. It uses `head_scrub_percent`.

The `prepare disk end` gauge visible label is `end`. It uses `tail_scrub_percent`.

The `copy Yaoshi image` gauge visible label is `image`. It uses `progress_percent`.

`current_rate_bps`, `average_rate_bps`, and `estimated_remaining_seconds` render as `measuring` before the first valid sample. `rate`, `average`, `left`, and `time` are separate rows and are never collapsed into one long row.

Locked `ActionRows`:

```text
Input locked
Writing target disk; wait for completion.
```

### 8.7 Done scene

`Done` renders one install completion result.

`TitleLine`:

```text
Yaoshi Installer - Done
```

`StateLine`:

```text
complete - writes flushed - target <dev_path>
```

`ContentLines`:

```text
installed
  target       <dev_path>
  identity     <stable_id_short>
  written      <payload_planned_extent_bytes>
  zeroed       <payload_zero_extent_bytes>
  boot         UEFI fallback
next
  remove installer media
  boot with one Yaoshi target attached
  first boot grows root filesystem when possible
access
  SSH root key
  tty2 root shell
```

`<stable_id_short>` is the selected target stable disk id basename, truncated to `32` cells with `truncate_ascii`.

The Done scene renders no completion gauge.

Actions:

| Order | Label       | Kind   | Enabled | Meaning                                                            | Effect     |
| ----: | ----------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------- |
|     1 | `Reboot`    | normal | true    | `Reboot into the installed target after removing installer media.` | `reboot`   |
|     2 | `Power off` | normal | true    | `Power off after completed and flushed writes.`                    | `poweroff` |

`Reboot` is focused every time the scene is entered.

When entering the `Done` scene, the installer emits:

```text
YAOSHI_MARK v=1 kind=installer event=complete
```

when `/dev/ttyS0` is open.

### 8.8 Erase Result scene

`Erase Result` renders one metadata-erase completion result. It is nonblocking and allows the operator to return directly to target selection without rebooting or powering off.

`TitleLine`:

```text
Yaoshi Installer - Erase Result
```

`StateLine`:

```text
erase complete - metadata cleared - target <dev_path>
```

`ContentLines`:

```text
erased
  target       <dev_path>
  identity     <stable_id_short>
  cleared      <erased_bytes>
  zeroed       <erased_bytes>
next
  choose another target or install now
  reboot or power off when finished
```

`<stable_id_short>` is the selected target stable disk id basename, truncated to `32` cells with `truncate_ascii`.

The Erase Result scene renders no completion gauge.

Actions:

| Order | Label           | Kind   | Enabled | Meaning                                                       | Effect          |
| ----: | --------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------- |
|     1 | `Choose target` | normal | true    | `Return to target selection without rebooting or powering off.` | `inspect-disks` |
|     2 | `Reboot`        | normal | true    | `Reboot after completed and flushed erase.`                   | `reboot`        |
|     3 | `Power off`     | normal | true    | `Power off after completed and flushed writes.`               | `poweroff`      |

`Choose target` is focused every time the scene is entered.

When entering the `Erase Result` scene, the installer emits:

```text
YAOSHI_MARK v=1 kind=installer event=complete
```

when `/dev/ttyS0` is open.

### 8.9 Stopped scene

`Stopped` renders one failure reason, the failed task, target state, and next action. It is not an audit report and does not render logs.

`TitleLine`:

```text
Yaoshi Installer - Stopped
```

`StateLine` when `failure_write_state = no-target-writes`:

```text
failed - no target writes started
```

`StateLine` when `failure_write_state = target-write-started`:

```text
failed - target image incomplete
```

`ContentLines`:

```text
reason    <rendered sentence>
task      <failed-task-name>
target    <affected-disk-rendered-value>
state     <failure_write_state_rendered>
next      <rendered recovery sentence>
poweroff  automatic in <seconds> s; Enter powers off now
```

Failure reason values and rendered sentences:

| Reason value                   | Rendered sentence                                   | Failed task name            |
| ------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- |
| `installation-media-not-found` | `No Yaoshi installer media was found.`              | `installer media`           |
| `installation-media-ambiguous` | `More than one Yaoshi installer media was found.`   | `installer media`           |
| `payload-validation-failed`    | `The Yaoshi payload did not validate.`              | `payload validation`        |
| `disk-inspection-failed`       | `Disk inspection failed.`                           | `target disk scan`          |
| `selected-disk-changed`        | `The selected disk changed before writing started.` | `verify target`             |
| `target-open-failed`           | `The selected disk cannot be opened for writing.`   | `verify target`             |
| `target-write-failed`          | `Installation copy failed.`                         | current write task          |
| `module-load-failed`           | `Installer device support failed to load.`          | `input and storage drivers` |
| `internal-error`               | `The installer hit an internal error.`              | `internal`                  |

`current write task` is the product task label that was active when the failure occurred.

`<affected-disk-rendered-value>` is `<dev_path>` when a target disk had been selected and `none` when no target disk had been selected.

`<failure_write_state_rendered>` values:

| Internal value         | Rendered value             |
| ---------------------- | -------------------------- |
| `no-target-writes`     | `no target writes started` |
| `target-write-started` | `target write started`     |

Recovery sentence when `failure_write_state = no-target-writes`:

```text
power off, check installer media and target disk, then boot again
```

Recovery sentence when `failure_write_state = target-write-started`:

```text
power off; reinstall to the same disk after checking hardware
```

Actions:

| Order | Label       | Kind   | Enabled | Meaning                        | Effect     |
| ----: | ----------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------ | ---------- |
|     1 | `Power off` | normal | true    | `<rendered recovery sentence>` | `poweroff` |

Rendered `ActionRows`:

```text
[ Power off ]
<rendered recovery sentence>
```

The `poweroff` row is present in Stopped failure scenes and updates once per second until automatic controlled poweroff.

### 8.9 Poweroff and Reboot scenes

Poweroff `TitleLine`:

```text
Yaoshi Installer - Poweroff
```

Poweroff `StateLine`:

```text
exiting - poweroff requested - input locked
```

Poweroff `ContentLines`:

```text
syncing disks and requesting poweroff
```

Reboot `TitleLine`:

```text
Yaoshi Installer - Reboot
```

Reboot `StateLine`:

```text
exiting - reboot requested - input locked
```

Reboot `ContentLines`:

```text
syncing disks and requesting reboot
```

Both scenes render these locked `ActionRows`:

```text
Input locked
Exiting; wait for firmware or power state change.
```

### 8.10 Installer input behavior

Allowed installer inputs:

```text
ArrowUp
ArrowDown
Tab
Enter
Resize event
```

Every unlisted input has no product effect. This includes printable text, Backspace, Escape, mouse input, function-key input, and control-key chords.

Focus and selection rendering rules:

1. The selected disk row marker is `>`.
2. The selected disk row applies `Focus` to the complete visible row.
3. Unselected disk rows apply `Plain` to the complete visible row.
4. A focused non-destructive action renders as `[ <label> ]` and uses `ActionFocus` across the complete visible action.
5. An unfocused non-destructive action renders as `<label>` and uses `Action`.
6. A disabled action renders as `<label>` and uses `Disabled`.
7. A focused destructive action renders as `[ <label> ]` and uses `DestructiveFocus` across the complete visible action.
8. An unfocused destructive action renders as `<label>` and uses `Action`.
9. Only the focused enabled action uses `ActionFocus` or `DestructiveFocus`.
10. A locked-state action line is not a focus target.
11. Product scenes never use arrow cursors, text-cursor-like markers, focus-state words, or background color for focus, selection, action, or destructive-action state.

Scene-specific behavior:

| Scene           | Input                                     | Result                                                                                          |
| --------------- | ----------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Prepare         | `Enter` on `Power off`                    | Enter `ControlledExit` with action `poweroff`.                                                  |
| Target          | `ArrowUp`                                 | Move selected disk to previous visible row.                                                     |
| Target          | `ArrowDown`                               | Move selected disk to next visible row.                                                         |
| Target          | `Tab`                                     | Move focus among enabled actions in action order.                                               |
| Target          | `Enter` on `Confirm target`               | Enter `ConfirmDisk` when at least one target-action disk exists.                                |
| Target          | `Enter` on `Refresh`                      | Enter `InspectDisks`.                                                                           |
| Target          | `Enter` on `Power off`                    | Enter `ControlledExit` with action `poweroff`.                                                  |
| Confirm Install | `Tab`                                     | Move focus among enabled actions in action order.                                               |
| Confirm Install | `Enter` on `Back`                         | Return to `SelectDisk`.                                                                         |
| Confirm Install | `Enter` on `Erase disk only <dev_path>`    | Enter `WriteTarget`, clear selected target metadata, flush, then enter `Complete` with Erase Result focused on `Choose target`; enter `Failure` when target revalidation or writing fails. |
| Confirm Install | `Enter` on `Install Yaoshi to <dev_path>`  | Enter `WriteTarget` after Write Start Rule passes; enter `Failure` when Write Start Rule fails. |
| Confirm Install | `Enter` on `Power off`                    | Enter `ControlledExit` with action `poweroff`.                                                  |
| Write           | Every allowed input except `Resize event` | Ignored.                                                                                        |
| Done            | `Tab`                                     | Move focus among enabled actions in action order.                                               |
| Done            | `Enter` on `Reboot`                       | Enter `ControlledExit` with action `reboot`.                                                    |
| Done            | `Enter` on `Power off`                    | Enter `ControlledExit` with action `poweroff`.                                                  |
| Erase Result    | `Tab`                                     | Move focus among enabled actions in action order.                                               |
| Erase Result    | `Enter` on `Choose target`                | Enter `InspectDisks` and render target selection again after disk inspection.                    |
| Erase Result    | `Enter` on `Reboot`                       | Enter `ControlledExit` with action `reboot`.                                                    |
| Erase Result    | `Enter` on `Power off`                    | Enter `ControlledExit` with action `poweroff`.                                                  |
| Stopped         | `Enter` on `Power off`                    | Enter `ControlledExit` with action `poweroff`.                                                  |
| Poweroff        | Every allowed input except `Resize event` | Ignored.                                                                                        |
| Reboot          | Every allowed input except `Resize event` | Ignored.                                                                                        |

`Resize event` recomputes display negotiation when the framebuffer or terminal grid changed, recomputes layout, and repaints the current scene without changing installer phase, selected disk, focused action, or write state.

Before `poweroff` or `reboot`, the installer restores terminal state, invokes global sync, and emits:

```text
YAOSHI_MARK v=1 kind=installer event=controlled_exit action=<poweroff|reboot>
```

when `/dev/ttyS0` is open.

## 9. Installed runtime and Yaoshi Dashboard

### 9.1 Boot and TTY ownership

The installed target boots through UEFI fallback path:

```text
/EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI
```

Installed boot support requires exactly one attached disk containing the Yaoshi v0.0.1 GPT identifiers and labels. When multiple Yaoshi-installed target disks are attached, boot selection, root selection, and `/boot` selection are outside the v0.0.1 support boundary.

The installed kernel boot console is mirrored to `ttyS0,115200n8` and `tty1` until Yaoshi userspace takes over tty1. This prevents local installed-boot failure from degenerating into a silent black screen with only a cursor. After Yaoshi Dashboard starts, tty1 is product-owned by `yaoshi-dashboard.service`; kernel console text and service stderr are no longer product tty1 state.

The file at `/EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI` is the Debian `systemd-bootx64.efi.signed` EFI binary staged at fallback path. It reads the loader entry `loader/entries/yaoshi.conf`, loads `/YAOSHI/BOOT/VMLINUZ`, loads `/YAOSHI/BOOT/INITRD.IMG`, and passes the exact options from Section 4.5.

Installed system TTY ownership:

| TTY    | Owner            | Behavior                                                                                |
| ------ | ---------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `tty1` | Yaoshi Dashboard | Root-owned read-only single-page ASCII status surface without login prompt or commands. |
| `tty2` | Root shell       | `/bin/bash --login` as root without getty or login prompt after prepare succeeds.       |

Runtime identity:

```text
root is the Yaoshi runtime user
physical tty1 starts the root-owned read-only Yaoshi Dashboard after prepare completion, even when prepare failed
physical tty2 starts root bash automatically only after prepare succeeds
SSH accepts root public-key login only after prepare succeeds
password SSH login is disabled
keyboard-interactive SSH login is disabled
no local login prompt exists on tty1, tty2, or ttyS0
no non-root Yaoshi user exists
no interactive account setup exists
```

### 9.2 First-boot prepare service

`yaoshi-prepare.service` owns installed target preparation. It performs root growth and ESP configuration application in one bounded oneshot service. Root growth failure is nonfatal only when the root-growth state is recorded. ESP configuration application is mandatory. The service exits `0` only after hostname and root SSH keys have been atomically applied, `/var/lib/yaoshi/config-state` contains `applied`, and `/var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state` contains `applied`.

Prepare order:

```text
systemd starts yaoshi-prepare.service during multi-user boot
/usr/lib/yaoshi/prepare-launcher performs bounded idempotent `/boot` and explicit YAOSHI_ESP vfat mount attempts
/usr/lib/yaoshi/prepare-launcher snapshots /boot/YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE into /run/yaoshi-prepare and exports YAOSHI_PREPARE_HOSTNAME plus YAOSHI_PREPARE_AUTHKEYS from the ESP config files
/usr/lib/yaoshi/prepare-launcher executes /bin/bash /run/yaoshi-prepare/PREPARE
the runtime prepare creates /var/lib/yaoshi and /root/.ssh
the runtime prepare writes running to /var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state
YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE reads mandatory YAOSHI_PREPARE_HOSTNAME
YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE stages the validated hostname in /etc/hostname.yaoshi.tmp
YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE reads mandatory YAOSHI_PREPARE_AUTHKEYS
YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE stages non-empty root authorized_keys in /root/.ssh/authorized_keys.yaoshi.tmp
if /var/lib/yaoshi/root-expanded is absent:
  YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE invokes systemd-repart with no block-device argument
  systemd-repart operates on the block device backing the mounted root filesystem partition
  systemd-repart grows existing GPT partition 2 according to /usr/lib/yaoshi/repart.d/20-yaoshi-root.conf
  systemd-repart writes the updated GPT to the physical target disk
  YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE invokes /usr/lib/systemd/systemd-growfs /
  systemd-growfs / grows the mounted root ext4 filesystem
  on success, YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE writes expanded to /var/lib/yaoshi/root-growth-state
  on success, YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE creates /var/lib/yaoshi/root-expanded
  on failure, YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE writes failed to /var/lib/yaoshi/root-growth-state and continues
YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE writes the validated hostname to /etc/hostname through an atomic rename
YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE writes non-empty root authorized_keys through an atomic rename
YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE writes applied to /var/lib/yaoshi/config-state through an atomic rename
YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE writes applied to /var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state through an atomic rename
YAOSHI/RUNTIME/PREPARE exits 0
yaoshi-dashboard.service starts after the prepare job completes, regardless of prepare success or failure
when prepare succeeds, yaoshi-first-boot.service runs /usr/lib/yaoshi/first-boot-launcher exactly once
yaoshi-first-boot.service records applied, failed, skipped, or timeout failure and exits 0
yaoshi-root-shell.service requires the successful yaoshi-prepare.service job before it starts
ssh.service requires the successful yaoshi-prepare.service job and is ordered after yaoshi-first-boot.service before it starts
```

Rules:

1. The service does not prompt for input.
2. The service does not create new partitions.
3. The service does not shrink, delete, or move partitions.
4. Partition 1 remains `YAOSHI_ESP`.
5. Partition 2 remains `YAOSHI_ROOT`.
6. The root filesystem label remains `YAOSHI_ROOT`.
7. The partition 2 GUID remains equal to `installed_root_partition_guid` after successful expansion.
8. The GPT backup header is written to the physical target disk end after successful expansion.
9. After expansion succeeds, `/var/lib/yaoshi/root-expanded` exists.
10. After expansion fails, `/var/lib/yaoshi/root-expanded` remains absent.
11. Root-growth failure records `failed` and does not prevent ESP configuration application.
12. Hostname and root SSH key application run on every boot.
13. `/boot/YAOSHI/CONFIG/HOSTNAME` must be readable by `prepare-launcher`, must contain one valid hostname line, and must not exceed 63 characters after it is exported as `YAOSHI_PREPARE_HOSTNAME`.
14. `/boot/YAOSHI/CONFIG/AUTHKEYS` must be readable by `prepare-launcher` and must produce at least one non-empty ASCII key line after it is exported as `YAOSHI_PREPARE_AUTHKEYS` and filtered by runtime prepare.
15. `/var/lib/yaoshi/config-state` contains `applied` only after `/etc/hostname` and `/root/.ssh/authorized_keys` are both atomically installed with their required ownership and modes.
16. `/var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state` contains `applied` only after configuration application completes.
17. Configuration input failure, write failure, ownership failure, permission failure, atomic rename failure, or prepare timeout exits nonzero and leaves `/var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state` containing `failed` when the runtime prepare script reached its trap.
18. When `/var/lib/yaoshi/root-expanded` exists, root-growth commands are skipped and configuration application still runs.
19. `yaoshi-prepare.service` has `TimeoutStartSec=90`; when the timeout is reached, the job fails and `yaoshi-dashboard.service` starts after the failed job completes.
20. Dashboard startup does not imply SSH readiness or tty2 readiness; SSH and tty2 require prepare success.
21. `yaoshi-first-boot.service` has `TimeoutStartSec=75`; script body execution is bounded to `60` seconds by the launcher.
22. First-boot script failure records `/var/lib/yaoshi/first-boot-state` as `failed`, writes `/var/log/yaoshi/first-boot.log`, exits `0`, and does not block dashboard, tty2, or SSH.
23. First-boot script state is not a dashboard data source and is not rendered on tty1.

### 9.3 Network and SSH

The installed system uses systemd-networkd.

Rules:

1. `systemd-networkd.service` is enabled.
2. `/etc/systemd/network/20-yaoshi-dhcp.network` matches Section 5.2.
3. The Yaoshi network file uses `[Match] Name=*` plus `Type=ether` and one generic DHCP policy for Ethernet links. Interface eligibility is not derived from legacy Ethernet name prefixes.
4. DHCPv4 uses `ClientIdentifier=mac` so reinstall-generated machine identity does not by itself change the DHCPv4 client identifier for the same NIC.
5. IPv6 router advertisements are accepted.
6. The Yaoshi Dashboard first frame does not wait for network convergence.
7. Dashboard route, access, interface kind, and interface attention are derived from the runtime `NetworkSnapshot`, not from the networkd match pattern.

SSH rules:

1. `openssh-server` is in the hard required package include list.
2. `ssh.service` is enabled through the symlink in Section 5.2.
3. `ssh.service` requires successful `yaoshi-prepare.service`.
4. `ssh.service` is ordered after `yaoshi-first-boot.service` and does not require it.
5. Root SSH login is enabled.
6. Password SSH login is disabled.
7. Keyboard-interactive SSH login is disabled.
8. Public-key SSH login is enabled.
9. `/root/.ssh/authorized_keys` contains at least one key after prepare succeeds.
10. SSH host private keys are generated by the default first-boot script before SSH ordering allows the service to start.

### 9.4 Dashboard scope and refresh

The Yaoshi Dashboard answers whether the installed target has booted, whether first-boot preparation applied, whether root growth succeeded or failed, whether SSH root-key access is ready, and whether core machine resources require attention. It is a product status surface, not a log viewer, not an inventory browser, and not an operations console.

Dashboard information density contract:

1. The dashboard has exactly one screen.
2. The dashboard has no pages, navigation, focus target, action row, command prompt, modal, event stream, scrollback, detail expansion, or scrollable inventory list.
3. The dashboard has no footer line.
4. The supported floor `80x24` fits `HeaderLine`, `StatusLine`, `ChromeGap`, all five section labels, and all required summary rows defined here; larger grids add bounded extra detail units allocated by Section 9.5.
5. CPU cores render through bounded summaries and bounded prioritized detail rows.
6. Storage devices render through bounded two-row disk detail units. A disk detail unit is either rendered as two rows or not rendered.
7. Network interfaces render through a route/access-aware network snapshot and bounded two-row interface detail units. The default-route interface, SSH access address, interface kind, and interface health are separate model fields and are never inferred from one display token.
8. Hidden CPU cores, disks, and interfaces are always counted by their required summary row even when no detail row or detail unit is rendered.
9. Warning and critical inventory rows and units are prioritized before normal inventory rows and units inside their section detail candidate list.
10. No section height grows merely because the machine has more CPU cores, disks, interfaces, mounts, routes, addresses, or sensors.
11. The dashboard renders no kernel message text, no kernel warning count, no kernel error count, no display profile row, no grid row, no service audit row, and no per-metric below-warning state word.
12. Kernel warning and error messages are reduced to one alert state.
13. Prepare failure, SSH unavailability, root-growth failure, and dashboard data-source unavailability are rendered as product states, not as logs.
14. First-boot script state and first-boot script logs are not dashboard data sources and are not rendered.
15. The dashboard renders no CPU microcode, CPU cache hierarchy, NUMA topology, DIMM serial, DIMM part number, SMART data, NVMe log page, wear-level field, full partition inventory, RAID topology, LVM topology, PCI topology, USB topology, kernel command line, service list, package list, or log excerpt.
16. Fine-grained investigation is performed through root SSH or the tty2 root shell, not through Dashboard expansion.

Allowed dashboard input:

```text
Resize event
```

Every keyboard and mouse input other than resize has no product effect. The dashboard has no focus target and no action.

Refresh rules:

1. The first frame is rendered as soon as `yaoshi-dashboard.service` starts and `/dev/tty1` is ready for terminal output.
2. The dashboard enters `/dev/tty1` and activates the corresponding foreground virtual terminal before the first frame is flushed.
3. The refresh interval is `1000` milliseconds.
4. CPU usage, per-core CPU usage, process CPU usage, disk I/O rates, disk IOPS, network byte rates, network packet rates, network error rates, and network drop rates are computed from deltas between consecutive samples.
5. First-sample delta-derived values render as `measuring`.
6. Static hardware identity fields are refreshed every `10000` milliseconds.
7. A failed field read renders `unavailable` for that field and does not stop the dashboard.
8. The dashboard never waits for network convergence.
9. The dashboard never performs DNS lookups.
10. The dashboard never executes external commands.
11. The dashboard does not write system state.
12. The dashboard stores only the previous and current samples required for delta-derived live metrics.
13. The dashboard does not persist metric samples to disk.
14. QEMU serial screen captures distinguish the first dashboard frame from a `dashboard-ready` frame. `dashboard-ready` means the settled dashboard frame captured only after root growth has completed or failed, SSH root-key login has succeeded when prepare succeeded, and at least one refresh interval has elapsed. Physical-target Dashboard redeploy and capture is performed only by `real-dashboard-check` and is not a `dashboard-ready` predicate.

Dashboard presentation rules:

1. `yaoshi-screen` renders a complete off-screen dashboard frame for every refresh.
2. `yaoshi-dashboard` owns a tty1 presenter with the previous rendered frame and the next rendered frame.
3. The first frame is a full repaint.
4. Normal refresh ticks use a differential presenter; startup, recovery, and periodic repaint ticks use a full presenter.
5. Full repaint occurs on first frame, startup settling frames, resize, VT reacquire, font profile reapplied, terminal grid changed, presenter previous frame missing, previous frame dimensions differing from next frame dimensions, invalid presenter style state, write failure recovery, or the periodic dashboard repaint interval.
6. Normal differential refresh ticks may emit only hide cursor, cursor move, foreground style set, bold modifier set/reset, changed ASCII bytes, spaces for stale-tail cleanup, and flush.
7. Normal differential refresh ticks never emit clear screen, terminal reset, alternate screen enter or leave, scroll region, erase display, whole-row erase, VT disallocate, or full frame write.
8. Differential row comparison uses cell byte and style equality. Unchanged rows emit no terminal writes.
9. Changed rows compute common unchanged prefix and suffix, split the remaining dirty range by style span, move to each span start, emit style, and write only changed bytes.
10. When a new row is shorter than the previous visible row, the presenter overwrites stale tail cells with ASCII spaces.
11. ttyS0 dashboard screen-frame output is emitted only after tty1 presentation flush succeeds or returns a bounded write error.
12. ttyS0 is opened with nonblocking/no-controlling-tty flags.
13. ttyS0 screen-frame writes are best-effort and droppable.
14. ttyS0 backpressure, absence, or write failure never changes tty1 rendering, dashboard health, service state, refresh cadence, or product text.

### 9.5 Dashboard root grammar and layout

Every supported dashboard frame uses this root-region grammar:

```text
HeaderLine   height 1
StatusLine   height 1
ChromeGap    height 1
BodyRegion   height root_height - 3
```

`HeaderLine`:

```text
Yaoshi Dashboard v0.0.1  <hostname>  up <uptime>
```

`StatusLine`:

```text
Prepare <prepare-state>  Root <root-expansion-state>  SSH <ssh-ready-state>  Net <network-state>  Health <health-state><health-reason-suffix>
```

`<health-reason-suffix>` is empty when `<health-state>` is `ok`. When `<health-state>` is `warning`, `critical`, or `unavailable`, it is a colon, one ASCII space, and the first matching reason token in this priority order:

```text
prepare
root
ssh
kernel
net
cpu
ram
disk
inode
thermal
source
```

`BodyRegion` uses exactly five named full-width sections in this order:

```text
System
Compute
Memory
Storage
Network
```

Base layout values are exact:

```text
body_height = root_height - 3
base_system_height = 5
base_compute_height = 3
base_memory_height = 4
base_storage_height = 4
base_network_height = 5
base_total_height = 21
extra_height = max(body_height - base_total_height, 0)
storage_detail_unit_height = 2
network_detail_unit_height = 2
```

Extra-row demand is computed from the current dashboard sample:

```text
compute_detail_candidates = ordered top-process and CPU-core detail rows defined in Section 9.7
storage_detail_units = ordered two-row disk detail units defined in Section 9.7
network_detail_units = ordered two-row interface detail units defined in Section 9.7
compute_extra_demand_rows = min(6, len(compute_detail_candidates))
storage_extra_demand_rows = min(6, len(storage_detail_units) * storage_detail_unit_height)
network_extra_demand_rows = min(6, len(network_detail_units) * network_detail_unit_height)
```

Storage and network detail allocation is unit-atomic. A two-row disk unit or two-row interface unit may be allocated only when two extra rows are available for that section. The renderer never emits an orphan first row or orphan second row for a disk or interface detail unit.

Extra-row allocation is exact:

```text
compute_extra_rows = 0
storage_extra_rows = 0
network_extra_rows = 0
remaining_extra = extra_height
while remaining_extra > 0:
  allocated_this_pass = false
  if remaining_extra >= storage_detail_unit_height
     and storage_extra_rows + storage_detail_unit_height <= storage_extra_demand_rows:
    storage_extra_rows = storage_extra_rows + storage_detail_unit_height
    remaining_extra = remaining_extra - storage_detail_unit_height
    allocated_this_pass = true
  if remaining_extra >= network_detail_unit_height
     and network_extra_rows + network_detail_unit_height <= network_extra_demand_rows:
    network_extra_rows = network_extra_rows + network_detail_unit_height
    remaining_extra = remaining_extra - network_detail_unit_height
    allocated_this_pass = true
  if remaining_extra > 0
     and compute_extra_rows < compute_extra_demand_rows:
    compute_extra_rows = compute_extra_rows + 1
    remaining_extra = remaining_extra - 1
    allocated_this_pass = true
  if allocated_this_pass == false:
    break
```

Section heights are exact:

```text
system_height = base_system_height
compute_height = base_compute_height + compute_extra_rows
memory_height = base_memory_height
storage_height = base_storage_height + storage_extra_rows
network_height = base_network_height + network_extra_rows
body_tail_canvas_height = body_height - system_height - compute_height - memory_height - storage_height - network_height
```

`body_tail_canvas_height` is never negative. When it is positive, those rows are blank canvas below `Network`.

Section placement is exact:

```text
System.x = BodyRegion.x
System.y = BodyRegion.y
System.width = BodyRegion.width
System.height = system_height
Compute.x = BodyRegion.x
Compute.y = System.y + system_height
Compute.width = BodyRegion.width
Compute.height = compute_height
Memory.x = BodyRegion.x
Memory.y = Compute.y + compute_height
Memory.width = BodyRegion.width
Memory.height = memory_height
Storage.x = BodyRegion.x
Storage.y = Memory.y + memory_height
Storage.width = BodyRegion.width
Storage.height = storage_height
Network.x = BodyRegion.x
Network.y = Storage.y + storage_height
Network.width = BodyRegion.width
Network.height = network_height
```

Layout validity rules:

1. At the supported dashboard floor `80x24`, `BodyRegion.height` is `21`, `base_total_height` is `21`, and no bounded extra detail rows are required.
2. At `96x28`, `BodyRegion.height` is `25`; when storage and network detail demand are both nonzero, the allocation renders exactly one two-row storage detail unit and exactly one two-row network detail unit, and compute detail rows are omitted unless storage or network demand leaves an extra row available.
3. Every section is full width.
4. The dashboard never uses a two-column layout.
5. The dashboard never requires horizontal scrolling.
6. Extra root height up to `44` rows increases bounded inventory detail capacity only through the allocation algorithm above.
7. Extra root width up to `160` cells reduces field compaction but never changes section order or detail-unit atomicity.
8. Detail rows and detail units are never allocated by CPU count, disk count, interface count, address count, or route count directly; they are allocated only by the bounded extra-row demand algorithm.
9. Storage and network overflow rendering replaces the final allocated two-row unit with one overflow row followed by one blank canvas row only when at least two rows were allocated for that section.

Dashboard row-budget renderer:

```text
DashboardRow = ordered Segment list
Segment.kind = mandatory | important | optional | gauge
```

Dashboard row-budget rules:

1. Every `HeaderLine`, `StatusLine`, section label, fixed section data row, bounded inventory summary row, bounded detail row, bounded detail unit row, and overflow row is rendered through a row-specific budget renderer.
2. Dashboard row renderers are not allowed to compose an arbitrary full row and then apply `truncate_ascii` to the whole row.
3. Dynamic field values are ASCII-sanitized before segment budgeting.
4. `mandatory` segments define the semantic identity of a row and must be present in every emitted row.
5. `important` segments may be compacted only by the row-specific compaction order defined for that row.
6. `optional` segments may be dropped only by the row-specific drop order defined for that row.
7. `gauge` segments are atomic. A gauge is rendered complete or omitted. A dashboard row never emits a partial gauge.
8. A row-specific renderer must return an emitted ASCII row whose byte length is less than or equal to the row region width before the frame is accepted.
9. If a row-specific renderer cannot fit its mandatory segments at the active dashboard floor, the renderer returns an internal layout error and the frame is not emitted.
10. Host-controlled strings are compacted inside their own field budget with `truncate_ascii`; this is the only dashboard use of `truncate_ascii` on dynamic content.
11. No emitted dashboard row may end with a dangling separator, an unclosed gauge bracket, a lone hyphen, a trailing `-`, or a field label whose value was fully removed.
12. Stable numeric fields use fixed-width formatting inside their local row format so ordinary refreshes do not shift adjacent labels.
13. Every emitted dashboard row has an explicit complete style span list. Style from one row is never inherited by the next row.
14. Warning, critical, and unavailable styles apply only to the value span and visible suffix span that carry that state, never to later neutral fields in the same row and never to a following row.

Row-specific compaction priorities are exact:

| Row family                                      | Compaction and drop order                                                                                                                                                       |
| ----------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `HeaderLine`                                    | Compact hostname to `32` cells, then compact uptime to the shortest duration formatter; no segment may be dropped.                                                              |
| `StatusLine`                                    | Preserve `Prepare`, `Root`, `SSH`, `Net`, and `Health` groups in that order; compact only `<health-reason-suffix>` by dropping the reason token when width requires it.         |
| `Machine`                                       | Preserve `<machine-display>` first, then distinct board display, then firmware version; board and firmware vendor strings are optional and are dropped before truncating names. |
| `CPU`                                           | Preserve CPU count and load values; compact `<cpu-display>` first.                                                                                                              |
| `Usage`, `RAM`, `Swap`, `Root`, filesystem rows | Preserve numeric percentage and alert suffix; omit the gauge before truncating the numeric percentage or alert suffix.                                                          |
| `DIMMs`                                         | Preserve DIMM count, total capacity, speed summary, and ECC state; compact vendor summary first.                                                                                |
| Storage disk unit row 1                         | Preserve disk dev path, size, transport, and disk role; compact disk display first.                                                                                             |
| Storage disk unit row 2                         | Preserve `sn` label, serial value, and filesystem state; omit gauge before truncating filesystem percentage or alert suffix.                                                    |
| Network `Route`                                 | Preserve route state, route interface, gateway, and metric; compact route display address first.                                                                                |
| Network `NICs`                                  | Preserve interface count, route summary, access summary, and attention count; compact access summary first.                                                                     |
| Network interface unit row 1                    | Preserve interface name, role, kind, state, MTU, and at least one address or `unavailable`; compact address list first and speed second.                                        |
| Network interface unit row 2                    | Preserve `mac`, rx rate, tx rate, and alert suffix when present; compact MAC first, then omit non-alert error/drop details when width requires it.                              |

### 9.6 Dashboard summary states

Prepare state:

| Filesystem state                                                        | Rendered state |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------- |
| `/var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state` contains `applied`                      | `applied`      |
| `/var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state` contains `running`                      | `running`      |
| `/var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state` contains `failed`                       | `failed`       |
| `/var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state` is absent                               | `pending`      |
| `/var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state` is unreadable or contains another value | `unknown`      |

Root expansion state:

| Filesystem state                                                            | Rendered state |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------- |
| `/var/lib/yaoshi/root-growth-state` contains `expanded`                     | `expanded`     |
| `/var/lib/yaoshi/root-growth-state` contains `failed`                       | `failed`       |
| `/var/lib/yaoshi/root-growth-state` is absent                               | `unknown`      |
| `/var/lib/yaoshi/root-growth-state` is unreadable or contains another value | `unknown`      |

SSH root key state:

| Filesystem state                                                   | Rendered state |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------- |
| `/root/.ssh/authorized_keys` contains at least one non-empty line  | `enabled`      |
| `/root/.ssh/authorized_keys` exists and contains no non-empty line | `disabled`     |
| `/root/.ssh/authorized_keys` is absent or unreadable               | `unavailable`  |

Service process states are process-liveness states, not systemd unit states.

| Process state               | Rule                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| --------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `<ssh-process-state>`       | `running` when at least one inspected process has `/proc/<pid>/comm` equal to `sshd`; otherwise `not-running`.                                                                                                  |
| `<networkd-process-state>`  | `running` when at least one inspected process has `/proc/<pid>/comm` equal to `systemd-network`; otherwise `not-running`.                                                                                       |
| `<dashboard-process-state>` | `running` when the current process is rendering a frame.                                                                                                                                                        |
| `<root-shell-state>`        | `running` when at least one inspected process has `/proc/<pid>/comm` equal to `bash` and the controlling terminal field in `/proc/<pid>/stat` equals the device number of `/dev/tty2`; otherwise `not-running`. |

`<ssh-listening-state>`:

| State           | Rule                                                                                                        |
| --------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `listening`     | `/proc/net/tcp` or `/proc/net/tcp6` contains a socket whose local port is `22` and whose TCP state is `0A`. |
| `not-listening` | TCP socket tables are readable and no port `22` listener exists.                                            |
| `unavailable`   | Both TCP socket tables are absent or unreadable.                                                            |

`<ssh-ready-state>`:

| State         | Rule                                                                                                               |
| ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `ready`       | SSH root key state is `enabled`, `<ssh-process-state>` is `running`, and `<ssh-listening-state>` is `listening`.   |
| `starting`    | SSH root key state is `enabled` and at least one of `<ssh-process-state>` or `<ssh-listening-state>` is not ready. |
| `disabled`    | SSH root key state is `disabled`.                                                                                  |
| `unavailable` | SSH root key state is `unavailable`, process scanning fails, or TCP listener scanning is unavailable.              |

`<network-state>`:

| State         | Rule                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `ready`       | The network snapshot is readable, one selected default route exists, the selected route has a display address or gateway, and at least one SSH access address candidate exists. |
| `configuring` | The network snapshot is readable and the `ready` condition is false.                                                                                                            |
| `unavailable` | Interface, address, or route inventory cannot be read from rtnetlink and sysfs.                                                                                                 |

`<kernel-alert-state>`:

| State         | Rule                                                                                    |
| ------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `unavailable` | `/dev/kmsg` is absent or unreadable.                                                    |
| `error`       | At least one retained kernel alert has priority `0`, `1`, `2`, or `3`.                  |
| `warning`     | No retained error alert exists and at least one retained kernel alert has priority `4`. |
| `none`        | No retained warning or error alert exists.                                              |

`<health-state>`:

| State         | Rule                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `critical`    | Prepare state is `failed`, at least one alert state is `critical`, root expansion is `failed`, SSH readiness is `disabled` or `unavailable`, kernel alert state is `error`, or network error state is `critical`.          |
| `warning`     | No critical condition exists and prepare state is `running`, `pending`, or `unknown`, at least one alert state is `warning`, root expansion is `unknown`, SSH readiness is `starting`, or kernel alert state is `warning`. |
| `ok`          | Prepare state is `applied`, enough CPU, memory, storage, network, and SSH readiness sources are readable, SSH readiness is `ready`, and no warning, critical, root, kernel, or network condition exists.                   |
| `unavailable` | The dashboard cannot read enough CPU, memory, storage, and network sources to produce a health judgment.                                                                                                                   |

### 9.7 Dashboard section rows

`System` section rows, in order:

```text
System
Boot      kernel <kernel-release> - prepare <prepare-state> - growth <root-expansion-state>
Access    ssh root@<ssh-access-address>:22 <ssh-ready-state> - tty2 <root-shell-state>
Machine   <machine-display> - board <board-display> - fw <firmware-display>
Signals   thermal <temperature> <thermal-source> <thermal-alert-field> - kernel <kernel-alert-state>
```

Machine row field rules:

1. `<machine-display>` is computed by the deterministic hardware identity rules in Section 9.9 after DMI placeholder filtering.
2. `<board-display>` is rendered only when it is readable, non-placeholder, distinct from `<machine-display>`, and not the same board name already used to build `<machine-display>`. When `<board-display>` is omitted, the row format is `Machine   <machine-display> - fw <firmware-display>`.
3. `<firmware-display>` renders firmware vendor and version when both are readable and non-placeholder, renders the single readable non-placeholder value when only one is available, and renders `unavailable` when neither is available.
4. The Machine row never allows a DMI placeholder product name such as `System Product Name` to hide a real board model.

When both `<temperature>` and `<thermal-source>` are `unavailable`, the `Signals` row renders:

```text
Signals   thermal unavailable - kernel <kernel-alert-state>
```

`<ssh-access-address>` is the first address from the SSH access address candidate order in Section 9.9. It is not the default-route address unless the access-address ordering selects the same interface and address. When no access address candidate exists, it renders `unavailable`.

`Compute` section rows, in order:

```text
Compute
CPU       <cpu-display> - <logical-cpu-count> CPUs - load <load1> <load5> <load15>
Usage     <cpu-used> <cpu-used-gauge> - max cpu<max-core-index> <max-core-cpu-percent> - psi <cpu-pressure-some-avg10> <cpu-alert-field>
```

When per-core data is unavailable, the `Usage` row renders `max unavailable` in place of `max cpu<max-core-index> <max-core-cpu-percent>`.

Compute detail candidate rows are built in this order:

1. The top-process row when aggregate CPU usage is `>= 85.000%` or the top process CPU percentage is `>= 25.000%`.
2. Per-core rows whose alert field is `critical`, sorted by CPU usage descending and then core index ascending.
3. Per-core rows whose alert field is `warning`, sorted by CPU usage descending and then core index ascending.
4. Remaining per-core rows sorted by CPU usage descending and then core index ascending.

Top-process row format:

```text
Top proc  <comm> pid <pid> - cpu <process-cpu-percent> - rss <rss>
```

Core row format:

```text
cpu<index>    <core-cpu-percent> <core-cpu-gauge> <core-alert-field>
```

Compute detail rendering rules:

1. The number of rendered compute detail rows is `compute_extra_rows`.
2. When `compute_extra_rows == 0`, no compute detail row is rendered; the required `CPU` and `Usage` rows still summarize logical CPU count and max core usage.
3. When candidate rows exceed `compute_extra_rows` and `compute_extra_rows >= 2`, the last rendered compute detail row is `... <n> more CPU cores not shown`.
4. When candidate rows exceed `compute_extra_rows` and `compute_extra_rows == 1`, the first candidate row is rendered and hidden CPU cores remain counted by the required `CPU` row.
5. Per-core rows are never rendered outside `compute_extra_rows`.

`Memory` section rows, in order:

```text
Memory
RAM       <memory-used>/<memory-total> (<memory-used-percent>) <memory-used-gauge> <memory-alert-field>
Swap      <swap-used>/<swap-total> (<swap-used-percent>) <swap-used-gauge> <swap-alert-field>
DIMMs     <populated-dimm-count> DIMMs - <dimm-total-capacity> - <memory-vendor-summary> - <memory-speed-summary> - ecc <memory-ecc-state>
```

The `DIMMs` row is a bounded hardware identity summary. It never expands into a per-DIMM list. When SMBIOS memory device data is absent, unreadable, malformed, or has no populated memory devices, the row renders:

```text
DIMMs     unavailable
```

`Storage` section rows, in order:

```text
Storage
Root      <root-used>/<root-total> (<root-used-percent>) <root-used-gauge> <root-alert-field> - inode <inode-used-percent> <inode-alert-field>
I/O       r <read-rate>/s w <write-rate>/s - iops r <read-iops>/s w <write-iops>/s
```
