```text
Disks     <disk-count> disks - root <root-dev> <root-size> <root-transport> <root-disk-display> - attention <disk-alert-count>
<storage-detail-unit>
```

`<disk-alert-count>` is the number of disks whose disk, mounted filesystem, or backing filesystem alert field is `warning` or `critical`.

When the root backing disk is unreadable, the `Disks` row renders:

```text
Disks     <disk-count> disks - root unavailable - attention <disk-alert-count>
```

Storage detail candidates are two-row disk units built in this order:

1. The disk backing `/` when readable.
2. Disks with a `critical` alert, excluding the root backing disk when it was already included.
3. Disks with a `warning` alert, excluding already included disks.
4. Mounted disks, sorted by kernel name ascending, excluding already included disks.
5. Remaining disks sorted by kernel name ascending.

Mounted filesystem selection for a disk detail unit is exact:

1. Select the mounted filesystem whose mountpoint is `/` when it belongs to the disk.
2. Otherwise select the mounted filesystem with a `critical` alert and highest used percentage.
3. Otherwise select the mounted filesystem with a `warning` alert and highest used percentage.
4. Otherwise select the mounted filesystem with highest used percentage.
5. If percentages tie, sort by mountpoint byte order ascending.
6. If no mounted filesystem belongs to the disk, the disk filesystem state is `not mounted`.

Storage detail unit format when a selected mounted filesystem belongs to the disk:

```text
<prefix><dev> <size> - <transport> - <disk-display> - <disk-role>
  sn <serial> - fs <fs-used>/<fs-total> (<fs-used-percent>) <fs-gauge> <fs-alert-field>
```

Storage detail unit format when no mounted filesystem belongs to the disk:

```text
<prefix><dev> <size> - <transport> - <disk-display> - not mounted
  sn <serial> - fs not mounted
```

Storage detail field rules:

| Field         | Rule                                                                                                                               |
| ------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `<prefix>`    | `* ` for the disk backing `/` and two ASCII spaces for every other disk.                                                           |
| `<dev>`       | Whole-disk device path, for example `/dev/nvme1n1`.                                                                                |
| `<disk-role>` | `root /` for the root backing disk; otherwise `mounted <mountpoint>` for the selected mounted filesystem; otherwise `not mounted`. |
| `<serial>`    | Disk serial after ASCII sanitization, or `unavailable`.                                                                            |
| `<fs-gauge>`  | Complete mounted filesystem gauge, or omitted by the row-budget renderer when width cannot fit the complete gauge.                 |

Storage detail rendering rules:

1. `storage_extra_rows` is always an even number because disk units are two-row atoms.
2. The number of rendered disk detail units is `storage_extra_rows / 2` before applying overflow-row replacement.
3. When `storage_extra_rows == 0`, no disk detail unit is rendered; the required `Disks` row still summarizes disk count, root disk, and attention count.
4. A disk detail unit is never rendered as one row.
5. When candidate units exceed allocated units and `storage_extra_rows >= 2`, the last allocated unit is replaced by exactly one overflow row followed by one blank canvas row:

```text
... <n> more disks not shown
```

6. The overflow row uses the first row position of the replaced two-row unit. The second row position is blank canvas.
7. Mounted filesystem percentage renders a gauge only when the complete gauge fits the second row budget.
8. A `not mounted` disk unit renders no gauge.

`Network` section rows, in order:

```text
Network
Route     default <route-iface> <route-address> - gw <default-gateway> - metric <route-metric>
Traffic   all rx <rx-rate>/s tx <tx-rate>/s - pkts rx <rx-packet-rate>/s tx <tx-packet-rate>/s
Errors    all err rx <rx-error-rate>/s tx <tx-error-rate>/s - drop rx <rx-drop-rate>/s tx <tx-drop-rate>/s <network-alert-field>
NICs      <interface-count> ifaces - route <route-iface> - access <ssh-access-summary> - attention <interface-alert-count>
<network-detail-unit>
```

When no selected default route exists, the `Route` row renders:

```text
Route     default unavailable
```

When a selected default route exists without a gateway, `<default-gateway>` renders `direct`. When a selected default route exists without a display address on the selected route interface, `<route-address>` renders `unavailable`. The renderer never combines a local address from one interface with a gateway from another interface.

`<interface-count>` counts non-loopback interfaces. `<interface-alert-count>` is the number of non-loopback interfaces whose error or drop alert field is `warning` or `critical`, plus the selected default-route interface when its carrier or operstate makes the selected default route unusable. A tunnel, virtual, bridge, VLAN, bond, veth, or unknown interface with missing MAC address, missing link speed, or zero traffic does not count as attention by itself.

`<ssh-access-summary>` renders `unavailable` when no SSH access address candidate exists. Otherwise it renders `<iface>` when all selected access candidates belong to one interface, and `<iface> +<additional-interface-count>` when selected access candidates span more than one interface. The first `<iface>` is the interface owning `<ssh-access-address>`.

`Traffic` and `Errors` rows aggregate all non-loopback interfaces. Loopback is excluded.

Network detail candidates are two-row interface units built in this order:

1. The selected default-route interface when available.
2. Interfaces that own SSH access address candidates, excluding already included interfaces, sorted by SSH access address candidate order.
3. Interfaces with a `critical` network error or drop alert, excluding already included interfaces.
4. Interfaces with a `warning` network error or drop alert, excluding already included interfaces.
5. Non-loopback physical interfaces with at least one non-loopback address, sorted by interface name ascending, excluding already included interfaces.
6. Non-loopback tunnel, bridge, bond, VLAN, veth, and other virtual interfaces with at least one non-loopback address, sorted by interface name ascending, excluding already included interfaces.
7. Remaining non-loopback interfaces sorted by interface name ascending.

Network detail unit format:

```text
<iface>   <role> <kind> <state> - <addresses> - <speed> - mtu <mtu>
       mac <mac> - rx <rx-rate>/s tx <tx-rate>/s - drop rx <rx-drop-rate>/s tx <tx-drop-rate>/s <interface-alert-field>
```

Network detail field rules:

| Field         | Rule                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `<role>`      | `route+access`, `route`, `access`, or `iface`, determined from the selected default route and SSH access address candidates.                                            |
| `<kind>`      | `physical`, `tunnel`, `wireguard`, `bridge`, `bond`, `vlan`, `veth`, `virtual`, or `other`, determined by the network snapshot rules in Section 9.9.                    |
| `<state>`     | Sanitized operstate when readable; `configuring` when the interface has no usable address and operstate is `unknown`; otherwise `unavailable` when state is unreadable. |
| `<addresses>` | Sorted non-loopback addresses joined by comma and one ASCII space, then compacted inside the field budget.                                                              |
| `<speed>`     | `<decimal> Mb/s`, `<decimal> Gb/s`, or `unavailable`. Link speed absence is descriptive for non-physical interfaces and is not an alert by itself.                      |
| `<mac>`       | Lowercase hex octets separated by `:`, or `unavailable`. Missing MAC is descriptive for non-physical interfaces and is not an alert by itself.                          |

Network detail rendering rules:

1. `network_extra_rows` is always an even number because interface units are two-row atoms.
2. The number of rendered interface detail units is `network_extra_rows / 2` before applying overflow-row replacement.
3. When `network_extra_rows == 0`, no interface detail unit is rendered; the required `Route`, `Traffic`, `Errors`, and `NICs` rows still summarize route, traffic, errors, interface count, access, and attention.
4. An interface detail unit is never rendered as one row.
5. When candidate units exceed allocated units and `network_extra_rows >= 2`, the last allocated unit is replaced by exactly one overflow row followed by one blank canvas row:

```text
... <n> more interfaces not shown
```

6. The overflow row uses the first row position of the replaced two-row unit. The second row position is blank canvas.
7. Interface address lists compact inside `<addresses>` before any other field on the first row is compacted.
8. Interface warning and critical style applies only to the numeric drop/error value that triggered the alert and the visible alert suffix.

### 9.8 Dashboard alert fields and severity thresholds

An alert field is a conditional state suffix rendered after a thresholded value or gauge. Alert field rendering is exact:

| Reducer state | Visible suffix | Style on value and suffix |
| ------------- | -------------- | ------------------------- |
| below warning | empty string   | metric default style      |
| warning       | `warning`      | `Warning`                 |
| critical      | `critical`     | `Critical`                |
| unreadable    | `unavailable`  | `Muted`                   |

Rules:

1. Rows below warning threshold do not reserve cells for an alert word.
2. Rows below warning threshold do not change foreground color because they are normal status rows.
3. Warning, critical, and unavailable rows include the visible suffix on the same row as the numeric value.
4. The visible suffix and foreground style are redundant carriers; the visible suffix is authoritative.
5. No dashboard metric row renders any word for a below-warning state.
6. Delta-derived alert fields whose current input is `measuring` or `configuring` render no alert suffix until numeric samples are available. They are not rendered as `unavailable` solely because the first delta sample has not completed.

Severity thresholds:

| Metric                      | Warning state condition            | Critical state condition    |
| --------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | --------------------------- |
| CPU usage                   | `>= 85.000%`                       | `>= 95.000%`                |
| Per-core CPU usage          | `>= 85.000%`                       | `>= 95.000%`                |
| Memory usage                | `>= 80.000%`                       | `>= 90.000%`                |
| Swap usage                  | `>= 80.000%`                       | `>= 90.000%`                |
| Root filesystem block usage | `>= 80.000%`                       | `>= 90.000%`                |
| Root filesystem inode usage | `>= 80.000%`                       | `>= 90.000%`                |
| Mounted disk filesystem use | `>= 80.000%`                       | `>= 90.000%`                |
| Thermal sensor temperature  | `>= 70.000 C`                      | `>= 85.000 C`               |
| Network error or drop rate  | `> 0.000/s`                        | `> 10.000/s`                |
| Kernel alerts               | `>= 1` warning alert               | `>= 1` error alert          |
| Prepare state               | `running`, `pending`, or `unknown` | `failed`                    |
| Root expansion state        | `unknown`                          | `failed`                    |
| SSH readiness               | `starting`                         | `disabled` or `unavailable` |

### 9.9 Dashboard data sources

Dashboard data sources are exactly Linux procfs, sysfs, rtnetlink, statvfs, `/dev/kmsg`, `/etc/hostname`, `/root/.ssh/authorized_keys`, `/var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state`, `/var/lib/yaoshi/root-growth-state`, `/sys/firmware/dmi/tables/smbios_entry_point`, and `/sys/firmware/dmi/tables/DMI`.

Rules:

1. `yaoshi-dashboard` does not invoke `ip`, `ss`, `netstat`, `lsblk`, `df`, `free`, `lscpu`, `dmidecode`, `lshw`, `findmnt`, `systemctl`, `journalctl`, `dmesg`, `nvme`, `smartctl`, `sensors`, `ipmitool`, `setfont`, or any other external command.
2. Unreadable data sources render `unavailable`.
3. Missing kernel files render `unavailable`.
4. The dashboard process continues after per-field read errors.
5. The dashboard process exits only on unrecoverable terminal initialization failure or process termination.
6. Every field read from a data source is ASCII-sanitized before rendering.
7. Kernel message text is read only to reduce retained warning and error priorities to `<kernel-alert-state>`; it is never rendered.
8. First-boot state and first-boot logs are deliberately not read by the dashboard.
9. SMBIOS memory device data is parsed only by an internal read-only parser over the sysfs DMI table files. The dashboard never invokes `dmidecode` or `lshw`.

Deterministic network snapshot rules:

1. Interface, address, and route inventory is read from rtnetlink. Link speed, MAC fallback, and selected sysfs attributes are read from `/sys/class/net/<iface>/`.
2. The dashboard builds one `NetworkSnapshot` before reducing network rows. It contains interfaces, addresses, routes, selected default route, SSH access address candidates, per-interface counters, aggregate counters, and per-interface alert fields.
3. Interface records are keyed by interface index and retain the sanitized interface name.
4. Loopback interfaces are excluded from dashboard interface counts, traffic aggregation, error aggregation, access address candidates, and detail candidates.
5. Candidate default routes are routes in the main table whose destination prefix length is `0`.
6. Candidate default routes sort by ascending metric, then IPv4 before IPv6, then ascending interface name bytes, then ascending gateway address bytes, then ascending route protocol value.
7. The first sorted candidate default route is the selected default route.
8. A selected default route owns exactly one output interface. `<route-iface>` is that interface name.
9. `<default-gateway>` is the gateway on the selected route record. When the selected route has no gateway, it renders `direct`.
10. `<route-metric>` is the selected route metric as a decimal integer. When no metric is reported, it renders `unavailable`.
11. `<route-address>` is selected only from addresses assigned to `<route-iface>`. For IPv4 default routes, IPv4 addresses sort before IPv6 addresses; for IPv6 default routes, IPv6 addresses sort before IPv4 addresses. Within the preferred family, addresses sort by global scope before link scope, then ascending address bytes, then ascending prefix length. If no address exists on `<route-iface>`, `<route-address>` renders `unavailable`.
12. The renderer never selects `<route-address>` from an interface other than `<route-iface>`.
13. SSH access address candidates are non-loopback addresses on non-loopback interfaces whose address scope is not host scope and whose address is not unspecified, multicast, or loopback.
14. SSH access address candidates sort by interface kind priority, then address family, then address scope, then interface role, then interface name bytes, then address bytes, then prefix length.
15. Interface kind priority for SSH access is: `wireguard`, `tunnel`, `physical`, `bridge`, `bond`, `vlan`, `veth`, `virtual`, `other`.
16. Address family priority for SSH access is IPv4 before IPv6.
17. Address scope priority for SSH access is global before link scope.
18. Interface role priority for SSH access is selected default-route interface before other interfaces only within the same kind, family, and scope priority bucket.
19. `<ssh-access-address>` is the first SSH access address candidate after sorting. When the candidate set is empty, it renders `unavailable`.
20. `<ssh-access-summary>` is derived from the sorted SSH access address candidates as defined in Section 9.7.
21. Interface addresses render sorted by IPv4 before IPv6, then global scope before link scope, then ascending address bytes, then ascending prefix length.
22. `<addresses>` renders sorted interface addresses joined by comma and one ASCII space, then compacted inside the row-specific field budget.
23. MAC addresses render as lowercase hex octets separated by `:`. Missing MAC renders `unavailable` and is not an alert by itself for non-physical interfaces.
24. Link speed is read from `/sys/class/net/<iface>/speed`; positive speeds render as decimal Mb/s or Gb/s. Unreadable, zero, or negative values render `unavailable` and are not an alert by themselves for non-physical interfaces.
25. Interface kind is determined first from rtnetlink `IFLA_LINKINFO/IFLA_INFO_KIND` when present. Known values map exactly: `wireguard` to `wireguard`, `tun` and `tap` to `tunnel`, `bridge` to `bridge`, `bond` to `bond`, `vlan` to `vlan`, `veth` to `veth`. Unknown link-info kinds render `other`.
26. When link-info kind is absent, interface kind is `physical` when `/sys/class/net/<iface>/device` exists. Otherwise it is `virtual`.
27. An interface with kind `wireguard`, `tunnel`, `bridge`, `bond`, `vlan`, `veth`, `virtual`, or `other` may be a valid access interface and may be rendered in detail rows without requiring MAC address or speed.
28. Per-interface error and drop rates are computed from delta samples of rtnetlink or sysfs counters when available. Aggregate network error and drop rates are the sum across non-loopback interfaces.
29. An interface alert is `critical` when any per-interface error or drop rate is `> 10.000/s`; it is `warning` when no critical condition exists and any per-interface error or drop rate is `> 0.000/s`; otherwise it is below warning.
30. A selected default-route interface with operstate `down`, `lowerlayerdown`, `dormant`, or `notpresent` is an interface attention item even before error-rate samples are available.
31. A tunnel, virtual, bridge, VLAN, bond, veth, or unknown interface with `mac unavailable`, `speed unavailable`, `rx 0 B/s`, or `tx 0 B/s` is not an attention item unless it also satisfies an explicit alert condition above.

Deterministic hardware and storage selection rules:

1. Hostname is read from `/etc/hostname` first and falls back to `uname.nodename` only when `/etc/hostname` is absent or unreadable.
2. Kernel release is read from `uname.release`.
3. CPU vendor is the first `vendor_id` field in `/proc/cpuinfo`. `GenuineIntel` renders as `Intel`; `AuthenticAMD` renders as `AMD`; every other non-empty value is ASCII-sanitized and rendered as read. If absent, CPU vendor renders `unavailable`.
4. CPU model is the first `model name` field in `/proc/cpuinfo`; if absent, it is the first `Hardware` field; if both are absent, it renders `unavailable`.
5. `<cpu-display>` is computed from CPU vendor and CPU model. When both are available and the lowercase CPU model starts with the lowercase CPU vendor, `<cpu-display>` is `<cpu-model>`. When both are available and the model does not start with the vendor, `<cpu-display>` is `<cpu-vendor> <cpu-model>`. When only one is available, `<cpu-display>` is the available value. When neither is available, it renders `unavailable`.
6. Logical CPU count is the count of distinct `processor` fields in `/proc/cpuinfo`; if absent, it is the count of `cpuN` rows in `/proc/stat`; if both are absent, it renders `unavailable`.
7. System vendor, product name, board vendor, board name, firmware vendor, and firmware version are read respectively from `/sys/class/dmi/id/sys_vendor`, `/sys/class/dmi/id/product_name`, `/sys/class/dmi/id/board_vendor`, `/sys/class/dmi/id/board_name`, `/sys/class/dmi/id/bios_vendor`, and `/sys/class/dmi/id/bios_version`.
8. DMI identity strings are ASCII-sanitized, trimmed, and placeholder-filtered before display. Placeholder matching is case-insensitive after trimming and collapsing ASCII whitespace. The exact placeholder set is `system product name`, `system manufacturer`, `to be filled by o.e.m.`, `to be filled by oem`, `default string`, `default system`, `default`, `not specified`, `none`, `unknown`, and `o.e.m.`.
9. `<machine-display>` is computed in this order: non-placeholder product identity, non-placeholder board-name identity, non-placeholder system vendor, then `unavailable`. Product identity renders `<system-vendor> <product-name>` when both are non-placeholder and product name does not already start with the vendor; otherwise it renders the non-placeholder product name. Board-name identity uses the first non-placeholder vendor in this order: system vendor, then board vendor. It renders `<vendor> <board-name>` when a vendor is available and the board name does not already start with that vendor; otherwise it renders the non-placeholder board name.
10. `<board-display>` is the board identity using board vendor plus board name when both are non-placeholder, otherwise the non-placeholder board name. It is omitted when unavailable, when byte-equal to `<machine-display>` after ASCII sanitization, or when `<machine-display>` was derived from the same board name.
11. `<firmware-display>` renders `<firmware-vendor> <firmware-version>` when both are non-placeholder and version does not already start with the vendor; otherwise it renders the non-placeholder firmware version, then the non-placeholder firmware vendor, then `unavailable`.
12. SMBIOS memory devices are parsed from `/sys/firmware/dmi/tables/smbios_entry_point` and `/sys/firmware/dmi/tables/DMI` by the internal parser. Only SMBIOS Type 17 Memory Device structures with decoded size greater than zero are populated DIMMs.
13. `<populated-dimm-count>` is the number of populated Type 17 memory devices. If it is zero, the `DIMMs` row renders `unavailable`.
14. `<dimm-total-capacity>` is the sum of decoded sizes for all populated Type 17 memory devices and is rendered through the Yaoshi byte-size formatter. `<memory-vendor-summary>` is computed from populated Type 17 manufacturer strings. If every populated DIMM with a manufacturer string has the same manufacturer, it renders `<manufacturer> x<count>`. If multiple manufacturers appear, manufacturers are sorted by total populated bytes descending, then count descending, then ASCII name ascending, and the summary renders `<first-manufacturer>+<other-manufacturer-count> vendors`. If no manufacturer string is readable, it renders `unavailable`.
15. `<memory-speed-summary>` uses configured memory speed when present and otherwise memory speed. If all populated DIMMs with a readable speed share one value, it renders `<speed> MT/s`. If readable speeds differ, it renders `mixed <min-speed>-<max-speed> MT/s`. If no populated DIMM has a readable speed, it renders `speed unavailable`.
16. `<memory-ecc-state>` is `yes` when at least one populated DIMM has total width greater than data width. It is `no` when every populated DIMM has readable total width and data width and every total width equals data width. It is `unknown` otherwise.
17. Disk inventory is read from `/sys/block` and excludes names starting with `ram`, `loop`, `dm-`, `md`, `zram`, `sr`, or `fd`.
18. The root backing disk is resolved from `/proc/self/mountinfo` for `/`, then from sysfs parent traversal until a whole disk under `/sys/block` is reached.
19. Disk transport is classified as `nvme` when the disk name starts with `nvme`, `virtio` when the sysfs device path contains `/virtio`, `usb` when the sysfs device path contains `/usb`, `mmc` when the disk name starts with `mmcblk`, `sata/scsi` when the sysfs device path contains `/ata`, `/scsi`, `/sas`, or `/sata`, and `other` otherwise.
20. Disk vendor, model, and serial are read from `/sys/block/<disk>/device/vendor`, `/sys/block/<disk>/device/model`, and `/sys/block/<disk>/device/serial`; missing values render `unavailable`. `<disk-display>` and `<root-disk-display>` render `<vendor> <model>` when both are available and the lowercase model does not start with the lowercase vendor, render `<model>` when the model already starts with the vendor, render the single available value when only one is available, and render `unavailable` when both are unavailable.
21. Mounted filesystem rows are joined to disks by major:minor device numbers from `/proc/self/mountinfo` and sysfs.
22. Thermal source selection sorts readable `/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon*/temp*_input` files by ascending path bytes and selects the highest current temperature as the rendered temperature; the source label is the sanitized hwmon `name` plus the temp input basename.

## 10. Development feedback model, tests, and acceptance

Development tests verify functional behavior defined in this document. Tests do not define additional product behavior.

### 10.1 Development and acceptance command set

The development and acceptance command set is not a product command surface. These commands are development and CI commands run from the direnv-loaded repository environment. The QEMU command in this section is release acceptance, not Dashboard UI iteration.

UI renderer validation:

```text
cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-screen
```

Installer state-machine and adapter tests:

```text
cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-installer --lib
```

Payload format, virtual target graph, and image writer tests:

```text
cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-payload -p yaoshi-image
```

Debian installed-root ext4 object, package index, and boot artifact checks:

```text
cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-debian
```

Dashboard parser, sampler, reducer, and model tests:

```text
cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-dashboard --lib
```

Real-machine Dashboard redeploy and capture check, recommended non-gating:

```text
cargo build --locked --target x86_64-unknown-linux-musl -p yaoshi-dashboard
cargo build --locked --target x86_64-unknown-linux-musl -p yaoshi-test --bin yaoshi-real-capture
YAOSHI_REAL_HOST=<root-ssh-host> YAOSHI_REAL_SSH_KEY=<private-key-path> cargo run --locked -p yaoshi-test --bin real-dashboard-check
```

Workspace default tests:

```text
cargo nextest run --locked --workspace
```

Single full-flow QEMU release acceptance test:

```text
cargo nextest run --locked --workspace --profile qemu
```

### 10.2 Change-class test routing

Change routing is exact for local change validation. When one change belongs to multiple rows, the required commands are the union of every matching row. `real-dashboard-check` is the only recommended non-gating physical-target Dashboard redeploy and capture check. It is not a product command, not a product image build, not a QEMU substitute, not a local-completion gate, and not a release acceptance gate.

| Change class                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Required tests before product image build                                                                                                                            | Product image build required for change validation | QEMU required for local change validation | Recommended non-gating physical-target check |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- |
| Installer visible text, layout, foreground style, ASCII sanitization, truncation, gauge, action-row rendering                                                                                                                                                                     | `cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-screen`                                                                                                                        | no                                                 | no                                        | no                                           |
| Installer input state machine                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-installer --lib` and `cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-screen`                                                             | no                                                 | no                                        | no                                           |
| Installer disk discovery or destructive target write path                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-installer --lib`, `cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-payload -p yaoshi-image`, and `cargo nextest run --locked --workspace` | yes                                                | no                                        | no                                           |
| Payload prelude, manifest, extent table, zstd parameters, virtual target graph rule, or payload hash                                                                                                                                                                              | `cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-payload -p yaoshi-image` and `cargo nextest run --locked --workspace`                                                          | yes                                                | no                                        | no                                           |
| Debian hard package list, configured installed package list, Debian package-root construction, build-system customization layer, root bridge overlay, installed-root source merge, mke2fs ext4 construction, read-only e2fsck validation, boot artifact export, or module closure | `cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-debian` and `cargo nextest run --locked --workspace`                                                                           | yes                                                | no                                        | no                                           |
| Dashboard visible text, layout, foreground style, ASCII sanitization, truncation, gauge, section composition, row order, hardware identity row text, or bounded inventory rendering                                                                                               | `cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-screen`                                                                                                                        | no                                                 | no                                        | `real-dashboard-check`                       |
| Dashboard parser, sampler, reducer, summary state, threshold, data-source fixture, SMBIOS parser, storage identity parser, CPU identity parser, or network identity parser                                                                                                        | `cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-dashboard --lib`                                                                                                               | no                                                 | no                                        | `real-dashboard-check`                       |
| Terminus font profile bytes, font loader, terminal backend, font negotiation, or framebuffer capture helper                                                                                                                                                                       | `cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-screen`, `cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-installer --lib`, and `cargo nextest run --locked -p yaoshi-dashboard --lib`    | yes                                                | no                                        | `real-dashboard-check`                       |
| Yaoshi prepare service, first-boot service, ESP runtime prepare, first-boot script staging, ESP mount, dashboard symlink, or fixed root bridge path                                                                                                                               | `cargo nextest run --locked --workspace`                                                                                                                             | yes                                                | no                                        | no                                           |
| ESP loader entry or kernel command-line option text                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `cargo nextest run --locked --workspace`                                                                                                                             | yes                                                | no                                        | no                                           |
| MBR/GPT/FAT32/virtual target graph/image parser                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `cargo nextest run --locked --workspace`                                                                                                                             | yes                                                | no                                        | no                                           |
| Cache intent, layered artifact CAS, composite-file object, current-image stamp, or invalidation rule                                                                                                                                                                              | `cargo nextest run --locked --workspace`                                                                                                                             | yes when the final image can change                | no                                        | no                                           |

No change class named `Kernel config` exists. Loader entry and kernel command-line option text are ESP/image behavior and are covered by the loader-entry change class above. The table routes fast local validation only. QEMU is mandatory only for the release acceptance command set in Section 10.7, not for local change validation. The physical-target check column names the single non-gating live Dashboard redeploy check for changes that affect installed Dashboard observation.

### 10.3 Test profiles

`.config/nextest.toml` defines these profiles:

| Profile   | Contents                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| --------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `default` | Unit, parser, config, functional cache graph, Debian package index, Debian boot artifact export, first-frame kernel display prerequisite validation, installed-root ext4 object validation, ESP runtime prepare failure injection, installer module closure, MBR, GPT, FAT32, virtual installed target graph, payload prelude, payload manifest, extent table, zstd round-trip, payload installer writer, split initramfs, composite image construction, semantic screen renderer checks, deterministic ASCII UI validation artifact generation, installer row truncation checks, dashboard row-budget and atomic-gauge checks, dashboard `96x28` floor layout checks, dashboard two-row storage and network detail-unit checks, dashboard route/access network snapshot checks, dashboard diff-presenter checks, dashboard SSH health reducer checks, Terminus font-profile checks, foreground-only style-map checks, numeric-format checks, dashboard data-source parser checks, and static product checks. No live QEMU. |
| `qemu`    | Exactly one live QEMU complete-flow test named `qemu::flow`, hardware-virtualized through KVM, using one QEMU process with an installer boot epoch and an installed-target boot epoch after QMP source-media detach. It includes serial capture, QMP operation log, progress log, keyboard driving, functional validation files, bounded payload write progress output, installed SSH login check, installed dashboard validation output, prepare and first-boot validation output, serial screen-frame extraction, and display-density marker validation. It runs QEMU tests only. It does not rerun the default profile test set.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

`.config/nextest.toml` contains:

```toml
[profile.qemu]
test-threads = 1
default-filter = 'test(qemu::flow)'
```

The `qemu` profile selects exactly the live test whose fully qualified test name contains `qemu::flow`. No `qemu::boot_*` test is part of v0.0.1 acceptance.

### 10.4 Static assertions

Default profile assertions include:

```text
No product code invokes Kbuild
No product code reads build.linux_source
No product configuration accepts a build table
No product build path uses a Yaoshi self-written ext4 packer
No product build path invokes debugfs in write mode
No product build path invokes libext2fs, lwext4, fs-ext4, fstool, ext4plus, or ext4_rs
No product build path mounts an ext4 image on the host
No product build path creates a loop device
No product build path creates work/image/installed-target.raw
No product build path creates work/image/installed-target.write-plan
No product build path contains a build-installed-target-raw phase
No product build path contains a derive-target-write-plan phase
Effective Debian package include list contains linux-image-amd64, initramfs-tools, systemd-boot-efi-amd64-signed, and every configured installed.packages entry
Package index validation rejects grub-*, dracut, dracut-core, cloud-init, kbd, network-manager, shim-*, dkms, and linux-headers-*; procps, sudo, make, and gcc are allowed when configured or pulled by dependencies
mmdebstrap invocation uses --mode=unshare, --format=tar, the effective configured mirrors defaulting to Aliyun, and the effective hard-plus-configured include list
mmdebstrap produces the Debian package-root without root bridge overlay injection and without build-system script execution
Build-system assets come only from `.yaoshi/vendor/` when that directory is present, are staged as `/YAOSHI-CUSTOM/assets/`, and their fs-tree manifest digest invalidates customized root and downstream root artifacts
mke2fs invocation uses -t ext4, -b 4096, -I 256, -i 8192, -L YAOSHI_ROOT, and the fixed installed root UUID
e2fsck -fn returns status 0 before installed-root ext4 cache index creation
e2label validates installed-root.ext4 label before installed-root ext4 cache index creation
read-only debugfs validates required fixed root bridge paths before installed-root ext4 cache index creation
Configuration accepts system, sources, root, installed, and scripts root tables and rejects build and unknown root tables
Repository contains no tracked `.yaoshi` default configuration or script file
Yaoshi-specific `.gitignore` ignores `/.yaoshi/` and contains no `.yaoshi` negation rule
Bootstrap creates missing `.yaoshi/yaoshi.toml`, `.yaoshi/scripts/build-system.sh`, and `.yaoshi/scripts/first-boot.sh` from Askama templates
Bootstrap never overwrites existing `.yaoshi/yaoshi.toml`, `.yaoshi/scripts/build-system.sh`, or `.yaoshi/scripts/first-boot.sh`
Bootstrap does not create `./keys/root.pub`; missing default root key file still returns CONFIG during load-config
Every file in the Section 2.1.1 template inventory has one `askama::Template` context with `escape = "none"`

Template context structs contain no timestamp, user, host, path-to-workdir, reason, provenance, approval, audit, report, or explanation fields
Installed networkd DHCP template uses `[Match] Name=*` plus `Type=ether`, uses MAC-based DHCPv4 client identity, and does not encode legacy Ethernet prefix matching
Default sources are https://mirrors.aliyun.com/debian and https://mirrors.aliyun.com/debian-security
Default installed.packages contains curl, git, sudo, less, nano, iproute2, procps, psmisc, iputils-ping, nftables, openssh-client, and rsync
Default installed.packages contains no rust, cargo, go, nodejs, npm, build-essential, make, gcc, g++, dkms, or linux-headers-*
Build-system script runs in the customized-root layer after Debian package-root construction and before fixed identity cleanup for the customized root
Build-system script failure returns BUILD and creates no customized-root cache index
Fixed identity cleanup removes SSH host keys, truncates /etc/machine-id, and removes /var/lib/dbus/machine-id in both Debian package-root export and customized-root emission
Debian package-root object contains root.tar, packages.index, and boot/; installed-root ext4 object contains installed-root.ext4 and packages.index; neither object contains build-system script log

Debian package-root object and installed-root ext4 object contain no root-build.json
```

```text
changing yaoshi-installer invalidates installer binary, installer app initramfs, installer boot FAT32, and final installer composite only
changing root SSH keys invalidates ESP config, installed ESP, payload, and final installer composite, but not Debian package root, customized root, root bridge overlay, installed-root source, or installed-root ext4
changing ESP runtime prepare or first-boot script invalidates installed ESP, payload, and final installer composite, but not Debian package root, customized root, root bridge overlay, installed-root source, installed-root ext4, or installer boot FAT32
changing configured installed.packages invalidates Debian package root, customized root, installed-root source, installed-root ext4, installer module closure, installer base initramfs, installed ESP, installed payload, installer boot, final installer composite, and current-image stamp
changing first-boot script does not invalidate any root object
changing Askama config bootstrap templates does not invalidate an existing unchanged instance file until the generated instance file bytes change
changing Askama root bridge templates invalidates root bridge overlay, installed-root source, installed-root ext4, installed payload, final installer composite, and current-image stamp
changing Askama installed ESP templates invalidates installed ESP, payload, final installer composite, and current-image stamp, but not any root object
changing Askama installer boot templates invalidates installer boot FAT32, final installer composite, and current-image stamp, but not installed payload
changing Askama Debian helper templates invalidates Debian package root and downstream images
changing the installed ESP required file set invalidates installed ESP, payload, and final installer composite under the active v0.0.1 grammar; it is not represented by an alternate compatibility grammar
installed ESP intent uses Debian package-root boot export digest, not installed-root ext4 digest
installer boot intent uses Debian package-root boot export digest, not installed-root ext4 digest
payload extent encoding cache key excludes byte source object digest
payload extent encoding cache reuses unchanged blocks across installed ESP object digest changes
changing only installed ESP content reuses valid payload extent encoding cache entries for unchanged installed-root-ext4 nonzero extents
changing yaoshi-build source without a cacheable-output grammar change does not invalidate image artifacts by source fingerprint alone
product build and qemu::flow current-image fast path use foundation-fingerprint, installed-root-fingerprint, installed-runtime-fingerprint, installer-envelope-fingerprint, and published-image-fingerprint
resolve-cache-index-graph does not delete work
prepare-work-root-on-first-producer-miss runs only before the first cache miss producer that needs work
A build with current-image hit does not create or delete work
A build with current-image miss but full cache hit creates no root, Debian, initramfs, image, or binary staging tree before publish
```

### 10.5 QEMU test

The `qemu` profile contains exactly one live QEMU test:

```text
qemu::flow
```

`qemu::flow` is the sole live complete-flow release acceptance path for v0.0.1. It is not a low-resource compatibility test. It is not a bus matrix. It is not a screenshot oracle. It is not a Dashboard UI development loop. It does not replace default-profile unit, parser, renderer, static image, payload, fixture tests, or `real-dashboard-check` for physical-target Dashboard redeploy observation.

`qemu::flow` is an image-subject test. It treats `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img` as the black-box product artifact, boots that artifact, drives one successful install-to-boot path, and validates installed-target bootability, partition/filesystem shape, installed runtime services, Dashboard readiness, and SSH access. It does not become a general UI branch runner. Product branches that cannot be exercised without abandoning the install-to-boot path, such as rescue metadata erase and Erase Result return-to-target behavior, are covered by default-profile state-machine, renderer, and write-primitive tests; `qemu::flow` captures their reachable confirmation scene when it passes through the installer decision point but does not execute them.

Before launching QEMU, `qemu::flow` treats `.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img` as the primary test subject and runs require-current image verification. It does not define a second image build path and does not execute image-producing producer bodies. When the stamp is valid and the lightweight media check passes, QEMU reuses the existing image without running the build resolver graph, rebuilding, repacking, or republishing. When the stamp is absent, stale, malformed, or the published image fails lightweight media checks, `qemu::flow` fails before VM launch with the exact miss reason and requires the developer to run `cargo run --locked -p yaoshi --` first.

`qemu::flow` uses exactly one QEMU process and exactly one virtual machine. The process has two boot epochs:

```text
installer boot epoch
installed-target boot epoch after QMP source-media detach and guest reboot
```

The QEMU release acceptance host requirements are exact:

```text
host operating system is Linux
host architecture is x86_64
/dev/kvm exists as a character device
current user can open /dev/kvm read-write
host exposes at least 8 logical CPUs
host MemTotal is at least 16 GiB
host MemAvailable is at least 10 GiB when qemu::flow starts
QEMU scratch filesystem has at least payload_target_minimum_bytes + 4294967296 bytes available
qemu-system-x86_64 exists in PATH
ssh exists in PATH
```

The QEMU process configuration is exact:

```text
qemu-system-x86_64
-machine q35
-accel kvm
-cpu host
-smp 8
-m 8192M
-display vnc=127.0.0.1:0,to=99
-vga none
-device VGA,xres=2560,yres=1440,vgamem_mb=32
-device qemu-xhci,id=xhci
-device usb-kbd,bus=xhci.0,id=usb-kbd
-device pcie-root-port,id=source-port,bus=pcie.0,chassis=1,slot=1
-device pcie-root-port,id=target-port,bus=pcie.0,chassis=2,slot=2
-serial file:<qemu-output>/serial.log
-qmp unix:<qemu-output>/qmp.sock,server=on,wait=off
```

Firmware configuration is exact:

```text
-drive if=pflash,format=raw,readonly=on,file=<OVMF_CODE.fd>
-drive if=pflash,format=raw,file=<per-run-copy-of-OVMF_VARS.fd>
```

The QEMU harness discovers `OVMF_CODE.fd` and `OVMF_VARS.fd` from the active QEMU installation. Missing OVMF code, missing OVMF vars, a read-only per-run vars copy, or QEMU startup failure returns `ENVIRONMENT`.

The QEMU harness executes `query-kvm` through QMP after QMP greeting and fails unless KVM is present and enabled. A QEMU command line containing `tcg`, `-kernel`, `-initrd`, `-append`, `-snapshot`, USB installer storage, virtio target storage, AHCI/SATA target storage, or a second slow/compatibility QEMU storage profile is invalid for v0.0.1 acceptance. QEMU release acceptance is a single fast functional chain and is allowed to use unsafe host caching because persistence semantics are covered by product code and real-machine installation, not by QEMU's virtual media durability.

The QEMU storage configuration is exact:

```text
source installer media backend:
  -drive if=none,id=source,file=.yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img,format=raw,readonly=on,cache=unsafe,discard=ignore,detect-zeroes=off,aio=io_uring

source installer media device:
  -device nvme,id=source-nvme,bus=source-port,drive=source,serial=YAOSHI_SOURCE_0,bootindex=1

target backend:
  target_raw_path = <qemu-output>/target.raw
  target_raw_size = payload_target_minimum_bytes + 1073741824
  target_raw_format = raw sparse file
  -drive if=none,id=target,file=<qemu-output>/target.raw,format=raw,cache=unsafe,discard=unmap,detect-zeroes=unmap,aio=io_uring

target device:
  -device nvme,id=target-nvme,bus=target-port,drive=target,serial=YAOSHI_TARGET_0,bootindex=2
```

Real-machine installation continues to treat USB removable media as the primary operator path. QEMU release acceptance does not simulate that medium and does not carry a fast/slow split. The source installer image and writable target are both attached as NVMe devices behind explicit PCIe root ports so the single live flow stays focused on fast functional installer-to-installed validation while still allowing source-media hot removal before reboot. USB installer media emulation, virtio target disks, AHCI/SATA, additional target disks, and controller-matrix boot smoke tests are absent from v0.0.1 QEMU acceptance.

The QEMU network configuration is exact:

```text
-netdev user,id=net0,hostfwd=tcp:127.0.0.1:<random-free-local-port>-:22
-device virtio-net-pci,id=net-virtio,netdev=net0
```

The network device is attached from VM start. The installer contains no network module seed and runs no network service, so the network device has no installer product behavior. The installed epoch uses the same virtual NIC for DHCP and SSH host forwarding.

The QEMU boot and process-lifetime rules are exact:

1. `qemu::flow` launches one QEMU process.
2. `qemu::flow` does not use `-no-reboot`.
3. `qemu::flow` does not launch a second QEMU process.
4. The installer source NVMe device has `bootindex=1` and the target NVMe device has `bootindex=2`.
5. During the installer boot epoch, firmware boots the source installer media.
6. After the installer reaches Done, QMP detaches `source-nvme` before the Reboot action is activated.
7. The same QEMU process then reboots into the target disk.
8. During the installed-target boot epoch, the installer source media is absent from QMP device inventory and from guest block inventory.
9. QEMU exits only after installed-stage validation has passed or after the harness terminates the process on failure.

QMP source-media detach is exact:

```text
wait for installer complete marker
wait for Done screen validation
execute QMP device_del id=source-nvme
wait for QMP DEVICE_DELETED event for source-nvme
fail on QMP DEVICE_UNPLUG_GUEST_ERROR for source-nvme
execute QMP query-block and query-pci-derived device inventory
verify source-nvme is absent
activate Done Reboot through keyboard input
wait for QMP RESET event and installed boot serial/dashboard markers
```

`qmp.log` is a line-oriented operation log for progress diagnosis and failure debugging. It is not a raw QMP operation log and does not store unrelated QMP traffic. The exact success lines are:

```text
YAOSHI_QMP v=1 op=query-kvm status=enabled elapsed_ms=<ms>
YAOSHI_QMP v=1 op=device-del id=source-nvme status=requested elapsed_ms=<ms>
YAOSHI_QMP v=1 op=device-deleted id=source-nvme status=complete elapsed_ms=<ms>
YAOSHI_QMP v=1 op=query-block status=complete elapsed_ms=<ms>
YAOSHI_QMP v=1 op=source-absent status=complete elapsed_ms=<ms>
YAOSHI_QMP v=1 op=reset status=observed elapsed_ms=<ms>
```

On QMP failure while the harness can still write output, `qmp.log` records exactly one failing line:

```text
YAOSHI_QMP v=1 op=<operation> status=failed reason=<ascii-reason> elapsed_ms=<ms>
```

Installer-epoch verification is exact:

```text
ensure-current-installer-image returns .yaoshi/out/yaoshi.img
no Linux kernel compilation occurs during the build
external USB image writers are not invoked
generated installer image boots through /EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI from the source NVMe device
systemd-boot loads /YAOSHI/BOOT/VMLINUZ, /YAOSHI/INSTALLER-BASE.CPIO.ZST, and /YAOSHI/INSTALLER-APP.CPIO.ZST
Debian kernel unpacks the base and app initramfs layers and executes /init from the app layer through rdinit=/init
installer runtime opens tty1 and ttyS0
installer loads declared module closure without modprobe
installer applies a Terminus installer font profile selected from 10x20 or 12x24
installer emits the display marker
installer reapplies the selected Terminus regular face after probing both size candidates
installer renders and flushes the first Prepare frame before media discovery
installer emits first_frame_flushed before installation-media discovery
installer discovers exactly one source image
installer validates YAOSHI_PAYLOAD_V1 prelude, manifest, extent table hash, blob-area bounds, structural bounds, and zstd window constraints
installer excludes the source NVMe disk from the target list
installer lists target-action disks as target rows
installer accepts the NVMe target disk whose logical sector size is 512 bytes and whose size is target_raw_size
installer enters Target with Confirm target focused
installer changes selected disk only through ArrowUp and ArrowDown
installer never treats a disk row as an action
installer enters Confirm Install only by activating Confirm target
installer enters Confirm Install with Back focused
installer starts no target write before the destructive action is activated
installer moves focus to Erase disk only <dev_path> and captures that confirmation scene
installer moves focus to Install Yaoshi to <dev_path>
installer emits target_write_started only after the Write Start Rule completes
installer emits write_task serial markers in the exact task order
installer emits write_progress serial markers during copy-yaoshi-image
installer clears target head and tail ranges
installer handles every payload extent at its declared logical offset
installer clears payload zero extents through target zeroing primitives
installer does not perform per-extent encoded or uncompressed payload hash audit during write
installer target ESP contains expected systemd-boot, Debian kernel, Debian initramfs, loader entry, ESP runtime prepare, and dashboard artifacts
installer target root ext4 label is YAOSHI_ROOT and root filesystem checks pass
installer emits complete and renders Done
source installer NVMe device is detached by QMP before Reboot
Done Enter on Reboot emits controlled_exit action=reboot
same QEMU process observes guest reset after reboot request
unsupported installer display and Stopped failure scenes have a 15-second automatic poweroff fallback
```

Installed-target-epoch verification is exact:

```text
same QEMU process continues after the installer reboot
target disk is the same target.raw written by the installer epoch
installer source media is absent from QMP device inventory
installer source media is absent from guest block inventory
target boots through /EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI
systemd-boot loads /YAOSHI/BOOT/VMLINUZ and /YAOSHI/BOOT/INITRD.IMG
installed loader entry mirrors kernel console to ttyS0 and tty1 before Yaoshi Dashboard takeover
installed Debian kernel mounts YAOSHI_ROOT by PARTUUID
installed ESP mounts read-only at /boot
/usr/bin/yaoshi-dashboard resolves to /boot/YAOSHI/DASHBOARD/YAOSHI-DASHBOARD
yaoshi-prepare runs the fixed launcher and ESP runtime prepare before dashboard, root shell, and SSH service start
yaoshi-prepare grows GPT partition 2 and the mounted ext4 root filesystem when root growth succeeds
yaoshi-prepare records root-growth-state as expanded or failed
yaoshi-prepare writes config-state applied after mandatory ESP configuration succeeds
yaoshi-prepare writes prepare-state applied after mandatory ESP configuration succeeds
yaoshi-first-boot runs after prepare succeeds and before SSH service ordering allows SSH start
yaoshi-first-boot records first-boot-state as applied for the default first-boot script
yaoshi-first-boot creates /etc/machine-id when it is empty
yaoshi-first-boot creates SSH host private keys through the default first-boot script
yaoshi-prepare preserves installed_root_partition_guid when root growth succeeds
yaoshi-prepare keeps the root filesystem label YAOSHI_ROOT
yaoshi-prepare moves the GPT backup header to the physical target disk end when root growth succeeds
tty2 reaches root bash without getty or login prompt
systemd-networkd process reaches running state
ssh.service reaches a process-listening state sufficient for root public-key login
yaoshi-dashboard process renders the first dashboard frame on tty1
yaoshi-root-shell process runs on tty2
virtio network interface receives DHCP configuration
QEMU host forwarding accepts root SSH public-key login
successful SSH command runs /usr/bin/true on the installed system
Yaoshi Dashboard first frame contains Prepare, Root, SSH, Net, and Health summary states
Yaoshi Dashboard first frame contains System, Compute, Memory, Storage, and Network sections
Yaoshi Dashboard renders Debian kernel release status in the System Boot row
Yaoshi Dashboard renders full-width Storage and Network sections with bounded inventory summary rows and no duplicate SSH row
Yaoshi Dashboard renders no Events section
Dashboard selected default route, route interface address, SSH access address, interface kind, disk transport, root disk vendor/model display, CPU display identity, DMI-placeholder-filtered machine identity, SMBIOS DIMM capacity/vendor summary, hardware identity, and thermal source selection are deterministic
Yaoshi Dashboard renders no event message rows
Yaoshi Dashboard renders no page navigation text
Yaoshi Dashboard renders no footer line
Yaoshi Dashboard renders no authorized-key count and no kernel warning/error counts
Yaoshi Dashboard emits a dashboard display marker proving the selected Terminus 10x20 or 12x24 profile
Yaoshi Dashboard emits ASCII-only visible cells
Yaoshi Dashboard emits foreground-only styles
Yaoshi Dashboard remains read-only
QEMU test outputs include both a first dashboard serial screen-frame capture and a `dashboard-ready` serial screen-frame capture after prepare state is applied, SSH success is observed, and at least one dashboard refresh interval has elapsed. The ready capture is the settled dashboard frame.
```

Host-side target-ready check after QEMU exit is exact:

```text
target.raw byte size equals payload_target_minimum_bytes + 1073741824
target GPT parses after yaoshi-prepare
target GPT contains exactly two partitions
target partition 1 remains YAOSHI_ESP
target partition 2 remains YAOSHI_ROOT
target partition 2 unique GUID equals installed_root_partition_guid
target backup GPT header is located at the physical target disk end when root growth succeeded
target ESP FAT32 tree matches the installed ESP contract

target root ext4 filesystem check passes

target root contains /var/lib/yaoshi/prepare-state with exact content applied

target root ext4 size is greater than the pre-grow root filesystem size
```

`qemu::flow` test output root is exact:

```text
.yaoshi/check/qemu/
```

At test start, `qemu::flow` removes the previous test output root and recreates it. Output files are exact:

```text
progress.log
```

Each `screens/*.txt` file contains capture metadata, the extracted ASCII cell rows, and the extracted semantic style span rows for the corresponding product-visible runtime state. A missing extracted frame is a QEMU failure. QEMU writes no PNG screenshot artifact in v0.0.1.

`qemu::flow` stdout progress lines are exact and are emitted by the harness whenever a major validation boundary is crossed:

```text
YAOSHI_QEMU_PROGRESS phase=image-ready status=checking mode=current-only elapsed_ms=<ms>
YAOSHI_QEMU_PROGRESS phase=image-ready status=<current-hit|rebuilt> elapsed_ms=<ms>
YAOSHI_QEMU_PROGRESS phase=preflight status=complete elapsed_ms=<ms>
YAOSHI_QEMU_PROGRESS phase=qemu-start status=complete elapsed_ms=<ms>
YAOSHI_QEMU_PROGRESS phase=installer-first-frame status=complete elapsed_ms=<ms>
YAOSHI_QEMU_PROGRESS phase=installer-target status=complete elapsed_ms=<ms>
YAOSHI_QEMU_PROGRESS phase=installer-write status=<write_task> percent=<progress_percent> elapsed_ms=<ms>
YAOSHI_QEMU_PROGRESS phase=installer-done status=complete elapsed_ms=<ms>
```
